# Blister 2026-2027 Winter Buyer's Guide (12th edition) -- Notes

Source: txt/2026-2027.clean (15,877 lines). Notes written sequentially; source line ranges in brackets. Specs format: measured dims (length measured at) / stated radius / measured weight per ski / tip-camber-tail profile (tip splay / camber / tail splay) / measured length / mount point (from true center; from tail) / lengths / core.

## 1. Guide structure / TOC / editorial framing [L1-281]

- Cover: Luke Koppa (managing editor) skiing edge of Baldy's Bowl, Snowbird UT; photo Otto Solberg (shot during Blister Summit 2026). Twelfth edition.
- Ads (one-line): Snowbird; Blister Summit 2027 (April 6-10, 2027 -- test next year's gear, panels); The Beckwith / Elevation Hotel redevelopment at Crested Butte base (residences from $1.3M, completion late 2027); BLISTER+ membership ($0-deductible $25k-per-incident injury insurance across many activities, member content, 1-on-1 gear recs, guide, Summit discount, deals).
- "About This Guide" (Jonathan Ellsworth, Founder & Editor): no advertising money from manufacturers whose products are reviewed; multiple days in varied conditions on every product; guide is a digest of 1500-3000+ word online reviews. Blister Spectrums rank skis in each class relative to one another (Best in Pow, Most Playful, etc.); close = similar, opposite ends = notable. Glossary on website. "Think before you buy": older gear still good, many Best Of picks unchanged for years, environmental impact -- keep using gear you're happy with. Skis in each section arranged narrowest to widest. No MSRP listed (misleading). Most people pictured are Blister reviewers / Summit 2026 attendees.
- "How to interpret our specs" (example Salomon QST 94: 134-94-120 mm @180; 16.5 m; 1862 & 1931 g; 63/2.5/31.5 mm profile; 178.7 cm; -9.6 cm / 79.8 cm from tail; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + basalt + cork tip/tail inserts + fiberglass). Explains: measured dims (shovel/waist/tail widest/narrowest points), measured weight of both skis, tip/tail splay & camber measured decambered (see "Rocker 101"), measured length straight-line tip to tail, mount point relative to true center (measured length/2) and from tail -- measure from tail since topsheets shift.
- TABLE OF CONTENTS (page numbers):
  - Articles: 6 About This Guide; 12 Letter from the Editor; 205 Ski-Quiver Selections; 278 Blister's 'Best Of' Awards; 287 Index of Skis.
  - Product reviews: 14 50/50: Backcountry & Resort Skis; 27 Backcountry Touring Skis; 42 Women's All-Mountain -- More Stable; 57 Women's All-Mountain -- More Forgiving; 70 Women's Powder Skis; 78 On-Piste Carving Skis; 91 Frontside Skis; 106 All-Mountain -- More Stable; 129 All-Mountain -- More Forgiving; 148 All-Mountain Chargers; 154 All-Mountain Freestyle; 170 Park Skis; 178 Powder -- More Directional; 190 Powder -- More Playful; 210 Ski Boots; 230 Touring Bindings & Climbing Skins; 242 Snowboarding & Splitboarding; 266 Outerwear; 272 Helmets & Goggles.
- Letter from the Editor, "Two Truths and a Lie" (Ellsworth): references his rant with Cody Townsend ("Reviewing the News") about people complaining about a "crappy" season. Truth 1: every day on snow is a good day; gear only exists to get us out there. Truth 2: warming planet is real and measured; we should act. The lie: casual chairlift complaining is productive -- it isn't, it just steals joy. Suggestions: (1) change your opener to something positive; (2) use mediocre conditions (low vis, rough bumps, firm groomers) to work on balance, body position, slowing down; (3) pick one real thing to act on (county transit meeting, support a climate org, learn your utility's power source and vote locally).

## 2. Product categories

### 50/50 Skis: Backcountry & Resort [L282-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Palisades Tahoe), Paul Forward (Chugach Powder Guides AK).
Class overview: for one setup for lift-accessed + touring. Still not ideal (dedicated setups more durable/stable/safer), but never a better time for single setup given AT binding/boot/ski advances. Weight is the major factor: lighter = harder in challenging inbounds snow but easier uphill. These ski better inbounds than the lighter Backcountry Touring skis, while not as heavy as many All-Mountain/Powder skis (though often less damp/stable). Pick your compromise; BLISTER+ members can get personalized recs.

- Volkl Blaze 94 -- 132.5-94-118 (179); radii 20/40/17/22 m; 1613 & 1631 g; 59/2.5/25; 178.7 cm; -13.3 / 76.1 from tail; 151-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + recycled PET stringer + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Like a much lighter Mantra-ish directional ski; one of the lightest here but comes alive on piste, quick into carves, strong edge hold. Not planted in crud; lively rebound. For directional skiers who skin up/ski groomers. Flight and Bent 100 are easier to release/pivot off piste.
- J Skis Flight -- 126.5-94.5-113.5 (182); 18.8 m; 1666 & 1682 g; 69/3/35.5; 179.5; -7.2 / 82.6; 162-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Narrower Escalator/Slacker tuned for firmer "everyday" conditions; forgiving tips/tails, still supportive when driven; easier to release than Blaze 94, surfy in soft snow. Not great for big fast turns in firm variable snow. Good backcountry option for those who find light skis stiff/chattery.
- Atomic Bent 100 -- 129-99.5-119.5 (186); 19.5 m; 1886 & 1925 g; 56.5/11.5/33.5; 182.9; -7.5 / 84; 158-186; poplar + fiberglass. Hard to categorize: light all-mountain, downhill tourer, or park ski. Drive the shovels or ski centered; surprisingly solid on edge. Most precise/directional of the Bent series; balanced and freestyle-friendly. "About as close to do-it-all" for one-ski resort+touring+park.
- Elan Ripstick 102 Black Edition -- 137.5-101.5-118 (182); 18.8 m; 1776 & 1817 g; 51.5/6.5/17; 181.1; -7.8 / 82.8; 161-189; poplar/paulownia + 4 carbon rods + carbon strips + carbon weave + flax tip layer + fiberglass. Light, agile, stands out for precision and exceptional grip underfoot; less eager than Blaze 104 for tight slow carves. Rewards dynamic hop/unweight style rather than smearing. Not smooth in crud; nimble. More support/edge hold than standard Ripsticks.
- J Skis Escalator -- 131.5-102-123 (182); 18.6 m; 1750 & 1761 g; 73/5/36; 180.2; -7 / 83; 162-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Easygoing, adaptable, no significant weak points; soft surfy tips/tails; good suspension for weight, more predictable in variable snow than stiffer light skis (Ripstick 102 BE, Blaze 104). Stiffer alternatives: Bent 100, Pionnier 104; more float: Slacker.
- Ferreol Pionnier 104 2.0 -- 135.5-103.5-124.5 (180); 16 m; 1758 & 1814 g; 53.5/9.5/34; 180.3; -9.2 / 81; 164-188; poplar + Scalium binding plate + carbon & fiberglass. All Ferreol skis now use in-house "Scalium" alloy plates (titanal alternative). Short radius & tapered shovels make carving/slashing easy; stiff midsection & "3-point camber" give security on edge. Nice suspension for weight; size down if mostly touring. Alternative to Blaze 104/Ripstick 102 BE with better off-piste maneuverability; stiffer, more precise Escalator. Tree skiers: Surfeur 112.
- Volkl Blaze 104 -- 142-103.5-127.5 (186); radii 21/45/19/24 m; 1806 & 1864 g; 57.5/4/26.5; 186.2; -12.1 / 81; 158-186; same core as Blaze 94. Sharp carving character, rewards aggressive edge angles on consistent snow; can feel hooky/twitchy in crud. Low swing weight; tails need directional stance. Floats well; few match its energy on corduroy/corn. Soft/deep days: Blaze 114.
- Wagner Summit 106 -- 140.9-105.1-126.9 (186); 19.9 m; 2008 & 2065 g; 72/1.5/31.5; 184.4; -10.6 / 81.6; 165-186 (custom available); aspen/maple + "carbon fiber torsion box" + fiberglass. Semi-custom "Factory" model; deep low rocker lines with minimal taper -- loose in tight spots but carves well. Standout versatility and intuitiveness across varied reviewers. Heavier, hard to unsettle. Like a damper, more accessible DPS Kaizen 105 or more maneuverable/stable Blaze 104. Pick graphic & flex (standard recommended).
- DPS Kaizen 105 -- 135.4-105.2-119.1 (184); 18 m; 1867 & 1908 g; 52/7/26; 183.5; -9.7 / 82; 155-189; poplar/ash + carbon binding plate + carbon laminate. High-end all-carbon; impressive damping-to-weight; not as harsh as similarly light competitors. Tapered, moderately rockered -- maneuverable; carves on firm with effort; prefers forward stance. Looser lighter Summit 106 or stiffer more directional Pionnier 104. DPS Koala for more playful/damp.
- Asnes Voss Z'N -- 132.7-105.7-119.2 (187); 22 m; 1897 & 1917 g; 65/4/12; 185.7; mounts "Boot Center" -9.6 / 83.3, "All-Mountain" -12 / 80.9; 167, 176, 187; poplar + carbon. Like a lightened old-school big-mountain ski: soft tapered shovels surfy, stiff flat tail gives edge hold/support at speed. Not forgiving (see Escalator, Summit 106). Great float for width; secure on steep firm; not engaging at moderate speeds. Less directional alternatives: K2 Mindbender 111, 4FRNT Hoji.
- Meier Crosscut BC -- 141.9-105.8-125.5 (185); 19 m; 1919 & 1928 g; 53/8/9; 184.8; -9.5 / 82.5; 178, 185; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + innegra & fiberglass. Tapered deeply rockered tips (plane, smear predictably) + less rockered tail and strong cambered mid; directional like Voss Z'N but less punishing backseat. Tighter radius = more engaging on piste (Summit 106/Blaze 104 better carvers). Strength: soft snow, tight terrain. Lighter alternative: Renoun Citadel 107.
- Blizzard Canvas 108 (new "freeski" line) -- 139.5-107.5-127 (186); 19 m; 1806 & 1814 g; 74/5/56.5; 184; -6 / 86; 168-192; poplar/paulownia + partial titanal + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Borderline too light for some, but downhill performance very impressive; damping-to-weight; notably more playful than other Blizzards; forward or centered stance; nimble, lively. Carves better than most freestyle options; not as forgiving as Slacker nor as surfy as Goat 108, but carves better than both.
- Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro -- 128.5-107.5-121 (186); 24 m; 1852 & 1883 g; 76.5/5.5/52; 183.9; -4.6 / 87.4; 174-186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Deep rocker tips/tails, loose and surfy; surprisingly strong grip at speed; stiff mid & non-hooky sidecut give more composure than Bent 110 in soft variable. Playfulness + predictability.
- Renoun Citadel 107 -- 137-107.5-124.5 (184); 19.5 m; 1672 & 1740 g; 69.5/5/27.5; 182.2; -10.3 / 80.8; 163-191; aspen + titanal binding reinforcement + VibeStop + carbon. Non-Newtonian VibeStop: softer at low speeds, stiffer/damper under high force; performs above weight for damping. Directional, floats well; not best firm carver; low weight hampers stability in dense crud. One of the lightest Blister would still use lift-accessed after recent snow. Renoun membership allows model swaps.
- J Skis Slacker -- 135-109.2-127.1 (188); 21.2 m; 1905 & 1919 g; 80/2/39; 185.4; -7.7 / 85; 169-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Forgiving flex, deep rocker, progressive mount, intuitive; less damp than J's heavier skis but keeps predictability/playfulness. Floats well, forgives backseat, easy on edge. Hoji for bigger faster turns; Bent 110 for freestyle; Escalator with more stability/flotation.
- Atomic Bent 110 -- 133.2-109.5-124.6 (188); 19 m; 1863 & 1872 g; 68/5/64; 186.3; -3 / 90.1; 164-188; poplar + fiberglass. Unapologetically playful (Chris Benchetler series); think narrower, more versatile Bent Chetler 120 rather than wider Bent 100. Extremely surfy/nimble, nearly centered mount; solid edge hold for class but bounced around in crud; happiest in soft snow. More stability: Canvas 108, Hoji.
- Folsom Giver (custom 50/50 build) -- 137.5-109.5-128.5 (186); 25 m; 1947 & 1960 g; 69.5/9/25.5; 184.4; -9.9 / 82.3; 184-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + 70/30 fiberglass/carbon. Built for downhill predictability in varied BC snow while fairly light; minimizes deflection; rocker/camber/rocker version easy to feather and adds bite/pop on firm (reverse-camber Givers better in grabby snow). For those who like Katana V-Werks/Hoji idea but not fully rockered looseness. Heavier charger version in All-Mountain Chargers.
- K2 Mindbender 111 (overhauled line) -- 135.5-110.5-128.5 (187); 21 m; 2002 & 2062 g; 59/2.5/35; 186.9; -6.7 / 86.8; 170-192; aspen/paulownia + 2 partial titanal layers + TPU/steel "targeted mass weighting" + fiberglass. Small TPU and steel inserts add mass where damping needed -- shovels notably calm for weight (confirmed on protos & production). Surfy, agile, playful despite being stiff. Less fun at slow speeds than Surfeur 112/Bent 110 but damper/stabler at speed; good edge hold; more precise firm-snow alternative to Hoji.
- Ferreol Surfeur 112 -- 140.5-111-129.5 (184); 17 m; 1953 & 1974 g; 64/5.5/38; 183.5; -8.2 / 83.6; 176, 184, 192; poplar + Scalium plate + carbon stringers + flax laminate (flax replaces fiberglass). Impressively damp for weight; tapered, deep rocker, highly maneuverable/playful; predictable and stable in soft-ish conditions. Not for big fast open-terrain turns; more engaging than most at moderate speeds. Tree-skiing pick.
- 4FRNT Hoji -- 128.2-111.6-119.3 (184); 30 m (all lengths); 2060 & 2075 g; 73/0/26 (reverse camber); 182.5; -6.6 / 84.7; 170-191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Eric Hjorleifson pro model: reverse camber, long radius, forward mount -- extremely easy to slash/drift yet happy laying down big turns; reliable firm edge hold mid-ski, predictable in firm/variable though not exciting carver. Rare blend of stability, maneuverability, adaptability. Lighter/narrower: 4FRNT Raven.
- Volkl Katana V-Werks -- 142.5-112-132 (184); 23.5 m; 1959 & 1975 g; 68/0/18; 182.2; -13.9 / 77.2; 177, 184, 191; poplar/beech + carbon. Decade-plus unique; go-to for directional skiers wanting light, strong, stable ski in technical terrain; surprisingly damp for carbon; long radius, set-back mount, no taper -> minimal deflection. Carves well (not quickest edge-to-edge). Not engaging if not driving shovels; thin construction -- don't slam into rocks. Mellower: Blaze 114.
- Dynafit Tigard 114 -- 140.5-113.5-129.5 (188); 23.5 m; 2011 & 2023 g; 65.5/4.5/31.5; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.6; 180, 188; poplar + carbon stringers. Dynafit's widest/boldest; floats well, easy to drift; surprisingly stiff and precise on firmer snow (drive through crusts/layers). Not forgiving backseat; harsh when very firm. Comfortable in air. Easier: Surfeur 112, Blaze 114; damper/more forgiving: Mindbender 111.
- Volkl Blaze 114 -- 153.4-114-136 (184); radii 39/18/29 m; 1938 & 2008 g; 68/3/22; 183.5; -11.8 / 80; 166-192; poplar/beech + recycled PET stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Hit with many reviewers; reliable in most pow-day scenarios (BC trees, resort chop). Wide soft rockered tips plane easily; rearward mount but not stance-picky; carves on piste. Not a charger but respectable suspension. Intuitive for directional skiers, experts & less-experienced alike; not for air-focused balanced skiers.
- Salomon S/Lab QST X -- 140-116-127 (184); 25 m; 1973 & 1997 g; 66.5/6/26; 183; -5.3 / 86.2; 178, 184, 192; karuba/poplar + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + flax/carbon + fiberglass. Most maneuverable, least directional QST; very playful in soft snow, long carves in pow from centered stance; fun in chop with dynamic style. Less at home on firm -- short/twitchy. QST Echo 106 far more versatile.
- Blizzard Canvas 118 (new) -- 146-117-134.5 (188); 20 m; 1862 & 1876 g; 71/2/60.5; 185.9; -6 / 87; 172, 180, 188; poplar/paulownia + partial titanal + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Impressive downhill performance for weight; stable like heavier skis in soft chop; playful, balanced, poppy without needing centered stance. Less loose than most playful, but minimal taper gives reliable grip. Alternative to Bent Chetler 120 (more composure) or less directional Blaze 114. Best when used for both BC and resort pow.
- ON3P Billy Goat 118 Tour (custom program) -- 145.5-118.5-128 (191); 29.6 m; 1949 & 1956 g; 79/5/35; 190.8; -10.2 / 85.2; 181, 186, 191; bamboo/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Lighter version of standard Billy Goat 118 (More Directional Powder). Directional but drifts/slashes easily; rearward mount & pin tail = flotation & support up front. Edge hold and crud stability not fortes. More directional QST X or surfier/stabler Blaze 114.
- Atomic Bent Chetler 120 -- 143.5-119.5-134.5 (192); 20 m; 1941 & 1989 g; 62.5/8.5/57.5; 190.2; -2.6 / 92.5; 176, 184, 192; poplar + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Long a reference playful pow ski; very surfy, slashes into butters; balanced in air, stiff mid supports landings. Centered stance takes adjustment; not for big fast turns in variable (Giver, Katana V-Werks); nervous/chattery on firm.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- 50/50 (top -> bottom):
- Best in Variable -> Worst: Katana V-Werks, Folsom Giver, Summit 106, Blaze 114, Hoji, Mindbender 111, Billy Goat 118 Tour, Canvas 118, Bent 100, Canvas 108, Pionnier 104, QST X, Slacker, Voss Z'N, Crosscut BC, Kaizen 105, Citadel 107, Tigard 114, Goat 108 Enduro, Escalator, Ripstick 102 Black, Surfeur 112, Bent Chetler 120, Blaze 104, Bent 110, Flight, Blaze 94.
- Most Inbounds-Oriented -> Most Touring-Oriented: Summit 106, Bent 100, Blaze 104, Blaze 94, Ripstick 102 Black, Katana V-Werks, Escalator, Blaze 114, Canvas 118, Canvas 108, Giver, Mindbender 111, Crosscut BC, Kaizen 105, Pionnier 104, Flight, Bent Chetler 120, Billy Goat 118 Tour, Surfeur 112, Bent 110, Slacker, Hoji, QST X, Citadel 107, Goat 108 Enduro, Tigard 114, Voss Z'N.
- Most Playful -> Least Playful: Bent 110, Bent Chetler 120, Surfeur 112, QST X, Canvas 118, Slacker, Canvas 108, Escalator, Goat 108 Enduro, Billy Goat 118 Tour, Mindbender 111, Hoji, Flight, Bent 100, Citadel 107, Kaizen 105, Pionnier 104, Tigard 114, Summit 106, Blaze 114, Crosscut BC, Giver, Ripstick 102 Black, Blaze 104, Blaze 94, Voss Z'N, Katana V-Werks.

### Backcountry Touring Skis [L1038-]
Reviewer pictured: Paul Forward (Chugach Range AK). Class overview: choose your balance of uphill vs downhill performance; don't fixate on ski weight -- consider total weight on feet (skis + bindings + boots + skins). If not doing long tours or racing, heavier skis/boots/bindings give better stability, suspension, safety features. Plenty of good options whether long big-vert days, quick laps, or tricks.

- Majesty Superwolf -- 129.9-90.6-110.1 (178); 18 m; stated 1540 g; 62/4/14; 176.3; -9.9 / 78.3; 154-184; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. Rare variable-conditions predictability at low weight; less harsh on firm, holds up at speed; rarely hooky; handled steep icy lines. One of most stable/versatile touring skis at this width/weight; strong 1-ski option (corn arcs to technical hop turns). Superwolf Carbon is lighter.
- La Sportiva Salto -- 120-91.5-107 (178); 18 m; 1384 & 1434 g; 63.5/7/15.5; 177.2; -7.9 / 80.7; 164, 178, 188; poplar/paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. Tapered tips/tails, lots of tip rocker, low tail -- maneuverable for width while holding edge; ~1400 g good for bigger missions. Tips twitchy in big variable turns but handles crust/sastrugi better than Zero G 96/Crux 93. Suspension & maneuverability over minimum weight/precision. Very firm: Zero G 96, MTN 96 Carbon.
- Head Crux 93 Pro -- 130-92.5-112.5 (177); 18.6 m; 1224 & 1260 g; 62.5/6.5/17; 175.5; -10.4 / 77.3; 163-184; karuba + graphene, carbon & fiberglass. Very light yet strong, directional; impressive stability-to-weight in consistent snow (groomers, windbuff, corn). Low weight shows in rough snow -- needs attentive, conservative pilot. For skilled skiers prioritizing light weight; add a couple hundred grams for Superwolf/MTN 96 Carbon.
- Majesty Superpatrol -- 129-94.1-111 (177); 19 m; 1630 & 1635 g; 61/8/9; 175.8; -14.5 / 73.4; 161-185; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. "Cut-off tail technology": skier far back so you can size down; drive the shovels for impressive stability/flotation for size; short tails snap around. Less intuitive for centered stance. Stiffer, more stable than Superwolf, needs more speed. Polarizing mount. Superpatrol Carbon lighter variant.
- Blizzard Zero G 96 -- 126-94.5-110 (178); 20 m; 1289 & 1329 g; 63/4/13; 175.3; -9.6 / 78; 164-185; paulownia/poplar + ash binding insert + carbon & fiberglass. For directional skiers with solid technique wanting a light setup they can ski hard; not damp/maneuverable in variable; home carving smooth snow (corn, light pow, near-ice). More versatile than Crux 93, more secure on firm than Salto; well-rounded for lighter skis. More suspension: MTN 96 Carbon, Raven.
- J Skis Flight (also in 50/50; specs above) -- standout touring ski for those wanting a narrower setup not overly light/directional; heavier than most narrow skis here but smoother in harsh snow; soft rockered tips/tails, closer-to-center mount -> accessible and playful; fun to carve, surfier. Not top pick for huge days precisely working steep firm lines.
- Salomon MTN 96 Carbon -- 130-95-116 (182); 19 m; 1451 & 1502 g; 68/6/13.5; 180.8; -11 / 79.3; 166-188; karuba + titanal binding reinforcement + cork tip inserts + carbon & basalt. Shares much with all-time favorite MTN Explore 95; very impressive suspension-to-weight; secure on edge on firm; good in tight/grabby terrain. Superpatrol slightly more composed at speed, Zero G 96 more firm precision at lower weight. Reliable partner, esp. spring touring.
- Head Crux 99 Pro -- 132-98.5-116.5 (184); 22.7 m; 1254 & 1327 g; 60.5/7.5/17; 182.6; -11.5 / 79.8; 163-184; karuba + graphene, carbon & fiberglass. Generalist standout for precision, support, low mass; shallow rocker, strong flex, rearward mount -> edge hold and power if you stay forward. Not for skidding from centered stance; nervous as snow roughens and speed climbs, though long radius tames twitchiness.
- 4FRNT Raven -- 120.5-103.1-111.7 (184); 29 m (all lengths); 1736 & 1767 g; 64/0/25.5 (reverse camber); 182.2; -5.7 / 85.4; 170-190; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Longtime reviewer favorite; more stable/predictable than all narrower lighter skis here, yet one of easiest to maneuver for beginners or experts; smears in weird BC snow; long radius not hooky. Excellent 4-Lock skin attachment. Downside: little tip engagement for slow-speed carves (see QST Echo 106). One of favorite all-round BC skis.
- Fauna Pioneer -- 123.4-103.2-118.3 (184); 22 m; 1787 & 1793 g; 63.5/10/51; 184; -7.1 / 84.9; 175, 184; paulownia/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Unconventional: very tapered tips/tails, progressive mount, twin tail, but lots of camber and very stiff cambered section. Extremely easy to pivot in soft, slices soft chop; decent firm edge hold with slightly centered stance; not for ice/carving. Good suspension for weight; tips twitchy in dense crud. Directional folks: Locator 104, Summit 105. For loose, playful BC style.
- Elan Ripstick Tour 104 -- 129.4-103.4-121.9 (187); 25.4 m; 1576 & 1592 g; 52.5/5.5/20.5; 184.3; -6.3 / 85.9; 173, 180, 187; paulownia/poplar + carbon rod + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Energetic flex and maneuverability of Ripsticks, retuned: lighter, softer, longer radius, more centered mount; adapts to stances; easier to release in grabby snow but less quick/snappy carving. Not as composed as heavier alternatives; lighter alternative to Raven with traditional camber bite.
- Armada Locator 104 -- 130-103.5-121.5 (178); 22 m; 1442 & 1474 g; 49/6/19; 177; -10 / 78.7; 162-186; caruba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Light directional ski with some Armada surfiness; needs attentive pilot in rough snow; deep rocker/tapered tips easy at slow speeds; comfortable big fast turns or slashes in pow/corn; good edge hold for width. Rearward mount & soft shovels = good flotation, prefers directional style. For bigger lines/deeper snow: excellent Locator 112.
- Ad (one-line): avalanche education (AIARE courses).
- La Sportiva Tempo -- 131.5-103.5-121.5 (190); 19 m; 1558 & 1589 g; 74.5/6.5/19.5; 187.8; -8.5 / 85.4; 164, 179, 190; paulownia/PVC foam + aluminum stringers + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon. Notably light mid-fat; shines in soft snow, not picky about style; highly tapered, deep rocker, moderately progressive mount -> playful, maneuverable. Knocked around in firm rough; floats/turns well in pow; accessible in corn/windbuff. Stiffer, lighter alternative to Weston Summit Carbon 105; more maneuverable less directional Locator 104 (less suspension).
- Wagner Summit 105 ("Factory" semi-custom) -- 134.7-104.4-122.6 (185); 23.8 m; 1705 & 1708 g; 72/5/31; 183.2; -9.8 / 81.8; 164-185 (custom); paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. Do-it-all touring ski: deep rocker slides easily, minimal taper makes it fun to carve; big sweet spot; prefers forward stance. For directional skiers who like Raven idea but want camber and tighter radius; or more forgiving Faction Agent 3. Choose graphic & flex.
- Head Crux 105 Pro -- 132.5-104.5-121.5 (184); 25.6 m; 1357 & 1380 g; 57/10/24.5; 182.7; -11.5 / 79.8; 170, 177, 184; karuba + graphene, carbon & fiberglass. Crux minimalism in a wider pow-focused design; traditional shape/rocker, less surfy than QST Echo 106/Tempo; long radius & slight taper boost soft-snow maneuverability vs narrower Cruxes. Rewards forward stance/technique; knocked around in variable. Floats well, wide range of turns; Faction Agent 3 if you want more suspension/composure.
- Weston Summit Carbon 105 -- 133.8-105.4-123.8 (186); 16.5 m; 1671 & 1675 g; 71/2/48; 184.2; -7.8 / 84.3; 156-186; paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. Made for mellower terrain/imperfect conditions; soft rockered tapered tips/tails -- easy tight carves, forgiving, slashy from various stances; nice suspension for weight. Not for big fast turns in tough snow but far from beginner-only; excellent for less-experienced BC skiers and mid-winter tree laps. Stiffer: QST Echo 106.
- Faction Agent 3 -- 133.2-105.5-123.5 (183); 21 m; 1725 & 1774 g; 69.5/5/24; 181.6; mounts Newschool -9.6/81.2, Progressive -11.1/79.7, Classic -12.6/78.2; 164-188; karuba + carbon weave + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Lighter take on metal Dancer series; stout, for drivers, but broad sweet spot; keep off stiff flat tails; strong edge hold, damp for weight, lively. Uncommon stability/support/precision for touring. Surfier directional: La Machine 3.
- Salomon S/Lab QST Echo 106 -- 138.8-105.7-125.9 (181); 19 m; 1820 & 1824 g; 54/4/26; 180; mounts "MTN binding" -8/82, "Alpine + Shift" -9/81; 157-189; karuba/poplar + cork tips/tails + basalt & fiberglass. Calm and composed where others rattle, without demanding perfect technique -- quick reviewer favorite. Excellent do-everything downhill-oriented BC ski: maneuverable, stance-adaptable, fun at varied speeds, capable in rough snow; carves fine outside ice. Alternative to Raven if unsure about reverse camber. Few lighter skis match its suspension/maneuverability/predictability.
- Moment Wildcat Tour 108 -- 134.5-107.7-127.1 (184); 24 m; 1692 & 1715 g; 72/5.5/65; 180.5; -7.1 / 83.2; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Mixes freestyle playfulness (balanced in air, centered-friendly, surfy) with directional stability (edge hold, can drive shovels, stable for weight, composed at speed); needs speed to engage when carving. Forward-stance skiers: Agent 3, Ronin 108 LTE, or Moment Countach Tour.
- ON3P Woodsman 108 Tour -- 138-108-127.5 (186); 21.0 m; 1766 & 1767 g; 67/4.5/40.5; 186; -8.1 / 84.9; 176, 181, 186; bamboo/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Brings standard Woodsman 108's freeride stability and surprising surfiness to much lighter package; not stance-picky; not as damp as standard but stands out vs similarly light skis. QST Echo 106 more precise carving, Wildcat Tour 108 more playful. Hard to make it wrong tool except icy steeps & booters.
- Ronin 108 LTE -- 140.8-108.1-124 (185); 20.5 m (all lengths); 1637 & 1647 g; 59/6/22; 185.4; -11.3 / 81.4; 160-192; balsa/bamboo + carbon & flax. Made in Chamonix; light enough for most but versatility/downhill-first; dependable, well-rounded for directional skiers; big shovels float, more precise than many wide skis; predictable with forward stance; comparable to Agent 3 in support/stability/carving but slightly more forgiving. Heavier "MFD" construction for resort.
- Faction La Machine 3 -- 131.7-108.7-123.8 (184); 21 m; 1560 & 1594 g; 68/3/40; 181.9; mounts Newschool -8/83, Progressive -9.5/81.5, Classic -11/80; 165-190; paulownia + phenol binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Impressively light; surfier directional alternative, predictable in weird BC snow; looser/more accessible than Agent 3 (which is more precise on firm). Impressive pow ski for width. QST Echo 106 and Heritage Lab UT110 better for high-speed composure & centered stance. Deep pow: La Machine 5.
- Atomic Backland 109 -- 134.5-109-123.5 (184); 19 m; 1526 & 1569 g; 56.5/10.5/24; 182.8; -6.6 / 84.8; 176, 184, 189; poplar/karuba + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Touring ski that feels like the playful Bent line (surfy, maneuverable) but much lighter and less center-mounted than Bent 110. Not precise on firm or stable in rough; predictable speed limit; stance-adaptable (less directional alternative to La Machine 3). More suspension: Woodsman 108 Tour, QST Echo 106.
- Heritage Lab Ultra Taper 110 -- 135-110-128 (186); 24.7 m; 1762 & 1797 g; stated profile rocker/1.75 mm camber/rocker; 186; -7 / 86; 180, 186, 192; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Light tourer for broad soft-snow range (UT122 is the truly ultra-tapered pow ski). Related to AM110 but lighter with revised rocker. Particularly impressive suspension for weight; deep rocker + low swing weight make it very easy to pivot, yet more stable in open alpine terrain than most. Composed in almost everything short of deepest pow; moderately progressive mount -- playful without losing directional confidence.
- Moment Deathwish Tour 112 -- 136-111-128 (184); 25 m; 1660 & 1680 g; 66/3/64; 183.4; -6 / 85.7; 174-190; paulownia/beech + carbon stringers + fiberglass. "Triple Camber," strong flex, twin tip -- more intuitive than it sounds; rare combo of surfy tips/tails and strong edge grip underfoot; damp/stable for weight though less so than resort Deathwish. Prefers centered stance; not for rear-mount directional fans. Wildcat Tour 118 for more float/stability.
- Armada Locator 112 -- 135.7-111.6-127.8 (187); 26 m; 1608 & 1661 g; 58/6/23; 186; -10.4 / 83; 166-187; karuba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Excellent all-round BC pow ski even for wide-ski fans; -10 mount helps flotation but still balanced in slarves; very good stability-to-weight; feels big on firm, long radius favors big turns; surprisingly adaptable. For variable snow, long approaches, bigger lines.
- Armada ARV 116 JJ UL -- 139-116-134 (185); 18 m; 1820 & 1835 g; 71/6/64; 183.5; -5 / 87; 165-192; karuba + fiberglass. Unabashedly playful; surfy, nimble in deep glades, great for BC booters; needs balanced centered stance. Struggles in firm/variable -- deflection-prone (more than Locator 112). Wildcat Tour 118 for more balance of playfulness and composure.
- La Sportiva Capo -- 141.5-117-131 (189); 21 m; 1603 & 1611 g; 66.5/6/20.5; 187; -11.6 / 81.9; 166, 178, 189; paulownia/PVC foam + aluminum stringers + fiberglass binding reinforcement + D3O tip/tail inserts + carbon. Very light pow ski; surprising camber, rearward mount, lots of taper. Knocked around in variable; decent float; best on moderate-angle terrain; directional, tougher to drift (like La Machine). Carves firm better than most this wide; good for pow-filled tree laps. More balanced: Wildcat Tour 118, wider Heritage Labs; more versatile at similar weight: Moonlight Cruiser.
- Moment Wildcat Tour 118 -- 142.9-117.8-134.4 (190); 27 m; 1885 & 1914 g; 73/6.5/65; 186.5; -6.8 / 86.4; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. "Playful charger" personality of resort Wildcat 118 for BC; fun and surfy, adequate (not outstanding) flotation, excellent stability for weight in variable; long radius predictable, easy to throw sideways. Uncommon versatility across conditions and skier types among big touring skis.
- Heritage Lab BC120 (limited runs) -- 144-121-139 (193); 27.2 m; 1852 & 1879 g; 60/0/37; 193; -8 / 88.5; 180, 187, 193; paulownia/poplar + carbon. One of Blister's primary pow reviewer's (Alaska) all-time favorites; extremely intuitive in big terrain; long carves in all soft snow, easy big drifts; excellent suspension-to-weight even at speed in bumpy runouts. Swallowtail polarizing for skins (simple skin-tail mods fix). UT122 / Guide 135 surfier for trees/low angle; few skis this light so at home on steep pow lines.
- Moonlight Cruiser Carbon All-Terrain -- 145-121-135 (186); 26 m; 1631 & 1662 g; (profile not given); 184.5; -11.2 / 81; 177, 186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Two constructions with same shape: Carbon Superlight (~1300 g @186; maybe lightest-for-size ever reviewed, but falls off quickly outside untouched pow) and All-Terrain (reviewed): extremely light for width, very good suspension across nearly all conditions, composed in pow, can be driven by <1200 g 2-buckle boots, drifts well for directional design. Heavier skis beat it in tough snow; impressive for efficiency-minded pow skiers.
- Heritage Lab Ultra Taper 122 -- 136-124-130 (190); 34 m; 1836 & 1845 g; 75/0/20; 190; -7 / 88; 183, 190; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Modern reverse-camber reverse-sidecut, purely for untracked pow. Heavier than Cruiser but much looser, stance-adaptable, better in grabby crust/wet snow; ludicrously easy to pivot in deep pow on steep or mellow terrain; impressive suspension-to-weight. Not versatile -- short effective edge, long radius, no camber feel weird on firm. Special experience in intended conditions.
- Heritage Lab Guide 135 (limited run; early testing) -- 160-135-150 (191); 27.5 m; 2079 & 2087 g; stated 83/0/75; 193.5; -4.7 / 92; 182, 191; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Dedicated BC pow ski bucking the trend toward narrower "pow" skis; builds on HB122 philosophy; max flotation, intuitive flex, lighter touring construction. Unsinkable shovel, big sweet spot, tail easy to release; low weight + good suspension + active flex rarely harsh/nervous. Poised to be among most compelling pow-touring skis; heavier non-touring HB135 upcoming.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Backcountry Touring (top -> bottom):
- Best in Heavy, Wet Snow -> Worst: UT122, Guide 135, BC120, Wildcat Tour 118, Locator 112, UT110, ARV 116 JJ UL, Raven, Moonlight Cruiser AT, La Machine 3, Deathwish Tour 112, Wildcat Tour 108, Woodsman 108 Tour, QST Echo 106, Capo, Backland 109, Locator 104, Weston Summit Carbon 105, Wagner Summit 105, Fauna Pioneer, Ronin 108 LTE, Tempo, Agent 3, Ripstick Tour 104, Crux 105 Pro, Flight, MTN 96 Carbon, Superpatrol, Superwolf, Salto, Crux 99 Pro, Zero G 96, Crux 93 Pro.
- Best in Steep, Firm Terrain -> Worst: Superwolf, MTN 96 Carbon, Zero G 96, Superpatrol, Agent 3, Crux 93 Pro, Crux 99 Pro, Flight, Salto, Crux 105 Pro, Ronin 108 LTE, Locator 104, QST Echo 106, Wagner Summit 105, Ripstick Tour 104, La Machine 3, Tempo, Raven, Deathwish Tour 112, Wildcat Tour 108, UT110, Woodsman 108 Tour, Backland 109, Weston Summit Carbon 105, Wildcat Tour 118, Fauna Pioneer, BC120, Moonlight Cruiser AT, Locator 112, Capo, ARV 116 JJ UL, Guide 135, UT122.
- Best in Punchy Crusts -> Worst: Wildcat Tour 118, BC120, UT110, Raven, UT122, Guide 135, Deathwish Tour 112, Moonlight Cruiser AT, ARV 116 JJ UL, Locator 112, Wildcat Tour 108, Woodsman 108 Tour, La Machine 3, QST Echo 106, Capo, Fauna Pioneer, Wagner Summit 105, Weston Summit Carbon 105, Locator 104, Ronin 108 LTE, Backland 109, Tempo, Ripstick Tour 104, Agent 3, MTN 96 Carbon, Superpatrol, Crux 105 Pro, Flight, Superwolf, Zero G 96, Salto, Crux 99 Pro, Crux 93 Pro.

### Women's All-Mountain Skis -- More Stable [L1958-]
Reviewers pictured: Kristin Sinnott (Palisades Tahoe), Sascha Anastas (Snowbird). Class overview: brands vary in women's/men's/unisex differentiation -- some skis here are women-specific, others not, but all tested by female reviewers. Women's all-mountain split into More Forgiving and More Stable (plus Women's Powder). More Stable = stiffer, less tolerant of backseat skiing, but damper/more stable at speed in tracked/variable/rough groomers; often heavier; good edge hold, powerful turn finish; good bump skis for strong skiers but punishing for less experienced; less appealing for playful/loose or easy slow-speed initiation. Read caveats -- some are fairly easy.

- Blizzard Black Pearl 84 -- 124-83.5-107 (170); 14 m; 1690 & 1700 g; 70/5/18.5; 168.4; -10.9 / 73.3; 146-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Surprisingly accessible despite stout on-paper traits; shines on piste -- easy to initiate/hold/finish carves without perfect technique or speed. Off piste requires driving shovels (vs Kore 87 Ti W). Aggressive skiers may want more high-speed composure; more energetic/agile than most stabler skis. Good for carving learners and advanced skiers on moderate groomers.
- Volkl Mantra 84 W -- 129-83.5-109.5 (170); radii 26/15/23 m; 1721 & 1734 g; 45.5/4/11.5; 170.2; -11.9 / 73.3; 149-170; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Excellent firm-snow precision, power, composure; stiff, damp, stable; not forgiving in tight firm moguls. Rivals piste skis for edge hold and energy; viable off piste for proficient skiers. Beginners: Black Pearl 84, Explo 86. More off-piste: Stance W Pro 88.
- Ferreol Explo 86 -- 123-86.5-111 (174); 15 m; 1544 & 1563 g; 55/9/19; 173.9; -8.7 / 78.3; 156-174; poplar + Scalium binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Nimble, light, design like wider all-mountain skis; impressive suspension for weight; carves cleanly once on edge; some found it more confident than BP84 in firm cut-up snow. Very low swing weight, loose tail off piste. Not damp/planted. Can do occasional skin laps.
- Head Kore 87 Ti W -- 126.5-86.5-110 (170); 14.6 m; 1664 & 1671 g; 53/10/20; 169.1; -9 / 75.5; 149-170; poplar/PET + 2 titanal layers + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Impressive off-piste maneuverability; not a standout carver but predictable skidding on ice, bends into carve in softer snow; commendable suspension. Easier to release than Mantra 84 W/BP84, damper than Explo 86. Intermediate-advanced, more bumps/trees than icy groomers.
- Blizzard Black Pearl 88 -- 127-87.5-109 (170); 15 m; 1773 & 1785 g; 73.5/4.5/20.5; 168.3; -10.4 / 73.8; 146-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Long-standing, deservedly popular; easy to recommend for firmer/shallower snow; easy on edge for ex-racers or carving learners. Can be hooky in variable, need to stay over shovels. More stability: Santa Ana 92, BP94; easier off piste: Explo 86, Ranger 90.
- Icelantic Tempest 88 (new line) -- 131.5-88-115 (169); 14 m; 1592 & 1640 g; 47/11.5/17; 168.2; -7.4 / 76.7; 150-169; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. New Tempest line much more precise, supportive, lively than prior Icelantics; agile, energetic for experienced hard-pushers. Harder to get on edge at slow speeds than BP88 but once bent, edge hold rivals Mantra 84 W with more rebound. Not forgiving backseat in firm bumps; looser in soft; stable in shallow crud for weight. Strong edge-holding ski without the weight.
- Fischer Ranger 90 -- 123.9-89.5-113.4 (170); 16 m; 1764 & 1794 g; 63/3/20; 168.6; -7.7 / 76.6; 156-184; poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Maneuverable ~90 mm ski that isn't overly light/unstable; soft tapered shovels; similar suspension to Stance 88 but easier carve initiation; looser off piste; strong tail can be unforgiving in bumps for beginners. Shovels "vague" on very firm. Damper alternative to Kore 87 Ti; piste-focused: BP88.
- Salomon Stance W Pro 88 -- 126.2-90-108 (174); 18 m; stated 1660 g; 55/6/12; 174.1; -9.6 / 77.5; 154-174; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal layers + C/FX inserts + fiberglass. More powerful/precise than QST, with freeride intentions; respectable suspension & composure but less demanding in tight off-piste spots than equally stable skis. Not for backseat skiers or low-speed carving; for off-piste time and longer groomer turns. Ranger 90 pivots easier; Stance more secure on firm and easier in bumps than BP94. More forgiving tail: Kore 93 Ti W.
- Shaggy's Belle 90 -- 136-90-116 (174); 16.25-17.9 m; 1760 & 1771 g; 52/5/16; 173.7; -8.5 / 78; 156-174; ash/poplar + magnesium sheets + carbon & fiberglass. Built to carve (corduroy or variable); rockered but minimally tapered shovel precise; "camber pockets" fore/aft of bindings add firm bite; flat stiff tail finishes powerfully but hard to release in moguls. Composed when groomers get cut up. Stance W Pro 88/Kore 93 Ti W easier in tight terrain; Belle holds edge much better. More off-piste: Tempest 94.
- Head Kore 93 Ti W -- 129-92.5-114 (170); 14.7 m; 1686 & 1719 g; 60/11/20; 169; -9 / 75.5; 149-170; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Doesn't need to be skied hard, but allows it; more stable than Kore 87 off piste without much more input. Not eager to hook up on groomers but predictable, fun to carve when not super firm. Much more nimble/forgiving in bumps than Santa Ana 92/BP94. Off-piste maneuverability > on-piste precision; broadly appealing mix.
- Blizzard Black Pearl 94 -- 132-93.5-114.5 (176); 17 m; 1933 & 1953 g; 76/6/20.5; 174.2; -10.4 / 76.7; 152-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Widest Black Pearl; calm, composed, precise; big step up in stability and demands vs narrower BPs. Among damp/stable skis, a bit livelier and easier to bend; one of most predictable in rough snow, more agile than Santa Ana 92 / M7 Mantra W. Excellent carver; capable off piste for strong technique. For those who find modern skis too light/loose/soft.
- Dynastar M-Pro 92 W -- 127.5-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762 & 1781 g; 71.5/5.5/23.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 146-178; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Merges M-Free maneuverability with directional shape; deep rocker, moderate weight = looser, more agile; strong tail, lively flex rewards input. Heavier skis calmer in rough snow; not precise carver on firm; pivots/slashes like Kore 93 Ti W. More energetic Kore 93 or tighter-turning Stance 88.
- Photo: pro skier Anne Wangler, Blister Summit 2026 @ Snowbird.
- Nordica Santa Ana 92 -- 128-93.5-116 (179); 17.5 m; 2045 & 2070 g; 65/5.5/19.5; 177.6; -7 / 81.8; 150-179; poplar/beech + partial titanal + elastomer layer + fiberglass. Stability, precision, smooth suspension define it; few more composed in crud; energetic when pushed; not agile or forgiving. Top pick for stability/precision on firm groomers or rough crud; capable off piste with good technique. Accessible alternatives: Mindbender 96 W, M-Pro 92 W.
- Icelantic Tempest 94 -- 135-93.5-119 (169); 14 m; 1749 & 1750 g; 52/9.5/20.5; 168.3; -7 / 77.2; 155-176; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Precise, lively, strong; needs force to bend but excellent edge hold and energy; prefers medium turns longer than radius suggests. Attentive pilot off piste; light swing weight, tails release in soft. More precise M-Pro 92 W or more agile/less damp M7 Mantra W. Easier: Dancer 99 Ti, Icelantic Maiden.
- K2 Mindbender 96 W (replaces Mindbender 99Ti W) -- 135-95.5-122.5 (168); 16 m; 1723 & 1741 g; 50/4.5/24; 167.7; -10.1 / 73.8; 153-173; aspen/paulownia + 2 partial titanal + TPU/steel targeted mass weighting + fiberglass. Rare combo of above-average edge hold and above-average bump/tree maneuverability; similarly damp to predecessor though notably lighter thanks to targeted mass; calm planted shovels in firm/crud. Less damp than Santa Ana 92/Mantra 102 W but much more agile, carves similarly. Very impressive do-everything.
- Ferreol Explo 96 -- 128.7-96-118.5 (172); 15 m; stated 1600 g; 75/9/34; 170; -7.5 / 77.5; 156-182; poplar + Scalium binding reinforcement + fiberglass & carbon. Agility & maneuverability standout; low swing weight; rockered tapered ends easy to skid; "3-point camber" gives strong grip on firm (takes getting used to). Explo 86 better on piste; 96 more stable in soft variable. Respectable suspension for weight; good hybrid-binding candidate. Damper: Mindbender 96 W.
- Volkl M7 Mantra W -- 138.5-96-121 (170); radii 24/38/15/20 m; 1883 & 1925 g; 42/4.5/11.5; 170.6; -12.1 / 73.2; 149-177; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Reference for stability and precision; one of best ~95 mm carvers; surprisingly easy to get on edge for stiffness; needs deliberate technique off piste (Mindbender 96 W, Dancer 99 Ti more maneuverable). Livelier alternative to BP94/Santa Ana. Wider: Mantra 102 W.
- Moment Siren 98 -- 136.5-98-119.5 (170); 14.5 m; 1659 & 1669 g; 49.5/5.5/20; 168.8; -8.1 / 76.3; 164, 170, 176; poplar/pine + carbon & fiberglass. One of Moment's most directional, but freeride-leaning here: deeper rocker, less set-back mount, low weight -> agile off piste; still stiff, can be pushed in soft/consistent. Not quite as precise/damp as Mindbender 96 W or AntiMatter 100 W; strong tail not forgiving backseat. Wider: Siren 104.
- Faction Dancer 99 Ti (replaces Dancer 2) -- 131.5-98.5-122.5 (171); 16 m; 1792 & 1866 g; 71.5/7/28; 169; -8.8 / 75.7; 164-188; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Similar shape to Dancer 2 but notably easier to release in tight bumps/soft snow; nice suspension without sluggishness; compromise is on-piste precision/grip. Middle-ground flex. Non-Ti Dancer 99 for more maneuverability.
- Head Kore 99 Ti W -- 131-98.5-120 (170); 15.6 m; 1803 & 1843 g; 55/7/18; 168.1; -8.7 / 75.4; 156-177; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Very adaptable for middle-of-bell-curve conditions; well-rounded; smoother than Explo 96; less exhilarating on firm than others but more maneuverable/forgiving off piste with good suspension in chop. Not as backseat-forgiving as More Forgiving skis. Agile: Kore 93; more stable: Kore 104.
- Armada AntiMatter 100 W (new) -- 129-99.5-119.5 (168); 16 m; 1720 & 1737 g; 57.5/6/28.5; 166.5; -7 / 76.3; 154-175; poplar + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Hit with reviewers; blurs line between metal-laminate and playful skis; nimble in tight steep zones yet intuitive to directional reviewers; lively, a bit more rockered. More engaging/precise on piste than Dancer 99 Ti/Kore 99, more agile than M7 Mantra W. Energetic, surfy, balanced.
- Icelantic Tempest 100 (new) -- 138.5-99.5-122.5 (169); 15 m; 1804 & 1834 g; 49.5/10.5/20.5; 168.5; -7 / 77.3; 162, 169, 176; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Nearly as precise/energetic as narrower Tempests with more stability in mixed snow; needs good technique/input; rare edge hold and rebound at ~100 mm; pop > suspension but stable when driven, lower swing weight. Strong tails need directional form when firm; not approachable in tight firm moguls.
- Dynastar M-Pro 98 W -- 133.5-99.5-123.5 (178); 17 m; 1817 & 1818 g; 67/8/23; 177.2; -8.5 / 80.1; 154-178; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Traditional-looking but M-Free-inspired rocker/mount; stiff directional, more stable than maneuverable, yet manageable in bumps if not tail-gunning. Quicker than M7 Mantra W/BP94; handles speed better than Dancer 99 Ti/Siren 98. Not ideal for aggressive carving on very firm; fun on fresh corduroy. More precision: Mindbender 96 W.
- K2 Mindbender 101 W (new) -- 130-100.5-121.5 (171); 17 m; 1732 & 1740 g; 50.5/3.5/30.5; 171.1; -6.7 / 78.8; 157, 164, 171; aspen/paulownia + 2 partial titanal + TPU/steel + fiberglass. Targeted mass weighting on less directional freeride chassis; strong and precise for width but deep rocker makes it easier to slash than equally grippy skis. 96 W more engaging/stable on firm; 101 W more agile, better for centered stance. Not as damp as Revolt 101 but closer than weights suggest, far lower swing weight. For playful-ski fans who like strong flex.
- Volkl Revolt 101 -- 132.5-100.5-114.5 (175); radii 22/17/20 m; 1979 & 2010 g; 63/4/28; 174.9; -7.2 / 80.3; 168-189; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Category-defying: as stiff/damp as the most stable skis here yet deeper rocker and more centered mount; drive shovels hard or ski centered; just not backseat in big moguls. Enables surfy style; loved by reviewers caught between heavy directional and loose freestyle skis. Agility over suspension: Siren 104.
- Volkl Mantra 102 W -- 141-101.7-123.8 (170); radii 26/16/23 m; 2010 & 2063 g; 59/3/17; 170.5; -12 / 74; 156-177; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. Benchmark for damp/stable claims; crud-erasing; manageable for skiers who like strong hefty skis. M7 more lively on piste; 102 more versatile off-piste. Similar to Santa Ana 102 (more forgiving in bumps, less precise). For less chatter, better firm grip, chop support.
- Atomic Maven 103 CTI -- 132-103.5-121 (178); 18 m; 1744 & 1921 g; 57/6.5/21.5; 177.1; -9.7 / 78.9; 156-178; poplar/ash + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Rare combo: carves very well (quick, precise on groomers) and at home in fresh snow (loose, surfy). Less slashy on firm; forward stance; agile for size. Not damp; knocked around in dense crud at speed.
- Moment Siren 104 -- 137.5-103.5-124 (176); 16.5 m; 1840 & 1848 g; 51.5/6.5/28; 174.6; -8 / 79.3; 170, 176; poplar/pine + carbon & fiberglass. Middle ground between directional and freestyle, cohesive; surfy and maneuverable in soft, precise/powerful when driven; stance-adaptable; punishes backseat in tight bumps; middle on dampness/agility. Vs Revolt 101: Siren more nimble/lively, Revolt damper/surfier.
- Nordica Santa Ana 102 -- 135-103.8-125.4 (179); 18.5 m; 2125 & 2139 g; 67/5/36; 177.4; -7.1 / 81.6; 155-179; poplar/beech + 1 partial titanal + elastomer layer + fiberglass. Damp, stable, slightly surfy; needs good technique; nearly unmatched composure at speed on imperfect snow; energetic when bent; tails release easily. Vs Mantra 102 W: less precise on firm, a bit livelier. Top pick for stiff, damp, stable all-mountain.
- Head Kore 104 Ti W -- 132.5-104.5-124.5 (177); 16.7 m; 1921 & 1930 g; 65.5/7/28; 176; -8 / 80; 156-177; poplar/PET + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Accessible for how well it handles chop/speed; one of easier-going off-piste options here; more effort than Kaizen 105 in tight bumps but more stable in deep chop. Carves clean when not icy (others grip better when scraped). Smooth easygoing ride with releasable tail.
- Folsom Cash 106 (customizable; standard build) -- 138-104.6-124 (176); 18 m; 1884 & 1894 g; 61/5/36.5; 174.4; -6.7 / 80.5; 164-189 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom available). Could fit either women's section depending on build; standard build can be pushed hard without being as demanding as Mantra 102/Dancer 106 Ti; versatile shape -- intuitive (not exhilarating) on piste, respectable float, maneuverable. Like livelier, more precise Kore 104 Ti or looser, agile Dancer 106 Ti.
- DPS Kaizen 105 (women's test, 179 cm) -- 134-105-118.2 (179); 18 m; 1762 & 1830 g; 49/6/22; 178; -10 / 79; 155-189; ash/poplar (horizontally laminated) + carbon. Agility/maneuverability defining; not planted in deep crud but smooths shallow variable well; for skiers who make more turns and creative lines; handles speed in soft/consistent snow if you stay on shovels. Not precise on firm groomers. More agile/lively Kore 104 Ti W or more forgiving Siren 104.
- Faction Dancer 106 Ti -- 133.7-105.6-123.5 (172); 18 m; 1958 & 1960 g; 66.5/4/26.5; 170.4; mounts Newschool -7.6/77.6, Progressive -9.1/76.1, Classic -10.6/74.6; 164-188; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Widest here but not a fresh-snow specialist; one of the better skis for skiing fast in chopped pow; strong, stable, lively; floats well; holds edge when driven. Mantra 102/Santa Ana 102 more planted; Dancer more energetic for aggressive-dynamic style; less backseat-forgiving than Kore 104/Kaizen 105.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Women's All-Mountain More Stable (top -> bottom):
- Best Edge Hold -> Worst: Mantra 84 W, M7 Mantra W, BP94, Santa Ana 92, Belle 90, BP88, BP84, Tempest 88, Tempest 94, Mindbender 96 W, Mantra 102 W, Santa Ana 102, Stance W Pro 88, Tempest 100, Maven 103 CTI, AntiMatter 100 W, Mindbender 101 W, Explo 86, Explo 96, Siren 98, Kore 87 Ti W, Kore 93 Ti W, Dancer 106 Ti, Ranger 90, M-Pro 92 W, Siren 104, Kore 99 Ti W, M-Pro 98 W, Revolt 101, Dancer 99 Ti, Kore 104 Ti W, Kaizen 105, Cash 106.
- Best Suspension/Damping -> Worst: Mantra 102 W, BP94, Santa Ana 102, Santa Ana 92, Kore 104 Ti W, Revolt 101, M7 Mantra W, Mindbender 96 W, M-Pro 98 W, Dancer 106 Ti, Cash 106, Kore 99 Ti W, Dancer 99 Ti, Mindbender 101 W, Tempest 100, AntiMatter 100 W, M-Pro 92 W, Mantra 84 W, Siren 104, Belle 90, Ranger 90, Stance W Pro 88, Siren 98, Kore 93 Ti W, Tempest 94, BP88, Tempest 88, Kore 87 Ti W, BP84, Maven 103 CTI, Kaizen 105, Explo 96, Explo 86.
- Best in Powder -> Worst: Maven 103 CTI, Kaizen 105, Siren 104, Cash 106, Dancer 106 Ti, Kore 104 Ti W, Revolt 101, Mindbender 101 W, Santa Ana 102, Mantra 102 W, M-Pro 98 W, Siren 98, AntiMatter 100 W, Mindbender 96 W, Dancer 99 Ti, Tempest 100, Kore 99 Ti W, Explo 96, M-Pro 92 W, Tempest 94, M7 Mantra W, Ranger 90, Kore 93 Ti W, BP94, Santa Ana 92, Belle 90, Explo 86, Kore 87 Ti W, Tempest 88, Stance W Pro 88, BP88, BP84, Mantra 84 W.

### Women's All-Mountain Skis -- More Forgiving [L2844-]
Reviewer pictured: Kristin Sinnott (Palisades Tahoe; Crested Butte). Class overview: same women's-section framing; "forgiving" vs "stable" are broad. More Forgiving skis generally (1) work well at slower speeds, (2) don't immediately punish backseat, (3) need less input/power. Many suit beginners/intermediates as everyday setups, but many also work for aggressive skiing in soft/consistent snow, and for finesse-oriented advanced/expert skiers wanting lighter, nimbler skis.

- Renoun Earhart 88 -- 126.4-86.6-110.6 (170); 14.5 m; 1364 & 1392 g; 70/5/10; 166.5; -9 / 74.2; 149, 156, 163; maple + 2 partial titanal + VibeStop + carbon & fiberglass. Extremely nimble yet adaptable thanks to non-Newtonian VibeStop (stiffens with force); can chatter with neutral stance but with input more stable than nearly any similarly light ski. Tapered, rockered -- easy to pivot; fun to carve consistent groomers; heavier skis better in deep crud. Broad appeal incl. experienced finesse skiers as daily driver.
- Faction Prodigy 1 -- 119.5-87-111.5 (171); 16 m; 1708 & 1711 g; 65.5/6/47.5; 168.4; -5.0 / 79.2; 158-184; poplar/ash + carbon/rubber reinforcement underfoot + fiberglass. Bridges Dancer (directional) and Studio (freestyle); more maneuverable/playful than most at width, not sketchy at speed; damper than lighter options; less set-back mount easy to pilot, still intuitive to directional reviewers. Carves well for how surfy it is.
- Meier Quickdraw 92W -- 133.2-92-124.4 (173); 21 m; 1840 & 1850 g; 55/5/42; 171.5; -9 / 77; 147-173; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Looks like a pure carver but blends on/off piste; among dampest/stablest here; little rocker/taper yet easy to skid in shallow snow; more accessible than More Stable skis; secure on edge when driven. Ripstick 94 W more nimble; Prodigy 1 easier in soft. Handles aggressive firm skiing better. Off-piste-biased alternative: QST 94.
- Elan Ripstick 94 W -- 129.5-93.5-109 (168); 15.8 m; 1550 & 1603 g; 50/3/20; 167.2; -7 / 76.6; 147-175; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip layer + fiberglass. Approachable for newcomers, exciting for experts; nimble, lively; soft flex for easy initiation; stance-tolerant. Quickness/energy over suspension. Blaze 94 better ultra-precise light carving; QST 94 similarly loose with better suspension; Earhart 88 similar agility with more speed potential.
- Salomon QST 94 -- 134-94-120 (180); 16.5 m; 1862 & 1931 g; 63/2.5/31.5; 178.7; -9.6 / 79.8; 156-188; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + basalt + cork tip/tail inserts + fiberglass. Quick reviewer favorite: easy to ski, not limited to specific conditions; stands out for maneuverability/playfulness off piste; shines in trees, moguls, steeps (firm or soft); smoother/more predictable than most equally maneuverable skis. Looser: QST 100.
- Volkl Blaze 94 (women's test) -- specs as in 50/50. Maximizes on-piste excitement; edge hold like heavier metal Volkls on smooth snow; explosive rebound when pushed; not stable/planted. Could be a downhill touring, 50/50, or piste-focused all-mountain ski. Comparably agile to Ripstick 94 W but stiffer, much better edge hold, less forgiving in bumps.
- Blizzard Sheeva 9 -- 129-96-118 (174); 16 m; 1751 & 1752 g; 79/4/41; 174.3; -7.7 / 78; 150-174; poplar/paulownia/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. More approachable than Black Pearls; mix of on-piste precision and off-piste maneuverability; easygoing but pushable; good for progressing skiers and confident skiers wanting non-demanding ski. Damper/stabler/easier to pivot than Blaze 94/Ripstick 94 W, less lively/forgiving than Ripstick. Looser: QST 94; more energy: Canvas 100.
- RMU Valhalla 97 -- 125.5-96.5-120 (172); 17 m; 1598 & 1607 g; 25/5/15.5; 170.3; mounts FR -4.3/80.9, TR -6.3/78.9; 162, 172; poplar + beech underfoot + fiberglass. One of best for maneuverability/playfulness; tapered, deep rocker "skis shorter"; tips knocked around in crud more than QST 94/Unleashed 98; fun on soft groomers, not for very firm high-edge carving. Even more agile/lively than Completo 100/QST 100; freestyle-friendly. Looser: Valhalla 107.
- Renoun Endurance 98 -- 133.2-96.8-118.4 (184); 18 m; 1901 & 1902 g; 68/4/29; 182.2; -10.1 / 81; 163-184; aspen + 6 VibeStop channels + 2 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Confidence-inspiring stability at speed (among the most stable here) yet soft/accessible at slow speed thanks to VibeStop. Versatile: respectable on piste, loose off piste. Unleashed 98 better on very firm; Prodigy 2 for centered stance. Rare low-speed ease + high-speed composure. Agility: Earhart 88.
- Faction Prodigy 2 -- 126.5-97-118.5 (171); 16 m; 1817 & 1822 g; 64.5/4.5/51; 168.5; -6.1 / 78.2; 159-183; poplar + carbon/rubber reinforcement underfoot + fiberglass. Freestyle-inspired maneuverability without being specialized; very easy to release; light swing weight; respectable edge hold/suspension on firm vs QST 100/Valhalla 97; stance-adaptable. Nearly as precise as Unleashed 98 but damper and more maneuverable.
- Nordica Unleashed 98 -- 132.4-97.5-121.3 (174); 17.4 m; 1741 & 1747 g; 70.5/3/54.5; 171.8; -7.2 / 78.7; 156-186; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. One of Nordica's most playful; quick in bumps (more than Santa Ana), more planted at speed than lighter skis; lively, balanced in air, carves really well. For playful skiers who find playful skis lacking edge hold; directional reviewers also liked it. Looser: Valhalla 97, QST 100.
- Line Pandora 99 -- 130.5-98.5-120 (170); 16 m; 1711 & 1748 g; 57/3.5/39; 169.3; -4.6 / 80.1; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass. Adaptable all-mountain with freestyle influence; agility over damping but good suspension for weight; centered mount, works with forward stance too. Completo 100/QST 100/Maiden 102 pivot easier; Pandora more precise on piste, looser than Unleashed 98/Endurance 98. Notably well-rounded.
- Folsom Completo 100 -- 129.3-98.9-119.3 (174); 16 m; 1666 & 1668 g; 70/3/43; 171.6; -5.3 / 80.5; 156-186 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Exceptionally nimble/surfy; highly tapered, deep rocker; forgiving ends but support up front. Compromises: imprecise on firm, deflected in crud more than Pandora 99/Canvas 100. More precision: Folsom Cash, Primary, or custom.
- Blizzard Canvas 100 (new) -- 131.5-99.5-119 (174); 16.5 m; 1692 & 1705 g; 70/5/51; 172; -5.8 / 80.2; 162-186; poplar/paulownia + partial titanal + carbon stringers + fiberglass. More playful/agile than Sheevas; impressed many reviewers; like a quicker, more forgiving, freestyle-friendly Sheeva 10; intuitive for directional skiers; stands out among playful skis for precision and stability-to-weight. As nimble as Ripstick 100 W/Pandora 99 but more composed in soft chop; easier to slash than Unleashed 98 without much less precision.
- Rossignol Sender 100 -- 130.5-99.5-122.5 (178); 18 m; 1901 & 1923 g; 54/9.5/53; 175.9; -4 / 84; 162-190; poplar + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. Freestyle/freeride mix; strong preference for centered upright stance (learning curve). More stable than many here without being sluggish; lighter swing weight than weight suggests; handles speed and big airs; carves firm respectably, looser in soft. Strong flex + long tail punishes backseat in bumps; directional skiers: Unleashed 98.
- Salomon QST 100 -- 134-99.5-121.5 (180); 17.5 m; 1944 & 1995 g; 66/5/39; 178.5; -8.7 / 80.6; 148-188; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + basalt + cork inserts + fiberglass. Most playful of Salomon freeride line; smooth ride; like QST 94 but less directional, more centered-friendly, looser tail; very nice suspension -- experienced skiers can push it. Not for borderline-icy carving; otherwise rarely the wrong tool; works for aggressive skiers and newcomers. Better edge hold: Canvas 100, Pandora 99.
- Elan Ripstick 100 W -- 136-100.5-116.5 (175); 17.8 m; 1680 & 1693 g; 46/5/16; 174.2; -6.9 / 80.2; 154-175; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip + fiberglass. Nimble, lively; engaging without speed; good edge hold underfoot (less eager than 94 W); easy to flick but not particularly loose/surfy; tails not very backseat-forgiving (less punishing than Sender 100). Best at moderate speeds, dynamic style; knocked around in crud.
- Fischer Ranger 102 -- 136.3-101.2-126.1 (176); 18 m; 1880 & 1916 g; 66.5/3.5/35; 174.4; -10.1 / 77.1; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Could fit either women's section: soft surfy shovels, strong powerful tails (punishing in bumps if centered/backseat). Easy carving on most groomers, surprisingly loose off piste, standout stability in soft chop; edge hold concentrated near bindings -- not for super firm. For experienced skiers wanting easy maneuverability with stronger directional flex.
- Blizzard Sheeva 10 -- 134-102-123 (180); 17.5 m; 1851 & 1856 g; 75/4/39; 178; -7 / 81.5; 156-180; poplar/paulownia/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Off-piste maneuverability with on-piste precision; well-rounded for intermediate-expert; not very backseat-forgiving; stiff mid, precise. Vs Unleashed 98: more demanding in tight terrain but floats/turns easier in fresh; vs Ranger 102: more secure on edge, more forgiving. Lighter/surfier: Canvas 100.
- Icelantic Maiden 102 -- stated 137-102-129 (176); 18 m; 1830 & 1835 g; 69/5.5/57.5; 173.1; mounts "PC" -4.8/81.8, "BC" -6.8/79.8; 155-176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Balance of stability and playfulness; deep rocker, soft ends -- very surfy, easy in tight terrain (more than Sheeva 10); minimal taper & moderate weight more stable than similarly maneuverable skis; huge sweet spot (drive hard or ski centered). Not precise on very firm; floats well; handles soft chop. Smoother: Line Optic 104.
- Line Optic 104 -- 131.8-103.5-123.2 (178); 19 m; 2074 & 2088 g; 56.5/4/44.5; 177.6; -6.7 / 82.1; 166-190; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Standout for damp-ski fans: heaviest in section, smooth in rough snow, yet one of most freestyle-friendly (deep rocker, centered mount, soft ends), loose, balanced in air, lively. Stiff mid gives decent edge hold, soft tips easy to initiate. For those wanting heavy planted ride without precision/demanding directional feel. Lower swing weight: Pandora 99.
- Wagner Summit 106 (women's test, 172 cm) -- 139-105-124.5 (172); 17.4 m; 1790 & 1798 g; 68/3/30; 170.0; -9.6 / 75.4; 165-186 (custom). Fairly light, highly adaptable wider all-mountain for directional skiers; excellent carver for width yet easy to release/pivot; "skis you forget about" (compliment). For those who like Pandora 106 but not its centered mount, or Maiden but want more support. Damp/heavy: Summit 107 or custom.
- Armada ARV 106 -- 131.5-105.5-124.5 (180); 20 m; 2010 & 2031 g; 53.5/8/50.5; 178.4; mounts Freestyle -2.6/86.6, Factory -5.1/84.1; 164-188; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Freestyle approach to whole mountain; one of most playful here; balanced in air, agile for weight; weight + strong mid give composure at speed; directional reviewers got along too. Pandora 106/Sheeva 10 more precise on piste; ARV more forgiving in bumps. Rear-mount fans/flotation: Maiden 108, Summit 106.
- Line Pandora 106 -- 133.5-105.5-125 (176); 16.7 m; 1912 & 1931 g; 58/3/41; 175.3; -4.9 / 82.8; 155-189; aspen + fiberglass. Keeps all-conditions versatility of narrower skis; damp/stable enough to ski hard, agile, lively; similar to Optic 104 (heavier, damper) with lower swing weight and equal/better firm carving. Less loose than ARV 106 but more precise. Playful yet not light/imprecise on firm.
- RMU Valhalla 107 -- 133.5-106-123 (172); 18.5 m; 1706 & 1733 g; 61/3/34.5; 170.8; mounts FR -4.1/81.3, TR -6.1/79.3; 162, 172; poplar + beech mounting zone + fiberglass. Powder-inspired in practical silhouette; exceptionally maneuverable/agile in soft and tight terrain; costs firm precision and crud stability. Easy, non-fatiguing for less experienced; quick and hoppy for playful experts; centered stance. Firmer versatility: Valhalla 97.
- Blizzard Canvas 108 (women's test, 174) -- 138-107.5-126 (174); 17 m; 1657 & 1684 g; 71/4.5/54; 172.1; -5.8 / 80.2; 168-192. Defies specs: light, centered mount, lots of rocker, yet very impressive suspension and firm grip (better than Valhalla 107). Much more agile/playful than Sheeva 10 without being much less fun on piste. Comparable maneuverability to Maiden 108/Optic 104 with more energy and secure piste feel. Playful alternative to Summit 106.
- Icelantic Maiden 108 -- stated 143-108-135 (176); 18 m; 1821 & 1835 g; 66/2.5/59.5; 173.2; mounts "PC" -4.4/82.2, "BC" -6.4/80.2; 162, 169, 176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Also a women's pow pick; soft ends forgiving and absorb crud impressively for weight; very easy to slip/slide; solid carver on soft slopes. Vs 102: 102 more agile on firm, 108 more composed at speed in soft chop. Surfy, playful, pushable.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Women's All-Mountain More Forgiving (top -> bottom):
- Easiest in Tight Terrain -> Most Difficult: Completo 100, Valhalla 107, Prodigy 1, Earhart 88, Valhalla 97, Ripstick 100 W, Ripstick 94 W, Canvas 100, Maiden 102, Canvas 108, Prodigy 2, QST 100, Summit 106, ARV 106, QST 94, Pandora 106, Maiden 108, Pandora 99, Optic 104, Sheeva 10, Sender 100, Endurance 98, Unleashed 98, Sheeva 9, Blaze 94, Quickdraw 92W, Ranger 102.
- Most Stable in Crud -> Least: Optic 104, Endurance 98, Maiden 108, Ranger 102, ARV 106, Summit 106, Pandora 106, QST 100, Sheeva 10, Maiden 102, Canvas 108, Sender 100, Quickdraw 92W, QST 94, Prodigy 2, Unleashed 98, Sheeva 9, Canvas 100, Pandora 99, Valhalla 107, Completo 100, Valhalla 97, Prodigy 1, Ripstick 100 W, Earhart 88, Blaze 94, Ripstick 94 W.
- Best in Deep Snow -> Worst: Maiden 108, Valhalla 107, Summit 106, Canvas 108, Pandora 106, ARV 106, Maiden 102, Sheeva 10, Ranger 102, Optic 104, Completo 100, QST 100, Valhalla 97, Pandora 99, Canvas 100, Unleashed 98, Prodigy 2, Endurance 98, Ripstick 100 W, QST 94, Sender 100, Sheeva 9, Blaze 94, Prodigy 1, Ripstick 94 W, Earhart 88, Quickdraw 92W.

### Women's Powder Skis [L3626-]
Reviewers pictured: Sascha Anastas, Kristin Sinnott (Snowbird). Class overview: same women's framing; skis excel in deep soft snow -- wider, more tip/tail rocker; range from directional to playful. Pow days include chop/crud, so performance in tracked conditions also described. Some also appear in other sections and could be daily drivers at snowy areas.

- Folsom Cash 106 (specs above) -- not Folsom's most pow-focused but versatile: enough weight for cut-up pow while quick; less surfy than ARV 106/Valhalla 107 but more stable and precise on edge (appreciated on tired legs). Deep days: Folsom's wider skis / Cash 117. Enjoyable with little new snow, fine up to a foot.
- Prior Flute ("Quad-Glass" build; customizable) -- 132.2-104.9-119.8 (163); 17 m; 1500 & 1504 g; 73/2/26; 162.3; -9.2 / 72; 163, 169, 175 (custom); yellow-cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Seriously impressive fresh-snow performance for modest width; light and nimble -- great 50/50; deep rocker & tapered shovel turn easier in deep snow than some wider skis; holds edge at speed; impressive damping for weight. Forward stance but playful swing weight; heavier skis deflect less in dense chop. Directional alternative to Valhalla 107.
- Wagner Summit 106 (172; specs above) -- impressed every reviewer; floats well for most storm days; less loose than ARV 106/Valhalla 107 but carves better on firm, forgiving for a directional ski; suits wide ability range; commendable suspension for weight; excellent 50/50.
- Faction Dancer 106 Ti (172; specs above) -- for aggressive skiers in all conditions; supportive yet surfier in soft snow; prefers bigger faster turns but manageable in tight ones; less surfy/forgiving on firm; tails release easily in soft; impressive composure/edge hold in chop/crud/firm. Playful alternatives: Armada, RMU, Icelantic options, Faction Prodigy.
- Line Pandora 106 (176) -- versatile but playful/soft-snow maneuverable; more forgiving/surfy than Dancer 106 Ti, enough mass for respectable chop stability; works for directional and playful skiers; carves well. ARV 106/Valhalla 107 looser; Maiden 108/Summit 106 float a bit better and deflect less. The well-rounded middle option.
- Armada ARV 106 (180) -- adaptable playful platform from slush to deep; ARV 112 floats more but 106 shines in shallow/chopped resort snow; directional reviewers impressed by composure in heavy snow/crud. Valhalla 107 quicker; ARV 106 better at speed/firm. Directional alternatives: Maiden 108, Summit 106.
- RMU Valhalla 107 (172) -- like a miniaturized pow ski; short effective edge & deep rocker very easy to release in pow, slush, firm; centered upright stance; can double as all-mountain if maneuverability > edge hold. ARV 106/Pandora 106 carve better and like bigger faster turns; nice suspension for weight.
- Icelantic Maiden 108 (176) -- avoids tradeoffs: buoyant yet versatile, stable yet forgiving; works centered or forward; handles chop well for weight; wide soft rockered shovels plane well. Sheeva 11 stiffer for aggressive directional skiers. For intermediates to experienced playful skiers.
- Dynastar M-Free 112 -- 140.5-110.5-132.5 (183); 21 m; 2256 & 2271 g; 63.5/7.5/52; 181.7; -3.4 / 87.5; 176, 183, 190; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. In many sections. Surfy maneuverability + class-leading suspension; maybe more fun in soft chop than untracked; flotation not super impressive for width (strong flex, centered mount) but easy to pivot. Agile and forgiving for its composure. More stable ARV 112, or quicker less directional Black Crows Anima. Not a great carver; rare combo in soft snow.
- Blizzard Sheeva 11 -- 139.5-111.5-129 (180); 19 m; 1959 & 1986 g; 77/3/42; 179; -7.6 / 81.3; 168, 174, 180; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. All-mountain versatility in deeper shape; playful yet directional; one of better ~112 mm carvers. Less float/maneuverability than wider skis at slow speed in deep snow; comes alive in resort-pow-day chop (strong minimally tapered tips drive through piles). More float/playful: Canvas 118.
- Armada ARV 112 -- 134.5-111.5-129.5 (185); 21 m; 1998 & 2026 g; 55/7/54; 183.4; mounts Freestyle -2.8/88.9, Factory -5.2/86.5; 165-192; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Most freestyle-focused; low swing weight, deep rocker, easy slashing from centered stance; supportive/damp enough in heavy pow/soft chop; can drive shovels a bit. Max composure: M-Free 112, Anima. Directional: QST Blank.
- Salomon S/Lab QST Blank -- 138.5-112-127 (178); 18 m; 2000 & 2047 g; 69/5.5/36.5; 176.5; -7.9 / 80.6; 170-192; poplar + basalt + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Freeride/freestyle merge; very impressive flotation and maneuverability for width; shovels plane better than center-mounted ARV 112/M-Free 112; respectably composed in soft chop; tips knocked around more than M-Free/Anima in dense snow; practical in shallow/firm; strong tail discourages backseat (helps landings). Maneuverable pow ski that isn't pow-specific.
- Black Crows Anima -- 145-114.5-134 (182.1); 19 m (all lengths); 2306 & 2332 g; 63/6/54.5; 180.3; -6.4 / 83.8; 176.6, 182.1, 189.2, 194.4; poplar + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. For surfy pow-ski fans who love charging cut-up snow; blasts heavy pow/dense chop/crud better than nearly every ski here; loose and playful aside from big swing weight; forward or centered. Much damper, slightly surfier Sheeva 11 or more stable, less maneuverable QST Blank. Looser/freestyle: M-Free 112.
- Salomon S/Lab QST X (women's test, 178) -- 138-116-125 (178); 24 m; 1826 & 1845 g; 61/4.5/28.5; 177.1; -5.6 / 83; 178, 184, 192. Most powder-focused here; unrivaled surfiness; deceptively light; great slarves, quick pivots and predictable long arcs. Not for crud/firm (short edge, deep rocker, long radius). Skiable hard in low-density chop; excellent touring/50/50 option.
- Blizzard Canvas 118 (women's test, 180) -- 144.5-117-133.5 (180); 19 m; 1760 & 1774 g; 78/4.5/65; 177.8; -5.9 / 83; 172, 180, 188. Similar-looking to Sheeva 11 but much more float, maneuverability, playfulness, and not dramatically less stable in soft chop despite light weight; good for touring too; carves oddly well; freestyle-friendly. Versatility: Canvas 108.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Women's Powder (top -> bottom):
- Most Stable in Chop -> Least: Anima, M-Free 112, Sheeva 11, QST Blank, ARV 112, Dancer 106 Ti, Canvas 118, ARV 106, Cash 106, Maiden 108, QST X, Pandora 106, Summit 106, Valhalla 107, Prior Flute.
- Most Surfy/Loose -> Least: QST X, ARV 112, QST Blank, Valhalla 107, M-Free 112, Canvas 118, Anima, Maiden 108, ARV 106, Flute, Pandora 106, Summit 106, Sheeva 11, Cash 106, Dancer 106 Ti.
- Most Flotation -> Least: Canvas 118, QST X, QST Blank, Anima, M-Free 112, Maiden 108, ARV 112, Sheeva 11, Summit 106, ARV 106, Pandora 106, Valhalla 107, Cash 106, Dancer 106 Ti, Flute. (Notes on chart: shorter lengths would rank lower / longer lengths higher.)

### On-Piste Carving Skis [L4086-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Snowbird), Paul Forward (Crested Butte). Class overview: all designed for carving groomers, but varied -- accessible vs demanding race-inspired; light/lively vs heavy/planted; slalom-like tight turns vs long arcs. Not adaptable off piste like Frontside/All-Mountain skis -- piste-specific tools, which is why they offer better edge hold, rebound, edge-to-edge quickness. Weights often include binding plates.

- Atomic Redster X9S Revoshock S -- 113.5-64.5-99 (174); 14.8 m; 2236 & 2237 g (w/ plates, no brakes); 39/8.5/6; 173.7; -12.4 / 74.5; 166, 174, 182; beech/poplar + 2 titanal + steel/elastomer modules + carbon & fiberglass. Demanding side of Redster line with G9/S9; needs input; exceptional edge hold on very firm, high-speed composure; most versatile across slalom-GS turn sizes. More accessible/lively than similar-looking Fischer RC4 Noize. Not for soft inconsistent snow.
- Fischer RC4 Noize -- 111-65-99.5 (178); 15.5 m; 2335 & 2342 g (w/ plates); 44.5/9/5.5; 176.6; -11.2 / 77.1; 168-183; beech + 2 titanal + printed metal pigment + fiberglass. "Noize Control" tech; very strong, damp, game-on; radius feels longer than stated; demands input; excellent edge hold, smooth on very firm; GS-ski itch without pushing as hard. Accessible damp alternative: Curv Noize GT 85.
- Atomic Redster G9 Revoshock S -- 109.5-66.5-96 (176); 17 m; 2153 & 2157 g (w/ plates, no brakes); 41.5/9/5.5; 175.1; -12 / 75.6; 166-181; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer modules + carbon & fiberglass. GS-inspired, more accessible/lively than RC4 Noize; medium-long turns; excels on steeper, longer runs; bogs down in variable. X9S more versatile in turn sizes; G9 more composed at bigger arcs/speed. Wider conditions: Stockli Montero AR, Head Supershape e-Titan.
- Atomic Redster S9 Revoshock S -- 119-67.5-104.5 (170); 13.1 m; 2148 & 2166 g (w/ plates, no brakes); 39.5/8.5/6.5; 169.2; -13.2 / 71.4; 155-170; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Slalom excitement without needing A-game; not wildly stiff; requires input but predictable when not committing -- good for dialing tight-turn technique; excellent firm grip and energy, ideal for moderate slopes/short runs. Holds up better than easier tight-turners.
- Head Supershape e-Magnum -- 125.5-71.5-109.5 (170); 13.1 m; 2003 & 2011 g (w/ plates); 46.5/10.5/5; 169; mount N/A (plate); 149-177; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + "Energy Management Circuits" + carbon & fiberglass. Great recreational tight-turn carver for firm consistent snow; precise and energetic vs e-Rally/e-Titan; predictable in bigger arcs if you ease off. Forza 70 Ti lighter/more accessible; e-Magnum better edge hold & stability. Easy to recommend.
- Volkl Peregrine 72 -- 122-71.5-102.5 (173); radii 21/15/17 m; 2180 & 2193 g (w/ plates); 34/6/9; 173.1; -12.6 / 74; 158-178; beech/poplar + titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Well-rounded for firmer conditions; less demanding than race-inspired skis; more stable than e-Magnum; intuitive initiation, excellent edge hold across speeds. Peregrine 82 more predictable in mixed snow; 72 easier and more rebound. Curv Noize GTX more precise/energetic but harder.
- Atomic Redster Q7 Revoshock C -- 126-74.5-110 (174); 14.2 m; 1748 & 1778 g (w/ plates); 42.5/10/6; 173.2; mount N/A (plate); 153-181; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer + fiberglass. Accessible Q series; much lighter/softer than Q9 with same dims; agile, lively; wide range of turns; skittish at high speed in variable groomers. Best at moderate paces. Wider: Q7.8.
- Atomic Redster Q9 Revoshock S -- 126.5-74.5-109.5 (174); 14.2 m; 2114 & 2115 g (w/ plates, no brakes); 43/8.5/5.5; 173; -12.2 / 74.3; 153-181; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Stands out for adaptability; easy initiation yet not limited; impressive and accessible edge hold on ice; mid-bell-curve turn shapes; much better suspension than Q7 without much more demand. Q9.8 better in softer variable. Broad appeal.
- Fischer The Curv Noize GTX -- 125-75.5-108 (175); 15 m; 2160 & 2178 g (w/ plates); 48/11.5/5.5; 173.8; -10 / 76.9; 161-182; "solid woodcore" + 2 titanal + printed metal pigment + carbon & fiberglass. Exhilarating for experienced skiers; energetic rebound over suspension; precise, powerful, lively, locked-in; not intuitive unless committed. Like a wider, more energetic Redster S9. Twitchy/hooky in variable. Easier: e-Magnum, Peregrine 72.
- Rossignol Forza 70 Ti -- 133.1-76.5-109.5 (173); 14 m; 1701 & 1706 g; 44/9/7; 172.5; -10 / 76; 163, 173, 181; poplar + ~1.5 titanal layers + carbon, basalt & fiberglass. Tight-turning fun for the masses; one of most accessible here despite "expert" Forza naming; fat tips, quick initiation, easy to feather; calmer at speed than weight suggests but not damp; less locked-in on super firm, more predictable in variable.
- Head Supershape e-Rally -- 127.5-77.5-111.5 (177); 15.3 m; 2199 & 2211 g (w/ plates); 51/11/11; 175.7; mount N/A (plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + EMC + carbon & fiberglass. Excellent suspension and versatility; notably stiff/damp recreational carver; can be pushed hard into fairly tight carves yet intuitive to skid casually, rarely hooky.
