Needs more input on very firm than Peregrine 82/Q9.8. Learners: e-Magnum; longer turns: e-Titan. Standout smooth, planted short-medium turner.
- Parlor Warbird (standard build; custom available) -- 130.9-77.7-107.1 (178); 16 m; 1724 & 1735 g; 67/4/9; 176.6; -10.8 / 77.5; 148-192; maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass (custom). Precision instrument with playful streak; accessible flex; very easy to get on edge; one of easier skis to carve ice (doesn't need speed/aggression); light, agile, lively. For damp/high-speed: heavier custom build or e-Rally. Longer turns with support: Dancer 79 Ti.
- Faction Dancer 79 Ti -- 118-78.5-106.5 (178); 17 m; 1687 & 1693 g; 52/11.5/18; 176.6; mounts Newschool -6.9/81.4, Progressive -8.4/79.9, Classic -9.9/78.4; 158-184; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Most piste-focused Dancer; proper carver for short-medium turns valuing energy/agility over suspension; easy to bend yet strong edge hold; not hooky in variable but twitchy in soft/deep crud due to low weight. Peregrine 82/e-Titan for bigger, faster turns in unpredictable snow.
- Renoun Atlas 80 -- 126.9-78.5-111.6 (177); 15 m; 1728 & 1750 g; 55/7/13; 173.1; -6.2 / 80.4; 163-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + carbon & fiberglass. VibeStop: soft and easy at slow speed, stiffer/more stable when driven; fun on mellow slopes, surprisingly composed at speed; but fights tight sidecut if driven hard in big turns; speed-sensitive flex takes adjustment.
- Nordica Dobermann Multipista 80 DC (new) -- 125-79.5-103.5 (175); 16 m; 2233 & 2242 g (w/ plates); 48.5/8/9; 174.2; -8.1 / 79; 170-185; wood + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Race-inspired Dobermann tuned for versatility across piste conditions; tapered rockered shovel not hooky; notably damp smooth; strong precise middle, looser ends; requires deliberate input to hook up shovels, less precision/pop at turn finish. Excellent mixed-conditions carver for strong skiers. Easier damp options: Nordica Steadfast 85, Curv Noize GT 85.
- Volkl Peregrine 80 -- 126-79.5-109 (177); radii 27/15/23 m; 1941 & 1968 g; 35.5/6.5/10.5; 178.1; mount N/A (plate); 162-182; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. More easygoing Peregrine 82 but far from beginner-only; big cruisy turns or medium/short arcs when driven; clean on very firm; easy to feather/release. Slightly less damp/stable at very high speed than 82. Safer bet for intermediate-expert wanting accessibility.
- Volkl Peregrine 82 -- 128-81.5-111 (177); radii 27/15/23 m; 2000 & 2016 g; 37/6/12; 178; mount N/A (plate); 162-182; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Reviewers' go-to when piste conditions unknown (corduroy to ice to crud); for more advanced/aggressive; better edge hold, damper at speed; grip tends to hold or break rather than feather. Not as game-on as race carvers; versatile turn shapes like Q9.8. More planted: Curv Noize GT 85.
- K2 Blur XT FDT -- 135-82.5-114.5 (177); 14.9 m; 2213 & 2214 g (w/ plates); 43.5/5.5/7.5; 177.5; -11.6 / 77.1; 149-184; aspen + partial titanal + flax & fiberglass. Very accessible without skimping on ride quality; easy on edge, easy to change edge angles or skid; still carves clean on very firm; smooth/damp in harsh snow. Gives up composure/precision when pushed very hard (wants tight turns, tail releases). Excellent ski to progress on -- beginners/intermediates won't quickly outgrow it.
- Kastle MX84 -- 126.5-82.5-111.5 (176); 16.3 m; 1940 & 1949 g; 53/7.5/9; 174.1; -11.6 / 75.4; 155-183; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Stands out on piste: tenaciously precise on ice, tons of energy; like wider Curv Noize GTX needing less force. Not forgiving -- happy skidding or high edge angles but transitions need finesse. More agile than specs suggest; suspension less smooth than Montero AR/Supershapes. Race-ski energy in a more variable-friendly silhouette.
- Stockli Montero AR -- 127.5-83-113 (180); 17.4 m; 2058 & 2079 g; 47/9/11.5; 178.7; -12.3 / 77; 165-185; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Strong case for Frontside but best as mixed-conditions piste ski; easy-bending shovel not hooky in soft snow; stiffer mid/tail with solid edge hold and powerful finish; not eager on low angles; medium-long carves for intermediate-expert; for those who dislike heavy dead carvers. Tighter: Q9.8.
- Head Supershape e-Titan -- 128.5-83.5-112.5 (177); 17.2 m; 2121 & 2147 g (w/ plates); 62/9/12; 175.4; mount N/A (plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + EMC + carbon & fiberglass. Suited to variable groomers at speed; not snappy/easy; excels at aggressive GS-inspired carving icy to chewed-up. Vs e-Rally: Rally more planted/shorter turns; e-Titan more composed and lively when driven hard. Accessible damp alternatives: Steadfast 85, Curv Noize GT 85.
- Atomic Redster Q7.8 Revoshock C -- 135-84.5-120 (181); 15.2 m; 2090 & 2131 g (w/ plates); 40/12/5.5; 180.2; mount N/A (plate); 159-181; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer + fiberglass. Wider Q7: accessible energy/agility, less deterred by soft cruddy snow; mid-range turn shapes; engaging for strong skiers, accessible for less experienced. Knocked around on firm rough snow more than other ~85 mm skis; good in slush. More suspension: Q9.8.
- Atomic Redster Q9.8 Revoshock S -- 135-84.5-120 (181); 15.2 m; 2362 & 2439 g (w/ plates, no brakes); 43/6/6.5; 180; -12 / 78; 159-181; ash/poplar + titanal + steel/elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Impressive all-conditions carver: commendable suspension and excellent edge hold for width; livelier than Curv Noize GT 85; holds edge through crud much better than Q7.8; not far off K2 Blur XT/Steadfast 85 accessibility while handling more speed. Not for really long turns or only smooth firm snow. Easy to recommend.
- Fischer The Curv Noize GT 85 (new "Noize" version) -- 133.5-84.5-117 (175); 15 m; 2380 & 2391 g (w/ plates); 49.5/13/6; 174.1; -10.9 / 76.2; 161-182; beech/poplar + 2 titanal + printed metal pigment + fiberglass. Same shape as favorite Curv GT 85, updated internals: even smoother, more planted -- extremely impressive suspension; short-medium-long turns like Q9.8; grips on ice, excels in inconsistent snow. Costs some rebound; not agile/dynamic, but snaps back when driven; easy to get on edge. Well-rounded.
- Nordica Steadfast 85 DC -- 136-84.5-115.5 (179); 15.8 m; 2096 & 2154 g; 51/7/11.5; 177.7; -7.3 / 81.6; 162-179; wood + 1 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Damp but not demanding; predictable in unpredictable conditions for huge ability range; slightly less edge hold/composure than Peregrine 82/Montero AR but much more accessible; suspension matches or beats them; easy to feather. Softer easygoing Dobermann Multipista 80, feels more precise to most. Hard to "fold"; reviewers occasionally take it off piste.
- Rossignol Arcade 88 -- 134.5-87-123.5 (178); 15 m; 2045 & 2057 g; 57.5/7/13.5; 175.7; -6.7 / 81.2; 154-186; poplar + ABS stringer + titanal + elastomer & AirTip inserts + fiberglass. Power/precision delivered differently: rockered shovels not eager to dive in; roll ankles then apply force and very stiff mid/tail deliver precision, power, energy. Engaging on low angles, supports aggressive angles on steeps; not best for long turns; nice suspension not dead; good in soft variable yet holds on ice. Off-piste-friendlier: Arcade 94.
- RMU Zephyr 88 -- 134-87.5-116.5 (176); 16.5 m; 1768 & 1826 g; 58/6/14; 174.2; -8.1 / 79; 168, 176, 184; poplar + beech mounting plate + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. RMU's exception to surfy lineup; soft shovels easy on edge, hold strong; ease into turns like Steadfast 85; predictable big low-angle turns, can be bent SL-GS sizes; nicer suspension than most light skis; strong tails pop. Better firm grip: Q7.8.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- On-Piste Carving (top -> bottom):
- Most Accessible -> Most Demanding: Forza 70 Ti, Steadfast 85 DC, Parlor Warbird, Redster Q7, Zephyr 88, Blur XT, e-Magnum, Peregrine 72, Q7.8, Atlas 80, Dancer 79 Ti, Peregrine 80, Q9, Q9.8, Curv Noize GT 85, Montero AR, Arcade 88, Peregrine 82, e-Rally, MX84, Curv Noize GTX, Redster S9, e-Titan, Dobermann MP 80, X9S, G9, RC4 Noize.
- Best for Longer Turns -> Best for Shorter Turns: e-Titan, G9, Montero AR, RC4 Noize, Dobermann MP 80, e-Rally, Peregrine 82, Q9.8, Peregrine 80, X9S, Q7.8, Dancer 79 Ti, Q9, Zephyr 88, Blur XT, Steadfast 85, Curv GT 85, Arcade 88, MX84, Peregrine 72, Q7, Curv GTX, e-Magnum, Warbird, Atlas 80, Forza 70 Ti, S9.
- Most Damp -> Least Damp: Curv Noize GT 85, RC4 Noize, e-Rally, Q9.8, Dobermann MP 80, e-Titan, Blur XT, G9, X9S, Steadfast 85, S9, Q9, Peregrine 82, Arcade 88, Montero AR, Peregrine 80, Curv GTX, Q7.8, Peregrine 72, Atlas 80, MX84, e-Magnum, Dancer 79 Ti, Zephyr 88, Q7, Warbird, Forza 70 Ti.

### Frontside Skis [L4860-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Crested Butte, Palisades Tahoe); Jonathan Ellsworth (Deer Valley). Class overview: "frontside" is nebulous; THIS YEAR Blister revised the section and created a whole new "On-Piste Carving Skis" section. Frontside now = skis that (1) carve/stand out on piste more than All-Mountain skis, and (2) are more compliant/practical off piste than On-Piste Carving skis. Not piste-specific, but best for folks mostly on groomers; generally less maneuverable/forgiving/stable off piste than All-Mountain skis, especially in soft/deep/variable snow. Some carve ice, some are blasts in moguls.
- Ad (one-line): Carv 2 digital ski coach.

- Faction Le Mogul Ski Pro -- 100-66-89.5 (171); 17 m; 1371 & 1403 g; 51/8.5/16; 169.8; mounts Newschool -5.7/79.2, Progressive -7.2/77.7, Classic -8.7/76.2; 171, 179; poplar + fiberglass. Not a frontside ski -- dedicated competition mogul ski, included so people try a purpose-built mogul ski. Dramatically more agile than all-mountain bump favorites, near-zero swing weight; rewards proper bump technique; not intuitive at first but a great learning tool; argues mogul skis deserve a quiver spot.
- Folsom Spar 78 -- 122-77-106 (177); 17 m; 1675 & 1732 g; 51/6/11.5; 176.3; -9.1 / 79; 158-184; poplar/maple/bamboo + 90/10 fiberglass/carbon (custom). Piste-priority sibling of favorite Spar 88: more eager carve initiation, more precise and lively; off piste not loose, needs technique, but very low swing weight for dynamic bumps/trees. Energy/agility over suspension (custom damper builds possible). Less firm focus: Spar 88.
- Black Crows Octo -- 130-83-115.5 (179.3); 14 m; 1724 & 1749 g; 38.5/10.5/8; 178.1; -7.5 / 81.6; 167.2-185.4; poplar + fiberglass. Excels at tight turns on piste but not confined; more conventional Mirus Cor (no centered mount) -- more stable at speed, intuitive for directional skiers; not smooth/planted or stable in big fast turns; holds edge securely; some off-piste duty. For tight-radius fans who find others too heavy/stiff/one-dimensional.
- Volkl Mantra 84 -- 129-84-109.5 (177); radii 29/16/26 m; 1869 & 1886 g; 45/3.5/11.5; 177.9; -12.1 / 76.9; 163-184; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Excellent carver with all-mountain DNA; most agile/piste-oriented and least damp of the Mantra trio; versatile turn shapes; for advanced/expert; enjoyable off piste except punchy snow/odd tight bumps. Nearly matches Peregrine 82 precision with better off-piste capability.
- Majesty HNX Ti -- 124.5-85-119.5 (178); radii 15.5/17/18/16 m; 1821 & 1827 g; 39/9/9; 177.4; -7.7 / 81; 166, 172, 178; poplar/ash + 1 titanal + triax & biax fiberglass. Precise, fairly powerful, medium-radius turns in most conditions; odd tail but not a tight-turn specialist; agility/rebound over suspension; icy or slushy groomers fine; not loose off piste. Like a lighter, livelier recreational GS ski with added maneuverability.
- Black Crows Mirus Cor -- 133.2-86.7-122.8 (178); 13 m (all lengths); 1796 & 1838 g; 50/2/40; 176.5; -3.9 / 84.4; 160.1-184.2; poplar + partial titanal plate + fiberglass. Baffling mix (87 waist, soft flex, 13 m radius, centered mount, metal underfoot, twin rocker, split tail) = one-of-a-kind ride some reviewers love after adjusting; loves very tight carves, nearly effortless initiation; easy to slash, airs, skis switch. Centered stance takes adjusting; can be hooky in variable. Niche experience, succeeds.
- Folsom Spar 88 (directional rocker build) -- 131.9-87.3-115.8 (182); 17 m; 2008 & 2015 g; 63/2/13; 181.2; -10.6 / 80; 172-186 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of favorite <90 mm skis for full frontside gamut, firm groomers to big moguls; not railroad-track ice carver but handles variable and technical off-piste better than most thanks to smooth suspension and releasable tail; comparable stability to Anomaly 88/Mantra 88 with easier skidding. Lighter/stiffer custom tested is more like Spar 78.
- Blizzard Anomaly 88 -- 127.5-87.5-109 (182); 18 m; 2047 & 2056 g; 73/7/16; 180.4; -10.4 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + titanal (full-width & partial) + fiberglass. Resists chatter on hardpack; heavy construction & not-super-stiff shovels = excellent suspension; minimal taper/shallow rocker = precise initiation and edge hold; capable off piste if forward. Spar 88/Quickdraw 88 easier. Less lively/agile than Mantra 88 but matches/beats edge hold & suspension.
- Dynastar M-Cross 88 -- 134.5-87.5-116.5 (184); 15 m; 1906 & 1907 g; 48.5/10.5/15.5; 183.4; -12.7 / 79; 159-184; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Snappy, dynamic on groomers; easy initiation despite tapered rockered tips; strong firm edge hold, easy to modulate carve/skid; rebound notable (air between carves). Less maneuverable off piste than M-Pro/M-Free; enjoyable in bumps if forward. Vs Octo: Octo easier slow tight carves; M-Cross damper, more predictable in variable.
- Head Kore 88 Ti -- 128-87.5-111.5 (177); 16 m; 1783 & 1801 g; 52.5/11/22; 176.2; -10.2 / 78; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Blurs All-Mountain/Frontside line; here stands out for easygoing maneuverability; fun to carve, versatile middle flex; less secure on ice/stable in crud than Mantra 88/Anomaly 88 but much easier to pivot. Lighter Spar 88 or more engaging Stance Pro 90.
- Kastle MX88 -- 129-87.5-113.5 (181); 18.8 m; 1960 & 2004 g; 54.5/8/10; 179; -8.9 / 80.6; 160-188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Very stiff, minimal rocker, powerful/precise on piste but needs speed/input -- otherwise wants big cruisy low-angle turns; that makes it surprisingly capable/non-hooky off piste, but very directional (no backseat). For advanced/expert: predictable off piste, powerful big-turn carver. More accessible: new Kastle Sublime 93.
- Volkl Mantra 88 -- 132.5-87.5-113.5 (184); radii 31/17/27 m; 1848 & 1986 g; 43/4.5/10; 183; -12.1 / 79.4; 163-184; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Appealing precision, stability, adaptability for experienced skiers; like a longer-turning, more maneuverable Peregrine 82 without losing much grip; off piste like M7 Mantra (not loose, good for directional technique). Vs Anomaly 88: Anomaly slightly smoother, Mantra livelier and quicker. Standout on groomers, more predictable off piste than equals.
- Icelantic Torrent 88 (new) -- 131.5-88-115 (182); 17 m; 1809 & 1815 g; 53/10.5/20.5; 181; -7.7 / 82.8; 166-188; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Far more precision/energy than previous Icelantics; drive hard for high-angle medium turns firm or variable; doesn't pull you in but rebounds strongly; low swing weight off piste, keep weight forward. Not damp but stable for weight. More agile/lively Mantra 88/Anomaly 88. Wider Torrents in More Stable all-mountain.
- Meier Quickdraw 88 -- 137.5-88-113.5 (181); 19 m; 2025 & 2028 g; 55/11/25; 181; -11 / 79.8; 156-186; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. More adaptable than shape suggests; subdued initiation, rarely hooky in big turns or casual skids, less engaging on low angles; easy to hop/pivot in bumps if not backseat; impressive suspension in rough snow; not for icy carving (Anomaly 88). Smooth and predictable for less experienced; composure for aggressive.
- Faction Dancer 89 (no metal; new) -- 125.5-88.5-114.5 (179); 18 m; 1713 & 1746 g; 59.5/9/24; 177.6; -8.8 / 80; 153-185; poplar + fiberglass. Notably agile; strong tail (no backseat) but low swing weight and surfy tips -- nimble off piste; high-angle medium carves on soft-ish groomers, not best on very firm; predictable casual sliding -- good for progressing skiers. More precision/support: Dancer 89 Ti.
- Faction Dancer 89 Ti (new) -- 125.5-88.5-114.5 (179); 18 m; 1817 & 1833 g; 59/11/24; 177.6; -8.9 / 79.9; 153-185; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Same as Dancer 89 plus two metal layers and stiffer shovel: more firm grip & high-speed composure, less low-speed accessibility. Less precise at slow speed than Mantra 88 but more agile off piste; low swing weight > suspension. Dancer 79 Ti for groomers only; much more firm capability than Dancer 99 Ti.
- Heritage Lab AM90 -- 124.5-88.5-115 (180); 16.5 m; 1982 & 2013 g; 56/4.5/27.5; 179.5; -7.5 / 82.3; 180 cm only; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Hard to categorize: super fun tight carves, surfier and more forgiving off piste than most here; one of dampest; short radius, progressive mount, moderate flex = playful for directional ski. Initiate from ankles; once on edge slices very firm snow (took time to trust). Not for big powerful turns on rock-hard groomers; AM100 more stable/surfier. Reviewers reached for it often in no-new-snow periods.
- Romp Sidehit 89 -- 133-88.5-123 (178); 14 m; 1829 & 1845 g; 56/6/29; 176.6; -7 / 81.3; 178 cm (custom); ash/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Freestyle playfulness plus surprising on-piste precision; tight radius fun at moderate speeds, less specialist than softer Mirus Cor; progressive mount & semi-twin -- good in air, fun carving switch; impressive edge hold on very firm, lots of rebound; slightly looser than average here. Stiffer, more directional Mirus Cor; more precise than AM90.
- Salomon Stance Pro 90 -- 128.3-90.2-109.6 (182); 19 m; 1854 & 1863 g; 56/8/9; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/basalt inserts + fiberglass. All-mountain-inspired; less eager to pull into carves, but impressive maneuverability and predictability in bumps/variable; secure on edge with speed/force. Similar on/off balance to MX88 but quicker and more accessible (MX88 more top-end). Commendable suspension for low swing weight; more planted: Kastle Sublime 93.
- Shaggy's Brockway 90 -- 136-90.3-116.1 (180); 17.5-19.25 m; 1833 & 1849 g; 60/6.5/15; 179.5; -8.8 / 81; 162-186; ash/poplar + nanoMAG magnesium stringers + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Supersized carving ski; "camber pockets" + minimal taper = excellent edge hold in medium-long turns; nanoMAG improves suspension; not quick edge to edge; shines when groomers get soft/variable (where narrow carvers get hooky). Powerful in open off-piste; harder in bumps. "All-conditions carving."
- Wagner Summit 91 -- 134-90.5-115 (182); 17.7 m; 2072 & 2094 g; 76/1/26; 179.8; -10 / 80; 168, 175, 182 (custom); aspen/beech + titanal + fiberglass (custom). Damp, holds edge on firm without being wildly stiff; nearly as maneuverable as Stance Pro 90 but much better initiation/high-speed composure; more precise on piste than Summit 97; less punishing than MX88/Anomaly 88. More rebound: 4FRNT MSP 91. Well-rounded firm-conditions setup.
- 4FRNT MSP 91 -- 129-91-116 (181); 16.5 m; 2047 & 2082 g; 55/6/15; 180; -8.5 / 81.5; 165-187; aspen + 2 titanal + neoprene tip insert + fiberglass. 4FRNT's most piste-oriented; calm in rough snow; "damp doesn't mean dead" -- much more energetic than equally stable skis; quick across fall line, strong edge hold. Off piste for good technique. Easier in bumps than Anomaly 88, less planted in big turns but similar edge hold and much more rebound.
- Northland AM178 -- 138.5-91-123 (178); 15 m; 1808 & 1834 g; 57/10/13; 177.7; -8.5 / 80.5; 158, 168, 178; hickory/ash/beetle-kill pine + kevlar, carbon & fiberglass. Feels like a much narrower carver; nearly fully cambered, strong flex; carves deep trenches in variable and clean arcs on firm; doesn't initiate for you -- learning curve, rewards technique with lively direct connection. Handful in bumps/trees (flat strong tail); wider platform resists bogging. For those finding modern frontside skis dead/imprecise.
- Kastle Paragon 93 -- 126.5-91.5-113.5 (177); 16.4 m; 1820 & 1867 g; 59.5/8/17.5; 174.8; -11.3 / 76; 163-184; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Kastle precision in a lighter, more agile, more rockered/tapered chassis than MX88/Sublime 93; secure on edge in firm shallow snow; not damp/stable in crud; low swing weight, looser in soft snow; stiff tail not forgiving. More suspension: Sublime 93.
- Kastle Sublime 93 (new) -- 131.5-92-115.5 (182); 18.2 m; 2008 & 2015 g; 60/8.5/13; 180.9; -10.6 / 79.9; 164-188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Very well-rounded: MX88's suspension/high-speed capability with some Paragon 93 maneuverability/accessibility. Predictable, adaptable carver (not very energetic) with enough grip for very firm; composed in cut-up groomers; heavier swing weight than Paragon but preferred off piste by many (less deflection, less punishing). Like a slightly more maneuverable Anomaly 88.
- 1000 Skis Carve -- 131-92.5-122 (175); 15 m; 1842 & 1865 g; 62/8/22; 175; recommended -4 / 83.5; 161-181; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Softer flex and tighter radius -- easy to bend; centered mount -> responds to neutral stance (adjustment). Once dialed, tons of on-piste fun, impressive edge hold and energetic rebound; tight carves are its happy place; easy to break into slashes/drifts. Ideal for mixing airs, butters with high-edge-angle turns.
- Rossignol Arcade 94 -- 137-93.5-126.5 (178); 16 m; 1988 & 2067 g; 61/9.5/11; 176.8; -6.7 / 81.7; 154-186; poplar + ABS stringer + 1 partial titanal + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. All-mountain width, carver shape; carves excellently, medium-radius in any piste condition; less quick than Arcade 88; more maneuverable off piste than narrower piste skis in soft snow but fat stiff flat tail resists pivoting from centered/backseat. Beats Stance Pro 90/AM90 on edge hold/stability. Tighter/forgiving: Folsom Turbo 94, Line Blade.
- DPS Pisteworks 94 (designed with Ted Ligety) -- 131-94-111.5 (178); 19 m; 1658 & 1665 g; 51.5/8.5/27.5; 175.8; -9.4 / 78.5; 165-189; poplar/red-oak + carbon fiber plate underfoot + carbon laminate. Unique; extremely nimble; very impressive firm edge hold for weight and commendable suspension in shallow crud; capable off piste in soft snow. Not smooth in variable. Precision of heavier skis in agile, energetic, accessible ride; soft shovels approachable; possibly the easiest wider ski here to carve very firm snow. For light-ski fans disappointed on groomers.
- Folsom Turbo 94 -- stated 148-94-120 (182); 15 m; 2112 & 2116 g; 51/7/15; mount -7 / 81; 152-186 (custom); maple/poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Tight-turning fun carver like Blade/1000 Carve but stiffer, more directional, more off-piste capable; those (and Mirus Cor) have slightly better edge hold and slow initiation. Spar 88 more versatile 1-ski quiver; Spar 78 better ice grip. Makes low-angle groomers interesting without needing to switch skis.
- Line Blade -- 154-95.3-124 (181); 13.5 m (176); 1999 & 2060 g; 49.5/6/27; 178.8; -7 / 82.4; 153-181; aspen + titanal sheet with cutouts + fiberglass. Early driver of "fun carver" trend, still a favorite; short-turn focused for intermediates & experts with rebound; tail surprisingly easy to release into drifts -> versatile off piste; enough mass not harsh. Others hold better on extremely firm; aggressive directional skiers: Turbo 94. Balanced/freestyle: 1000 Carve, Mirus Cor, Sidehit 89.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Frontside (top -> bottom):
- Most Piste-Oriented -> Least: Mantra 84, Brockway 90, HNX Ti, Spar 78, Northland AM178, Arcade 94, MSP 91, Anomaly 88, Mantra 88, Torrent 88, MX88, M-Cross 88, Pisteworks 94, Paragon 93, Octo, Sidehit 89, 1000 Carve, Sublime 93, Summit 91, Quickdraw 88, Mirus Cor, Turbo 94, Blade, Dancer 89 Ti, AM90, Spar 88, Dancer 89, Kore 88 Ti, Stance Pro 90, Le Mogul Ski Pro.
- Easiest in Moguls -> Hardest: Le Mogul Ski Pro, Kore 88 Ti, Dancer 89, AM90, Stance Pro 90, Blade, Spar 88, Turbo 94, Mirus Cor, 1000 Carve, Sidehit 89, Octo, Pisteworks 94, Summit 91, Quickdraw 88, Dancer 89 Ti, Torrent 88, Paragon 93, M-Cross 88, Mantra 88, Sublime 93, MSP 91, HNX Ti, Arcade 94, Spar 78, Mantra 84, Brockway 90, AM178, Anomaly 88, MX88.
- Most Stable -> Least: Anomaly 88, Sublime 93, MX88, Mantra 88, Summit 91, Spar 88, MSP 91, Quickdraw 88, Arcade 94, Turbo 94, Stance Pro 90, Mantra 84, Dancer 89 Ti, Brockway 90, HNX Ti, Blade, AM90, Torrent 88, Sidehit 89, Kore 88 Ti, Dancer 89, M-Cross 88, Spar 78, AM178, Paragon 93, Pisteworks 94, 1000 Carve, Octo, Mirus Cor, Le Mogul Ski Pro.

### All-Mountain Skis -- More Stable [L5707-]
Reviewers pictured: Jonathan Ellsworth (Palisades Tahoe), Luke Koppa (Crested Butte). Class overview: stiffer, less tolerant of backseat than More Forgiving, but more stable/damp at speed in tracked/variable/rough groomers. Historically heavier, but not true for the past few years -- Blister "not exactly thrilled" since mass still correlates strongly with stability, though brands have gotten better at strong, directional, lighter skis that aren't harsh. Good edge hold, powerful finish; good bump skis for strong skiers; punishing for less experienced; less appealing for loose/playful or easy slow initiation. Some could fit More Forgiving.
- Ad (one-line): Taos Ski Valley / The Blake hotel.

- Renoun Endurance 88 -- 126.5-86.5-110.5 (184); 17 m; 1939 & 1959 g; 61/7.5/24; 180.4; -10 / 80.1; 156-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + fiberglass. Off-piste-friendly shape + VibeStop (soft slow, stiff fast): maneuverable in bumps/trees, very impressive suspension when let run; tapered tips slower to initiate carves but good edge hold with force. Off-piste-biased, capable yet accessible. More agile: Folsom Cash 93, Black Crows Serpo.
- Folsom Spar 88 (182; heavier-than-Signature build; specs in Frontside) -- long a favorite; narrower ski at home in tight technical terrain; carves well, very easy to pivot, rarely hooky; more forgiving than Anomaly 94/Enforcer 94, nice suspension. Go-to for ~50/50 on/off piste.
- Folsom Cash 93 ("mid-light" build) -- 128-92-118 (185); 19 m; 1952 & 1964 g; 65/5/18; 183.5; -8.8 / 83; 170-190 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Leans into off-piste maneuverability/agility; easy to flick; tapered tips not hooky but less precise initiation than Spar 88; still carves firm groomers; mid-class suspension. Firm carving: Serpo, Enforcer 94.
- Heritage Lab RC95 ("Directional Thoroughbred") -- 125.5-92-113.5 (180); 24 m (feels ~20 m); 2144 & 2149 g; 63.5/5/19; 179.2; -10.5 / 79.1; 180, 187; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Far less niche than expected: very composed in crud, accessible flex (for class); less precise on piste than Enforcer 94/Anomaly 94 but better in big turns/rough snow and easier to release in bumps. Substantial swing weight. Excellent damp option for firmer/shallower conditions.
- Black Crows Serpo -- 131.1-92.3-114.9 (180); 20 m (all lengths); 1824 & 1835 g; 53/3/20; 179.1; -7.8 / 81.8; 152.3-186.3; poplar + 2 H-shaped titanal plates + fiberglass. Playful-looking rocker with stout lively build; floats/skids easily yet powerful exciting carver; rare maneuverability + edge hold combo; not plush but skiable hard. Middle ground between heavy More Stable and softer More Forgiving. Smoother: Stockli Stormrider 95.
- ON3P Woodsman 92 -- 127.5-92.5-115 (181); 17.4 m; 1804 & 1819 g; 58/6.5/29.5; 181.3; -7.9 / 82.8; 171-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. For skiers who find ~90 mm skis lacking off piste: maneuverable and predictable in variable; rarely chattery for light weight; composed in grabby snow. Not for railroad-track firm carving. More stable M-Pro 94 Ti or damper/surfier Serpo. Wider Woodsmans for more suspension.
- Blizzard Anomaly 94 -- 133-93.5-114 (182); 18.5 m; 2138 & 2163 g; 65/6.5/15.5; 180.7; -10.5 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + titanal (full & partial) + fiberglass. Traditional, precise, damp; beats most rivals on suspension/high-speed composure; carves like narrower Frontside skis in any piste condition. Not agile/forgiving in tight off-piste. For directional skiers with solid technique; versatile outside deep snow. Off-piste-leaning alternatives: Enforcer 94, RC95.
- Dynastar M-Pro 94 Ti -- 127-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762 & 1769 g; 70/8/21.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 154-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Freeride maneuverability in this section; best forward, but agile, even playful; more backseat-forgiving than most here; shines hopping/slashing technical terrain; needs commitment to hold on super firm. Softer, lighter, looser Serpo; for dynamic light-footed skiers.
- Head Kore 94 Ti -- 130.5-93.5-115.5 (177); 16.3 m; 1874 & 1877 g; 58/12/18.5; 176.3; -10.3 / 77.9; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Well-rounded versatility; unlike prior Kores not especially light/stiff; respectable suspension, agile, not demanding. Vs M-Pro 94 Ti: M-Pro more agile, Kore more stable and better edge hold. More off-piste stability: Stance Pro 96.
- Nordica Enforcer 94 -- 128.5-93.5-116 (179); 17.5 m; 2024 & 2035 g; 63.5/7/17; 177.8; -6.9 / 82; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer layer + fiberglass. For those unhappy with lighter, less damp trend: suspension, composure, precision; demands technique off piste but less than Anomaly 94; easy initiation, precise hold, rebound. Less planted than Anomaly 94 but more energetic and easier to flick.
- Stockli Stormrider 95 -- 131-94-119 (182); 18.5 m; 1821 & 1838 g; 47/6.5/10.5; 180.4; -10.2 / 80; 170-188; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal layers + "Freeride tip & tail" inserts + fiberglass. Surprise favorite, even for More Forgiving fans; fairly light for metal ski yet supple; not harsh/pingy on firm; knocked around by dense crud; nimble, lively off piste; edge hold not exceptional for class but impressive for its agility; big sweet spot. Off-piste agility with fewer compromises.
- K2 Mindbender 96 -- 135-95.5-122.5 (183); 20 m; 1969 & 1988 g; 54/5/24.5; 182.6; -10.6 / 80.7; 168-183; aspen/paulownia + 2 partial titanal + TPU/steel targeted mass weighting + fiberglass. Heavy inserts in targeted areas -> as damp/stable as heavier Mindbender 99Ti; keeps class-leading versatility: excellent edge hold, impressive off-piste maneuverability, accessible flex. Less stable than Mantra 102 but gap smaller than weights suggest. Near-equal on/off piste balance without excess weight. Surfier/progressive: Mindbender 101.
- Volkl M7 Mantra -- 138.5-95.5-120 (184); radii 26/41/18/23 m; 2111 & 2144 g; 43/3.5/10.5; 183; -12.2 / 79.3; 163-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Reference for directional, damp, precise all-mountain; carves exceptionally (matches narrower skis), energetic when pushed; pivoting in variable/big bumps not its forte (no worse than Anomaly 94). More off-piste: Mantra 102, Summit 97; more piste: Mantra 84/88. Rare support/composure/precision/energy combo at ~95 mm.
- Icelantic Torrent 96 (new; replaces Pioneers) -- 136.5-96-121 (182); 18 m; 1991 & 2008 g; 54/11.5/20.5; 181; -7.9 / 82.6; 166-188; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Big change for brand: energetic and precise everywhere; stiff, needs input, powerful precise turns with lively transitions; tails need deliberate unweighting in bumps (vs softer Nomads) but low swing weight keeps it not too demanding; slashier in soft. Less damp than Summit 97/M7 Mantra; enables aggressive dynamic skiing.
- Salomon Stance Pro 96 -- 131.9-96-113.9 (182); 20 m; 1946 & 1968 g; 56/9/12; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/flax inserts + fiberglass. "Lesson in moderation" -- no party trick, just works; moderately stiff/damp/light metal ski; less precise than M7/Torrent 96 but respectable carver at speed; easier to skid and more backseat-forgiving than those; nimble yet damper than agile off-piste skis. Lighter: Stormrider 95; heavier: Summit 97.
- Wagner Summit 97 (semi-custom) -- 134.2-96.3-118.8 (182); 19 m; 2165 & 2186 g; 79/3.5/30; 179.6; -9.9 / 79.9; 161-182 (custom); maple/ash + titanal + fiberglass (custom). Refreshing damp/stable do-everything: suspension at least as good as M7 Mantra/Enforcer 94 but much more forgiving and maneuverable; deep low-slung rocker for pivoting without losing carving; heavier swing weight but releasable tails make it approachable. More maneuverable Anomaly 94 or much damper Stormrider 95.
- Fischer Ranger 96 -- 128.5-96.6-119.6 (180); 18 m; 1935 & 1962 g; 66/3.5/21; 178.7; -7.8 / 81.6; 159-187; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Off-piste maneuverability priority; loose/surfy shovels, easy flicking; strong tail rewards forward skiers; respectable edge hold underfoot but not locked-in (M7 Mantra, Mindbender 96, Enforcer 99 for that). Balances swing weight and suspension. More forgiving tail: Faction Dancer 99 Ti.
- Moment Countach 98 -- 135.5-97-119 (182); 17.5 m; 1899 & 1902 g; 53/5.5/20; 180.7; -8 / 82.4; 170-188; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Narrowest Countach: directional stability + freeride maneuverability in a firm-snow-focused shape; drive hard or ski centered, but not tail-friendly in tight bumps; looser for width, carves firm well; respectable damping, above-average energy among lighter skis. More precise Woodsman 100, more nimble Kore 100 Ti, less directional Ranger 96. More planted: Revolt 101, Enforcer 104.
- Shaggy's Mohawk 98 -- 135.5-98-119.2 (186); 20.5-22.5 m; 2079 & 2089 g; 70/3/22; 185.5; -8.5 / 84.5; 162-186; ash/aspen + nanoMAG magnesium strips + carbon & fiberglass. Two-part story: very soft surfy shovel, much stiffer precise tail; deep rocker -- easy to release; good pow for width; stout tail punishes backseat/centered in tight bumps. Light swing weight for mass, composure in dense crud suffers. Similar to Ranger 96 (more agile); Mohawk better firm edge hold & soft-snow maneuverability.
- Faction Dancer 99 Ti (183) -- 131.5-98.5-122.5 (183); 19 m; 2047 & 2061 g; 70/8/26.5; 181.4; -9.3 / 81.4; 164-188; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Faction overhauled most Dancers, offering metal and non-metal versions. Notably looser/easier to skid than Dancer 2 it replaces at cost of precision (Anomaly, Enforcer, Mantra for grip). Predictable, adaptable; neither super damp nor skittish; pushable but less punishing. More maneuverable Kore 100 Ti; better suspension/more forgiving tail than Ranger 96.
- Nordica Enforcer 99 -- 133.5-99-121.5 (185); 18.5 m; 2199 & 2229 g; 63.5/6/26.5; 183.6; -7.1 / 84.7; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Sets itself apart in rough snow at speed; few ~100 mm skis mute crud as well; more energetic/easier than All-Mountain Chargers; carves really well; not agile/surfy but fine for experts valuing suspension over swing weight. Stiffer than Summit 97, damper/more maneuverable than M7 Mantra. Off-piste focus: Enforcer 104.
- Armada AntiMatter 100 (new; replaces Declivity X 102) -- 130-99.5-120 (182); 18 m; 1922 & 1950 g; 57/7/32.5; 180.6; -6.8 / 83.5; 154-189; poplar + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Versatile directional ski, more balanced/agile/playful than on-paper rivals; quicker and more precise than predecessor, less damp. Mantra 102/Enforcer 99 much better in crud; AntiMatter far more agile and accessible, dynamic style. Less directional, more exciting Kore 100 Ti; lower swing weight & more forgiving than Revolt 101.
- Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti -- 133.5-99.5-123.5 (186); 18 m; 1967 & 1972 g; 69.5/8.5/23; 185; -8.9 / 83.6; 162-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Fills gap between precise skis and surfy M-Free; mid-pack suspension, quite maneuverable, not punishing, centered stance OK in open terrain; easy to bend; Enforcer 99/Mantra 102 hold better near ice. Slashes/hops off piste. More damping: Kore 100 Ti; better firm precision: Mindbender 96.
- Head Kore 100 Ti -- 132.5-99.5-121.5 (177); 17.2 m; 1951 & 1991 g; 58/8.5/23.5; 176.1; -10 / 78.1; 156-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Arguably most well-rounded Kore; damper/easier to skid than 94 Ti; more exciting on piste and forgiving than 106 Ti; big suspension upgrade vs older light Kores; quick and intuitive. Does most things well -- what many want in a daily driver. Livelier/playful: AntiMatter 100.
- K2 Mindbender 101 -- 130-100.5-121.5 (185); 20.5 m; 1885 & 1926 g; 49.5/3.5/31; 185.1; -6.8 / 85.8; 164-192; aspen/paulownia + 2 partial titanal + TPU/steel + fiberglass. Targeted mass on less directional freeride platform; carves firm very well for how loose it feels; impressive stability for weight; less eager into carves than 96 but more maneuverable/agile off piste; progressive mount, centered-friendly, stiffer than Reckoners. Vs Revolt 101/Countach 104: most precise on firm, surfier than Countach 104, less stable/punishing than Revolt.
- Kastle Paragon 101 -- 134-99.5-120.5 (182); 18 m; 2032 & 2037 g; 59/9/17.5; 180; -11.5 / 78.5; 169-190; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + Hollowtech tip insert + fiberglass. Off-piste agility AND on-piste precision; initiates/holds carves very well, comes alive bent hard; low swing weight; not surfy on firm, tail punishes backseat; drive hard in soft, light-footed in rough. Damper crud option: Sublime 93. Quick in tight terrain, precise on piste.
- Liberty Radian 100 -- 134.5-100-122.5 (179); 19 m; 1731 & 1750 g; 55.5/7/17.5; 179.1; -13.8 / 75.7; 167-185; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass & carbon. Very directional (mount), yet tapered/rockered -- unusually easy to smear in soft, even deep snow; locks in on firm, energetic. Light, agile > damp; easy in bumps unless steering from stiff tails. Punches above width in fresh. Nicer suspension: Radian 106, Paragon 101.
- Heritage Lab AM100 -- 130-100.5-121 (187); 21.5 m; 2286 & 2295 g; 58.5/6/30; 186.6; -8.5 / 84.7; 180, 187; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Rides like a wider big-mountain ski: long-feeling radius, substantial build, progressive mount, deep rocker; very loose/surfy off piste, holds edge when driven, smooth suspension and composure. Mantra 102/Anomaly 102 carve better at slow speed; AM100 much more maneuverable/forgiving, centered-friendly, huge sweet spot. Among dampest AND most maneuverable/playful here.
- Volkl Revolt 101 (182) -- 132.5-100.5-114.5 (182); radii 25/19/22 m; 2028 & 2111 g; 56.5/5/23.5; 182.2; -7 / 84.1; 168-189; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Directional Revolt in freeski line; strong yet deep rocker -- among the looser options here; for those stuck between More Stable and All-Mountain Freestyle; carves well, solid suspension; backseat in bumps tests forgiveness. Quicker/forgiving: Woodsman 100; more agility/energy: Countach 104.
- ON3P Woodsman 100 -- 133.5-101-122.5 (186); 19.5 m; 2067 & 2076 g; 69/4.5/38; 186.3; -8 / 85.2; 171-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. For aggressive skiers who like surfy maneuverability; less planted/precise on firm than Mantra 102/Anomaly 102 but more agile, floats better, centered-friendly; resists getting knocked around in chop better than most equally maneuverable skis. Not for firm smooth carving. More nimble/forgiving Revolt 101 or damper/surfier Countach 104. More float: Woodsman 108.
- Blizzard Anomaly 102 (also in Chargers) -- 135.5-101.5-122.5 (188); 21 m; 2259 & 2278 g; 62/5/14; 186.6; -10 / 83.3; 176-192; poplar/beech + titanal (full & partial) + fiberglass. Dampest, most composed Anomaly; less engaging/agile at slow speeds; excellent suspension/stability esp. in crud/chop; excellent carver at speed; not as demanding in tight terrain as expected if forward. Mantra 102 better balance; livelier precision: Enforcer 99. For strong, technical skiers.
- Volkl Mantra 102 -- 141.3-101.8-124.1 (184); radii 30/19/27 m; 2166 & 2237 g; 60/2.5/21; 182.9; -11.3 / 80.1; 170-191; poplar/beech + titanal layers + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. All-time favorite for damp, stable, confidence at speed plus time in tight terrain; surprisingly accessible for heavy-ski fans; far more planted than easier skis; excellent carver for width; releases more easily than M7. Romp Oso 105 for mostly tight zones. Few skis as steady in open terrain yet easy in bumps/trees.
- Icelantic Torrent 102 (new) -- 141-102-124.5 (182); 19 m; 2025 & 2084 g; 53.5/11.5/21; 181.3; -7.7 / 83; 171-188; birch/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Stiffer, more precise, livelier than prior Icelantics; rewards driving shovels with impressive edge hold and energy; tails don't want leaning in bumps but more agile than Mantra 102/Anomaly 102; looser in soft. Nomads more broadly appealing; Torrent can be pushed much harder. For precision/support without heavy sluggishness.
- Stockli Stormrider 102 -- 134.5-102-124.5 (182); 19.8 m; 1998 & 2010 g; 64.5/5/13; 180; -11.7 / 78.3; 173, 182, 191; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal layers + "Freeride tip & tail" inserts + fiberglass. Signature ride, surprisingly surfy though mostly cambered; easy to release (if not on tails); smoother suspension than most similar weights. Vs 95: less engaging/agile, more composure at speed and in deep snow; more directional -- responds to driving the front. Less rearward mount: Revolt 101, Woodsman 100.
- Parlor Cardinal 102 (custom variant of Cardinal 93) -- 141.1-102.5-129.1 (182); 18.5 m; 1976 & 2028 g; 66/4/16; 180.8; -12.8 / 78; 154-196 (custom); maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass (custom). Precision/agility of narrower ski; big shovels initiate quickly, strong flat tails finish powerfully; easy to pivot if forward; impressive suspension for weight; handles variable/speed well. On-piste/shallow: standard Cardinal 93.
- Moment Countach 104 -- 137-103.5-124 (188); 19.5 m; 2084 & 2123 g; 56.5/7.5/28.5; 186.7; -8 / 85.3; 170-188; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. "Directionally playful": strong flex, progressive mount, deeper rocker; maneuverable/agile yet handles speed in consistent/soft snow; less damp/forgiving than Countach 110 but stiffer tail = better firm edge hold. Vs Revolt 101: Revolt slightly better suspension; Countach quicker/livelier. Planted: Enforcer 104, Oso 105.
- Atomic Maverick 105 CTI -- 134-104-123 (185); 19 m; 2014 & 2038 g; 57/2.5/20; 184.2; -9.8 / 82.3; 170-191; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. One of the only skis that genuinely carves groomers as well as it surfs pow; floats well, drifts like Bent series; far more engaging/secure on firm than equally loose skis. Light -- not plush in rough crud; stiff tail punishing in firm bumps. For directional skiers liking lighter, nimble skis.
- Nordica Enforcer 104 -- 136-104-126.5 (185); 19 m; 2273 & 2297 g; 60.5/6/27.5; 183.8; -7.4 / 84.6; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Enforcer 99 suspension/stability in more maneuverable, off-piste-focused design; one of heavier in class, mutes rough snow, still has pop; demands effort in tight terrain but more forgiving than Anomaly 102; reviewers mostly prefer 104 over 99. Vs Mantra 102: Mantra easier to skid, Enforcer more engaging on piste.
- Romp Oso 105 -- 140.5-104.5-130.5 (188); 20 m; 2215 & 2231 g; 57.5/6/40.5; 186.4; -7.2 / 86; 172, 180, 188; ash/aspen + carbon stringer + fiberglass. Maneuverability + stability: damp ride for tight terrain; suspension beats lighter skis; moderately tapered/rockered, easy to release and shed speed. Less precise on low-angle groomers than Enforcer 104/Mantra 102; more maneuverable in soft and centered-friendly (not backseat). Max agility: Romp Zorro (More Forgiving).
- Folsom Cash 106 (Signature-like build, 188) -- 138-105-124 (188); 21 m; 1989 & 2000 g; 73/4/32; 185.5; -8 / 84; 164-189 (custom); poplar/bamboo + fiberglass & carbon (custom). Agile, maneuverable, skiable aggressively in soft/consistent snow; rarely out of place except carving icy groomers; lighter, more forgiving Oso 105; heavier custom build comparable to Oso 105/Woodsman 108.
- Head Kore 106 Ti -- 134-105.5-126 (184); 18.5 m; 2134 & 2142 g; 62.5/9/30; 183; -9.9 / 81.6; 163-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Potential "Goldilocks" wider ski for directional skiers: quick for size, much smoother than lighter skis; carves respectably, easy to skid; more backseat-forgiving than Paragon 107/Maverick 105 CTI. Minimal compromises. Soft chop speed: Kore 112 Ti.
- Liberty Radian 106 -- 138-105.5-126.5 (185); 21 m; 1854 & 1904 g; 61.5/8/17; 183.9; -8.6 / 83.4; 173, 179, 185; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass & carbon. Impressive firm precision for how surfy/agile in soft; less rearward mount than Radian 100 -- more stance-versatile, more forgiving & stable; light, not planted. Lighter, more forgiving Maverick 105 CTI/Paragon 107. Better suspension: Countach 110, Woodsman 108.
- Wagner Summit 107 (semi-custom) -- 142.7-106.1-128 (186); 19.6 m; 2258 & 2303 g; 75/4/35; 183.8; -10.5 / 81.4; 165-186 (custom); aspen + titanal + fiberglass (custom). Super smooth suspension without punishing flex; phenomenal in rough snow thanks to heavy build + forgiving flex = big sweet spot; carves well yet easy to release. Rivals AM110 in damping/maneuverability (AM110 more centered-friendly; Summit 107 forward). Agile alternative: Summit 106. Glued-to-snow feel, accessible.
- Armada AntiMatter 108 (replaces Declivity X 108; subtle update) -- 129-107.5-120.5 (184); 24 m; 2161 & 2167 g; 55.5/5/30; 182.8; mounts Modern -5.7/85.7, Factory -8.7/82.7; 176, 184, 191; poplar + partial titanal + elastic compound + fiberglass. Revised metal layers -> carves firm better without losing loose surfy ride; best in soft (slush to deep pow), floats/turns well; more versatile now; enough mass for aggressive skiing except dense crud. Compelling pow ski where deep days are rare.
- Kastle Paragon 107 -- 139.5-107.5-128.5 (184); 19.2 m; 2066 & 2083 g; 62/8/18; 182.5; -11.3 / 80; 168-192; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Keeps both edge hold and soft-snow maneuverability; precise on firm though initiation not snappy on low angles; loosens in soft, low swing weight. Heavier skis better in dense chop. Slightly heavier Radian 106; forward stance, stay off stiff tails. Damper: Summit 107.
- Black Crows Corvus (modern version) -- 136.5-109-119.5 (186.2); 25 m; 2008 & 2049 g; 53/3.5/34.5; 184.7; -6.1 / 86.2; 169.4-191.4; poplar/beech + partial titanal + fiberglass. Very different from old Corvus chargers; progressive mount, tapered soft tips/tails, symmetric rocker -- one of most agile/playful here; stiff cambered mid & long radius allow big steep turns. Not exciting carver, not damp in crud. For centered-stance skiers wanting quickness but not soft freestyle flex.
- ON3P Woodsman 108 -- 139.5-109-129 (186); 21.0 m; 2194 & 2200 g; 67.5/4.5/38.5; 186.3; -8.1 / 85.1; 171-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Not easily unsettled; strong flex, substantial build -> composure at speed though not exceptionally heavy; deep rocker easy to release; progressive mount centered-friendly. Less precise/planted on firm than Summit 107/Enforcer 104, quicker and livelier -- like stronger, more stable Countach 110. Excellent flotation; good pow-day option.
- Heritage Lab AM110 (also in More Forgiving) -- 134.5-110-128.5 (186); 24.7 m; 2341 & 2348 g; 73/5/56.5; 185.6; -6.8 / 86.1; 180, 186, 192; poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Excellent suspension with looser, less directional design; big fast turns in soft chop yet one of easier in tight terrain; carves fairly long turns; shines adapting to mixed off-piste. Vs M-Free 112: M-Free calmer in dense crud; AM110 more precise on piste, more forward-friendly, more energetic. Not that stiff -- heavy-ski benefits without planky flex.
- Moment Countach 110 -- 139.6-110-130 (188); 21 m; 2078 & 2131 g; 63/5/31; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.5; 176-194; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Born from blending playful Wildcat with directional Commander; intuitive and adaptable; lighter/quicker than many here yet more supportive/damp than More Forgiving/Freestyle; shines in soft/mixed snow; holds edge fine, floats well. More maneuverable/forgiving/composed than narrower Countachs. More: Wildcat 118.
- K2 Mindbender 111 (187; specs in 50/50) -- natural extension of Mindbender 101; targeted mass keeps deep-rockered tips planted; carves well on cambered middle half; surfy in tight/soft, forward or centered. Lighter, more precise alternative to Countach 110/Woodsman 108; less damp than AM110/M-Free 112 but far more agile.
- Dynastar M-Free 112 (190) -- 140.5-111-132.5 (190); 22 m; 2404 & 2405 g; 61/6.5/46; 189.6; -3.4 / 91.4; 176, 183, 190; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Cemented as a favorite despite paper specs (wide, rockered, very centered mount); also in All-Mountain Freestyle; directional reviewers love it as soft-snow all-mountain ski; can drive shovels in shallow snow; surfier than most; lighter swing weight than weight suggests. AM110 better for firm groomers / more rebound. Excellent suspension and high-speed stability...
  ...delivered through a loose, playful, fairly forgiving platform.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- All-Mountain More Stable (top -> bottom):
- Best on Ice -> Worst: M7 Mantra, Anomaly 94, Enforcer 94, Serpo, Anomaly 102, Mindbender 96, Enforcer 99, Mantra 102, Spar 88, Paragon 101, Radian 100, Enforcer 104, Summit 97, Cardinal 102, Torrent 96, Torrent 102, Kore 94 Ti, Stormrider 95, Endurance 88, RC95, Maverick 105 CTI, Countach 98, AntiMatter 100, Mindbender 101, Kore 100 Ti, Radian 106, Stance Pro 96, Cash 93, M-Pro 94 Ti, Woodsman 92, AM100, Kore 106 Ti, M-Pro 100 Ti, Paragon 107, Countach 104, Stormrider 102, Ranger 96, Mohawk 98, Summit 107, Dancer 99 Ti, Revolt 101, Oso 105, Woodsman 100, Cash 106, Mindbender 111, AM110, Corvus, Countach 110, M-Free 112, Woodsman 108, AntiMatter 108.
- Best Suspension/Damping -> Worst: M-Free 112, Summit 107, AM110, Anomaly 102, Enforcer 104, AM100, Mantra 102, Enforcer 99, RC95, Summit 97, Woodsman 108, Anomaly 94, Enforcer 94, Oso 105, Countach 110, Kore 106 Ti, Mindbender 96, M7 Mantra, Endurance 88, Stormrider 102, Woodsman 100, AntiMatter 108, Revolt 101, Dancer 99 Ti, Kore 100 Ti, Torrent 102, Mindbender 101, Spar 88, Stance Pro 96, Torrent 96, Cardinal 102, Mindbender 111, AntiMatter 100, M-Pro 100 Ti, Cash 106, Countach 104, Woodsman 92, Countach 98, Maverick 105 CTI, Paragon 107, Stormrider 95, Corvus, Kore 94 Ti, Ranger 96, M-Pro 94 Ti, Cash 93, Serpo, Paragon 101, Mohawk 98, Radian 106, Radian 100.
- Best in Powder -> Worst: AntiMatter 108, AM110, M-Free 112, Mindbender 111, Countach 110, Woodsman 108, Summit 107, Corvus, Maverick 105 CTI, Paragon 107, Radian 106, Cash 106, Countach 104, Oso 105, Kore 106 Ti, Enforcer 104, Revolt 101, Mindbender 101, Mohawk 98, Woodsman 100, AM100, Stormrider 102, Paragon 101, Radian 100, Mantra 102, Countach 98, Mindbender 96, M-Pro 100 Ti, AntiMatter 100, Torrent 102, Dancer 99 Ti, Kore 100 Ti, Cardinal 102, Summit 97, Ranger 96, Enforcer 99, Anomaly 102, M-Pro 94 Ti, Stormrider 95, Torrent 96, Stance Pro 96, M7 Mantra, Woodsman 92, RC95, Serpo, Cash 93, Kore 94 Ti, Enforcer 94, Endurance 88, Anomaly 94, Spar 88.

### All-Mountain Skis -- More Forgiving [L7058-]
Reviewer pictured: Luke Koppa (Palisades Tahoe; Snowbird). Class overview: very diverse; common traits (1) work at slower speeds, (2) don't immediately punish backseat, (3) need less input than Chargers/More Stable. Suit beginners/intermediates as everyday skis, and aggressive skiers in soft/consistent snow, and finesse-oriented experts.
- Ad (one-line): Hickory & Tweed ski shop, Armonk NY (lease program since 1961).

- Line Optic 88 -- 121-87.5-110.5 (184); 17.9 m; 1933 & 1977 g; 56.5/3.5/37.5; 183.4; -7.4 / 84.3; 156-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Blends agility, playfulness, composure at speed; more maneuverable than most at width yet suspension matching some More Stable skis; deep rocker, progressive mount. Great to progress on; not for icy groomers or backseat skiers.
- ZAG Mata Ti -- 141.5-90-118 (178); 14.5 m; 1980 & 1981 g; 43.5/9.5/19.5; 177.1; -9.4 / 79.2; 163-183; poplar + partial titanal + quadraxial fiberglass + rubber inserts. Unconventional shape, adaptable; short radius and soft shovels make tight carves easy; forgiving tails release in bumps/trees; not for high-edge angles on very firm; blast on softer snow. Vs Line Blade: Mata Ti quicker/more energetic; Blade holds edge better, calmer at speed.
- Meier Wrangler -- 135.5-93-118.3 (185); 17 m; 1947 & 1962 g; 57/3/18; 185; -10 / 83; 170-185; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Does a bit of everything well; damper, more stable/precise than most here but less demanding than More Stable; reliable grip, turn-shape versatility; not quickest in tight bumps. Off-piste playful alternatives: QST 94, Optic 96.
- Salomon QST 94 (180) -- favorite; enjoyable without demanding much but encourages aggressive dynamic skiing; impressive suspension for weight; easy to skid/hop in tight terrain; weak on scraped-off groomers. Vs Rustler 9: QST less precise but more maneuverable/forgiving. Impressively well-rounded.
- Shaggy's Ahmeek 95 -- 131.5-94.9-116.1 (180); 20-22 m; 1808 & 1823 g; 57/1.5/45; 179.3; -6.6 / 83; 162-186 (custom); ash/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Off-piste maneuverability + firm-snow precision + style adaptability; "camber pockets" give strong firm grip despite deep rocker; easy slashes from centered stance; less damp than Ahmeek 105 but better at speed than Ripstick 96; needs input for tight on-piste carves. More forgiving tail: Optic 96, Apostle 96 Ti.
- Line Blade (181) -- oddball in best sense; very tight carves; tail releases/feathers easily -> forgiving, playful; wide enough not to bog; big shovels slightly sluggish in tight bumps; nice suspension, lively. Reviewer favorite; long-term review online.
- Elan Ripstick 96 -- 133-95.5-113.5 (182); 17.8 m; 1735 & 1741 g; 48/5/17.5; 181.3; -8.4 / 82.2; 161-189; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip + fiberglass. Agility and energy; less damp/stable in rough snow; easy to flick/slash/carve at slow speeds; forgiving flex, stance-tolerant; carves well when driven. Less precise than prior-gen Ripsticks but better than Completo 100/QST 94. Great to progress on; experts with active style.
- RMU Apostle 96 Ti -- 134-95.5-119 (184); 18.5 m (all lengths); 1894 & 1919 g; 65/5/26; 182.5; mounts FR -5/86.5, TR -7/84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mount plate + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Playful freestyle chassis with directional construction, cohesive; deep rocker and low swing weight -- very easy to pivot/slash/flick; stance-adaptable; not quick to punish backseat. Ripstick 96/Rustler 9 grip better on very firm; impressive on piste for its maneuverability. Optic 96: better suspension, less firm precision.
- Line Optic 96 -- 129.1-95.6-118.7 (184); 18 m; 2077 & 2096 g; 59/3/42; 183.8; -7 / 84.9; 156-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Still stands out: few skis as surfy/playful while as damp; composed at speed, engaging at lower speed; plenty of rebound; soft tips/tails; loose off piste like Apostle 96 Ti. Not for aggressive firm carving. Most versatile Optic (88 stiffer/more precise; 104 most maneuverable/damp).
- Renoun Endurance 98 (184) -- adjusts on the fly via VibeStop: forgiving when easy, damper/supportive when pushed; versatile intuitive all-mountain ski for intermediates who want something that holds up at speed and advanced skiers wanting nimble support. Looser: QST 100, Apostle 96 Ti; more firm precision: Rustler 9.
- Blizzard Rustler 9 -- 131-97-121 (180); 17 m; 1880 & 1887 g; 78/3/41; 178; -8 / 81; 162-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Stiffer, more precise ride than most here; not as loose in grabby snow; initiates/holds/finishes carves with real precision; more maneuverable than More Stable skis. Surfier: Optic 96, QST 100, Nomad 100. Less piste-biased: Apostle 96 Ti or Blizzard Canvas 100.
- Faction Prodigy 2 (183) -- 126.5-97.5-118.5 (183); 20 m; 2007 & 2026 g; 63.5/6/49.5; 180.8; -6.5 / 83.9; 159-189; poplar + carbon/rubber reinforcement + fiberglass. Best-seller; very accessible, not stance-specific; not super light/soft so experienced skiers enjoy it; carves well despite deep rocker/taper. Prodigy 3 more stable in chop; Prodigy 2 lower swing weight, better on piste; capable park/freestyle setup. More agility: Completo 100, Zorro 100.
- Scott SEA 98 ("Ski Everywhere Anytime") -- 133-97.5-121.5 (180); 17 m; 1901 & 1905 g; 46.5/7.5/36; 178.7; -7.9 / 81.5; 156-188; poplar + partial titanal reinforcement + fiberglass. Leans into moderation (unlike Scott's prior radical designs); neither sketchy nor exhilarating on piste; pivots easily off piste, a touch more precise/supportive than Completo 100/Zorro 100; middle mount/flex intuitive forward or centered. Firm carving: Bent 100. A safe bet.
- Liberty Scope 99 -- 129-98-119.5 (186); 21 m; 1686 & 1820 g; 59/7.5/43.5; 184.2; -6 / 86.1; 164-186; poplar + phenolic binding plate + carbon & fiberglass. Lightweight agility/energy, versatile; carves surprisingly well given rocker and twin tail; stance-versatile; low weight hampers suspension (Prodigy 2, Optic 96 more planted). Playful without alienating directional skiers. More: Scope 104.
- Line Pandora 99 (unisex test, 184) -- 130.5-98.5-120 (184); 19.5 m; 1976 & 2000 g; 61.5/3/38; 183.5; -5 / 86.8; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass. Lighter, livelier take on Optic series; echoes Optic 96 but quicker, less damp, and a better firm carver; versatile middle ground. Looser, less directional Rustler 9 or more stable Ellis Sabr 100. More suspension: Optic 96, Nomad 100.
- Folsom Completo 100 (186) -- 129.3-99-119.6 (186); 18 m; 1851 & 1868 g; 60/4/42; 186.3; -6.8 / 85; 156, 174, 186 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Changing direction on the fly; very tapered, rockered, soft ends -> nearly effortless pivoting; less firm precision/high-speed composure but stable in soft-ish snow for weight; centered-friendly yet drivable. One of best for max off-piste agility. More composure: heavier custom, Cash, Primary.
- Romp Zorro 100 -- 131.5-99-127 (183); 18 m; 1891 & 1909 g; 61.5/6.5/42; 181.6; -7 / 83.8; 155-190 (custom); poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Best Zorro version yet; standout maneuverability/forgiveness/agility; soft ends help recover balance; carves as well or better than before -- rare surfy ski that encourages tight high-angle carves. Speed limit in rough snow. Holds edge better than Completo 100/QST 100, slightly less stable than QST 100. Customizable.
- Atomic Bent 100 (186) -- hard to pin down: freestyle design but directional feel; surfy in soft, precise and lively carving; light enough to skin, solid enough for firm resort days. For directional skiers wanting agility or playful skiers wanting backbone. QST 100/Zorro 100 slash easier; heavier skis smoother on firm bumps.
- Salomon QST 100 (180) -- most forgiving/playful QST but not at expense of versatility; moderate flex/weight broadens appeal; beginners find it easy, experts push it in steeps/bumps. Not for aggressive firm carving; stiffer directional: QST 94. Surfier: Salomon Depart .01 (Park); better carving precision: Zorro 100.
- Icelantic Nomad 100 -- stated 135-100-127 (188); 20 m; 1893 & 1925 g; 65/7/61; 185.1; mounts "PC" -4.9/87.7, "BC" -6.9/85.7; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Playful/maneuverable but not boxed into short turns; deep rocker, soft lively ends -- accessible, surfy; minimal taper and supportive middle = more stable than most equally easy skis. Others carve very firm snow better; stiffer: Bent 100, Rustler 10. Excellent float, minimal effort, more composure than most light skis.
- Ellis Sabr 100 (new-ish brand) -- 131-101-120.5 (186); 20.2 m; 1945 & 1946 g; 63/4.5/55; 183.8; -7.1 / 84.8; 174, 180, 186; poplar + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Well-rounded without being generic; progressive mount, near-symmetric rocker -> playful, viable all-mountain freestyle, but precise/powerful when driven. More maneuverable Bent 100 or more forgiving, less directional Rustler 10. Sweet-spot weight: agile, not harsh. Heavier skis smoother; Sabr beats them on energy/swing weight. Softer: Ellis Kanso 106.
- Rossignol Soul 102 -- 134-102-124.5 (180); 17 m; 2097 & 2098 g; 57/7.5/27.5; 180.1; -7.1 / 83; 156-188; poplar + partial titanal + ABS stringer + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. Like old Soul 7, accessible -- forward, centered, or backseat without drama -- but heavier, more traditional shape adds suspension/stability; easy to bend/slash, fun soft-snow carving; big shovels resist chop. Stands out for suspension in this section. For progressing and experienced skiers wanting loose, forgiving, damp.
- Fischer Ranger 102 (183) -- 137.5-102.2-127.8 (183); 19 m; 2057 & 2061 g; 70/4/34; 181.2; -10 / 80.6; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Blurs More Stable/Forgiving: soft surfy shovel, stiff precise tail; easy carve initiation, middling overall edge hold; low swing weight, loose up front; stiff tail punishes backseat. More forgiving: Soul 102, QST 100. More maneuverable: Ranger 108; less directional: Fischer Nightstick 97.
- Liberty Scope 104 -- 133-103-123 (188); 22.5 m; 1778 & 1792 g; 57/8/47.5; 186.4; -6.7 / 86.5; 164-188; poplar/ash + phenol binding plate + fiberglass. Among lighter skis, carves well without compromising off-piste maneuverability; very low swing weight, progressive mount, playful; holds edge better than equally maneuverable skis; not damp but handles speed for weight; stance-versatile. Lighter, better-carving Nomad 106; surfier, more forgiving Rustler 10.
- Blizzard Rustler 10 -- 136-103.5-126 (186); 18.5 m; 1956 & 1976 g; 78/3/41; 184; -7.8 / 84.2; 162-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Middle ground between Anomaly (precise) and Canvas (playful); most stances (not way backseat); more playful than More Stable skis; quick initiation, impressive edge hold; fun in soft snow. Suspension not especially smooth for weight; less backseat-forgiving. More agility: Canvas 108.
- J Skis Hotshot (overhauled) -- 136.5-103.6-122.5 (188); 19.3 m; 2076 & 2105 g; 70.5/4/30.5; 185.4; -6.5 / 86.2; 167-188; aspen + 2 partial titanal + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Much lighter than prior versions -- less damp/stable but much more nimble and better firm carving, retains surfy maneuverability. Softer, more agile Pandora 106 or more precise, directional RMU Apostle 105; alternative to Salomon QST 106 without stiff tail. Very well-rounded ~105 mm forgiving ski.
- Line Optic 104 (185) -- 131.6-103.7-123.2 (185); 20 m; 2230 & 2290 g; 59.5/3.5/44.5; 184.4; -7 / 85.2; 166-190; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Heavy + playful; mass smooths rough snow; skis switch, balanced in air, bends easily, loose and surfy; not dead -- energetic for damp ski; stiff metal mid gives solid edge hold; soft ends forgiving. Aggressive directional skiers may want more shovel support; more effort to flick than lighter skis.
- Black Crows Atris -- 137.2-103.9-121.8 (184); 20 m; 1946 & 1962 g; 59.5/3.5/41; 182.6; -7.9 / 83.4; 172-190; poplar + fiberglass. Playful (low swing weight, deep rocker, lively flex) with some directional precision/stability; mounted a couple cm forward it's a strong freestyle option; fun carver when driven. Tight piste turns: Line Sakana, Rustler 10; surfier: RMU Apostle 105. Good on/off balance. More big-mountain: Corvus...
  ...Atris works for a much broader range of skiers.
- RMU Apostle 105 (new; replaces Apostle 106) -- 136-104-124.5 (184); 18 m; 1971 & 1973 g; 58.5/5.5/29.5; 182.6; mounts FR -5/86.3, TR -7.1/84.2; 168-192; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Surfy, agile, playful for directional and freestyle skiers; tails release easily into drifts; carves well except icy. Not quite as forgiving as Nomad 106/Kanso 106 but more drivable; enough mass for aggressive skiing if light on your feet. Balanced freestyle: RMU Professor.
- Line Sakana (updated) -- 149-104.5-137 (181); 16.2 m; 2049 & 2064 g; 51/6.5/30.5; 180.5; -10.7 / 79.5; 166, 174, 181; paulownia/maple + carbon/flax stringers + fiberglass. Still eager to carve tight turns anywhere while loose/surfy; less directional now (more balanced flex, kicked-up tail for switch); heavier -> better suspension, durability. Not for big fast turns in variable (soft shovels, tight radius). Makes you see terrain anew. More composure: Line Poisson.
- Faction Prodigy 3 -- 132.5-105-124.5 (184); 20 m; 2077 & 2110 g; 66/8.5/51.5; 182.1; -7.1 / 84; 164-190; poplar + fiberglass. Cohesive freestyle/directional merge; centered-friendly but stiff-ish shovels drivable; loose in soft off piste yet holds edge surprisingly; agile for weight, better suspension than lower-swing-weight skis. Nomad 106 more backseat-forgiving; Optic 104 better suspension, softer. Few weak points; for those caught between directional and freestyle.
- Shaggy's Ahmeek 105 -- 140.3-105.2-128.7 (186); 20-22.5 m; 2202 & 2209 g; 54/3/40; 185.4; -7 / 85.7; 162-186; poplar/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of dampest/most stable here yet playful; firm/soft balance; camber pockets give above-average edge hold; long radius & stiff mid need speed; deep rocker maneuverable, heavier swing weight; tells you if backseat. Alternative to Optic 104 with better edge hold/stronger flex. Lighter: Unleashed 106 CA, Canvas 108.
- Line Pandora 106 (unisex, 183) -- 133.5-105.5-125 (183); 18.4 m; 2025 & 2090 g; 56/4/43; 182.3; -5.2 / 86; 155-189; aspen + fiberglass. Leans more playful than Optic 104: quicker, livelier, carves as well or better without metal; maneuverability/quickness > suspension but pushable. Optic 104 more planted.
- Nordica Unleashed 106 CA (new; replaces Unleashed 108) -- 139.5-105.5-127 (185); 18.7 m; 1880 & 1904 g; 61.5/8.5/39.5; 183.7; -8.7 / 83.2; 152-185; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. More energy/agility than 108; playful, freestyle-friendly yet intuitive for directional skiers; stands out for surprising precision -- carves shockingly well for a wide twin; respectable suspension for weight. Metal-laminate Unleashed 106 for more damping/precision.
- Icelantic Nomad 106 -- stated 141-106-133 (188); 20 m; 2022 & 2029 g; 69/5.5/60; 185.2; mounts "PC" -5/87.6, "BC" -7/85.6; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Forgiving maneuverability, freestyle flair, more stability than expected; very easy to pivot; composed at speed in soft; one of loosest/easiest here; carves soft groomers; above-average soft-chop composure. Nomad 100 better when firm.
- Salomon QST 106 -- 140-106-127 (189); 20.5 m; 2103 & 2255 g; 65/7/40; 187.6; -9.1 / 84.7; 157-189; poplar + basalt + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Big step up from QST 94/100 in width and skill level; impressive pow ski for width (floats/slashes like wider skis); holds up in soft chop; better-than-average suspension; predictable (not great) on firm; NOT very backseat-forgiving -- softer-tailed alternatives QST 100, Nomad 106, Kanso 106. Supportive tail; narrower QST Blank.
- Ellis Kanso 106 -- 136-106.5-126.5 (186); 19.5 m; 2026 & 2054 g; 62.5/4.5/52; 184.7; -6.9 / 85.4; 174, 180, 186; poplar + poured PU tip & tail inserts + fiberglass binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Super lively, playful, versatile; floats/turns better than Sabr 100, carves nicely, easier-bending ends, a bit damper; fun for airs. Vs Apostle 105 (surfiest) and Atris (most precise): Kanso in between and more forgiving.
- Blizzard Canvas 108 (186; specs in 50/50) -- significant departure for Blizzard: more playful, centered mount, twinned tail, light; skis surprisingly well for weight -- not dramatically less damp than Rustler 10, still drivable; slightly less precise but much more maneuverable/forgiving/balanced. Lighter, more maneuverable Unleashed 106 CA or more stable Scope 104. Works as all-mountain freestyle, 50/50, or daily driver.
- Wagner Summit 109 (custom) -- 141.7-107.9-129.1 (186); 20.4 m; 2171 & 2191 g; 70/5/33; 184.2; -7.3 / 84.8; 186 (custom); beech/aspen + carbon fiber "torsion box" + fiberglass (custom). For soft snow but not limited; minimal taper, shallow low rocker -> very good initiation/edge hold, looser when soft; skis lighter than weight; better suspension than Ranger 108 with similar swing weight. Surfier: Ranger 108, QST 106, AM110; Optic 104 smoother in crud. Hard to fault.
- Fischer Ranger 108 -- 142.7-108.4-133.7 (185); 18 m; 2110 & 2117 g; 68/2.5/33; 183.4; -9.6 / 82.1; 171-192; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Ranger 102 tweaked for flotation and off-piste maneuverability; handles soft chop; easy drifts; tails punish backseat in firm bumps but more forgiving/balanced than 102; excellent flotation; predictable not exciting on piste. Vs QST 106: QST more stable, Ranger nimbler/more forgiving. Fischer "Nothing" (112) similar, more balanced/precise.
- Line Poisson (new, "Concepts" collection) -- 143.5-109.5-131 (186); 18.5 m; 2234 & 2242 g; 57.5/5.5/26.5; 185.5; -4.8 / 88; 165-186; aspen + partial metal layers + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Blends Sakana (tight radius, unusual tail) with Optic (articulated metal); simultaneously eager to carve and super surfy -- pressure front to carve, centered to pivot; heavy, not soft -> impressive suspension/stability here; tight radius can be twitchy in big turns vs AM110. Adaptable, intuitive, fun slush to pow.
- Heritage Lab AM110 (also More Stable) -- originally modern take on cult classics (e.g., Rossignol Sickle), now its own ski; smooth suspension + off-piste maneuverability; very little camber, accessible flex; easy to release from various stances; swing weight lower than weight suggests; beats most here for crud stability; carves firm pretty well but not engaging at slow speed; one of most stable AND maneuverable wide skis.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- All-Mountain More Forgiving (top -> bottom):
- Most Stable in Crud -> Least: AM110, Optic 104, Ahmeek 105, Summit 109, Prodigy 3, Poisson, Nomad 106, Ranger 108, QST 106, Ranger 102, Hotshot, Apostle 105, Pandora 106, Wrangler, Soul 102, Kanso 106, QST 100, QST 94, Endurance 98, Unleashed 106 CA, Canvas 108, Prodigy 2, Pandora 99, SEA 98, Optic 96, Rustler 10, Optic 88, Nomad 100, Atris, Apostle 96 Ti, Sabr 100, Bent 100, Zorro 100, Completo 100, Scope 99, Scope 104, Ahmeek 95, Rustler 9, Blade, Mata Ti, Ripstick 96, Sakana.
- Most Accessible -> Most Demanding: Zorro 100, Completo 100, QST 100, Nomad 100, Ripstick 96, QST 94, Sakana, Optic 96, Soul 102, Kanso 106, Scope 104, Scope 99, Nomad 106, Pandora 106, Optic 104, Hotshot, Prodigy 2, Canvas 108, Apostle 105, Apostle 96 Ti, Blade, Sabr 100, Pandora 99, Atris, Unleashed 106 CA, Bent 100, SEA 98, Prodigy 3, Endurance 98, Rustler 9, Ahmeek 95, Optic 88, Mata Ti, Wrangler, Poisson, Summit 109, AM110, Rustler 10, Ranger 108, Ahmeek 105, Ranger 102, QST 106.
- Best in Deep Snow -> Worst: Ranger 108, QST 106, Summit 109, AM110, Nomad 106, Poisson, Hotshot, Scope 104, Apostle 105, Canvas 108, Unleashed 106 CA, Pandora 106, Optic 104, Kanso 106, Ranger 102, Atris, Prodigy 3, Completo 100, Rustler 10, Ahmeek 105, Nomad 100, Sakana, Scope 99, Apostle 96 Ti, QST 100, Bent 100, Zorro 100, Soul 102, Pandora 99, Sabr 100, Optic 96, Endurance 98, SEA 98, Prodigy 2, QST 94, Ripstick 96, Rustler 9, Optic 88, Blade, Mata Ti, Wrangler, Ahmeek 95.

### All-Mountain Chargers [L8182-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Snowbird), Jonathan Ellsworth (Palisades Tahoe). Class overview: diversified over the past decade though arguably fewer true chargers; now heavy & stiff, light & stiff, subtly/seriously rockered, very demanding, even forgiving. After years of lightening, some manufacturers acknowledging weight is valuable -- especially for chargers. Not for everyone but favorites of passionate skiers; for those prioritizing high-speed stability over low-speed maneuverability.

- Blizzard Anomaly 102 (188) -- blurs all-mountain/charger; more firm-snow precision than most here; very substantial, hard to unsettle; carves better than many here and not hard to get on edge (moderate radius, not-overly-stiff shovels); for strong technical skiers in tight terrain (not loose, not tail-forgiving). Versatile at any speed. More float/maneuverability: Mantra 108.
- Folsom Primary 104 ("Blister Pro" Edition; custom) -- 136.4-103.4-122.5 (186); 21 m; 2325 & 2352 g; 75/4/27; 184.7; -9.6 / 82.7; 163-189 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Very stiff and damp yet highly maneuverable in tight terrain; mutes rough snow; much easier to throw sideways than most ~104 directional skis; Anomaly 102/Mantra 108 much better carvers on very firm. Requires input to bend; rare high-speed stability + low-speed maneuverability. Softer version available.
- Wagner Summit 107 (186) -- arguably most accessible charger; excellent suspension, maneuverable rocker, not-super-stiff (probably softest here); huge sweet spot; carves very well, easy to release; others blow through crud better if you stay on top, but Summit 107 doesn't require high speed/aggression for its bump-erasing suspension. Stiffer flex and graphics available.
- Volkl Mantra 108 (formerly Katana 108) -- 145.4-107.7-128.6 (184); radii 39/19/34 m; 2353 & 2360 g; 57.5/2.5/21; 183.1; -11.5 / 80; 170-191; poplar/beech + "titanal frame" + carbon tips + fiberglass. Gives up some Mantra 102 maneuverability for max stability in variable; big shovels heavy/awkward in tight terrain but releases more than Anomaly 102; not too demanding for strong skiers; carves impressively, blasts chop/crud. Most people: Mantra 102.
- Prior Husume (standard build; custom) -- 135.7-109-123.5 (188); 25 m; 2312 & 2386 g; 78/2/22; 187.5; -10.7 / 83; 175-193 (custom); yellow-cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Specs suggest semi-truck, but shape/rocker make it notably surfy for a directional ski; very stiff, excellent suspension, less demanding than expected; outstanding flotation; shines in pow then chop. Mantra 108 more engaging on groomers; Husume nearly matches stability with better maneuverability. Progressive mount: Folsom Giver.
- Folsom Giver ("The Hammer" Edition) -- 138.3-109.2-129.3 (188); 25 m; 2371 & 2374 g; 63/0/42 (subtle reverse camber); 186.4; -7 / 86.1; 184-195 (custom); maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Very, very stout build of less-directional Giver shape; centered-friendly; easy to pivot despite hefty swing weight; happy place smashing through everything at speed. Floats slightly less than Husume, similar maneuverability/suspension; much stiffer, slightly more directional M-Free 112. "For those who view every patch of chop and crud as a nail."
- Dynastar M-Pro 108 Ti -- 138.5-109.8-128.7 (192); 22 m; 2448 & 2499 g; 68/6/26; 191.9; -13.3 / 82.7; 192 only; beech/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Throwback to iconic Dynastar comp freeride skis (like old Pro Rider); very heavy, exceptional suspension, for skilled powerful skiers; rearward mount, considerable swing weight; very hard to reach speed limit; one of smoothest rides on market. Not punishing at slow speed, just boring. Blast from the past for directional speed fans.
- Dynastar M-Free 112 (190) -- modern interpretation of big-mountain comp ski: mass/stiffness for sketchy runouts, plus balanced surfy platform for tricks; one of most maneuverable/forgiving chargers but not meaningfully less damp/stable. Not a great firm carver; directional skiers may dislike centered mount. Reviewer favorite with soft snow. Size up if between lengths.
- Volkl Katana V-Werks (184; specs in 50/50) -- outlier: true "lightweight charger"; shape like early Katanas with drastically less weight; quickest, most agile here; excellent big-mountain pow ski; big sweet spot, carves well; least stable/damp here by sizable margin, but pushable for weight -- great for charger feel via skin track.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- All-Mountain Chargers (top -> bottom; chart annotations in parentheses):
- Least Demanding -> Most Demanding: M-Free 112 (183), Katana V-Werks (light, but wants good technique), Summit 107, M-Free 112 (190), Husume (stiff & heavy, but surfy), Primary 104, Mantra 108, Giver Hammer, Anomaly 102, M-Pro 108 Ti (due to size & weight; not ultra-stiff).
- Best in Powder -> Worst: Husume, Katana V-Werks, M-Pro 108 Ti, M-Free 112 (190), Summit 107, Mantra 108, Giver, M-Free 112 (183), Primary 104, Anomaly 102.
- Best On-Piste -> Worst: Anomaly 102, Mantra 108, Summit 107, Katana V-Werks, M-Pro 108 Ti, Primary 104, Husume, Giver, M-Free 112 (183), M-Free 112 (190).

### All-Mountain Freestyle Skis [L8469-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Snowbird), Abel Sosa (Crested Butte). Class overview: more maneuverable than other All-Mountain sections; for skiers who spin, flip, ski switch, play -- but no need to throw tricks. Suit (a) experienced skiers wanting playful maneuverable ride, (b) feature hunters, (c) those taking a few park laps, (d) intermediates wanting forgiving, easy-turning skis. Some narrow enough for park, some wide for pow. Mostly centered upright stance, some drivable; symmetrical consistent flex helps balance.

- Moment Deathwish 96 -- 128-95.5-119.5 (184); 21 m; 1981 & 1983 g; 65.5/7/64.5; 181.6; -6 / 84.8; 164-190; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. "Triple Camber" in a firmer/shallower-snow silhouette; deep rocker surfy, camber pockets add serious grip; one of the most agile here; stance-versatile; less backseat-forgiving than next two but better support at speed/landings. Few match its edge hold while equally easy to slash.
- Fischer Nightstick 97 -- 132-96.5-123 (184); 18 m; 1944 & 1952 g; 56.5/8.5/51; 182.3; -3.4 / 87.7; 163-184; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Dependable do-everything freestyle ski for park and groomers; ride quality like Ranger series in symmetric shape; middle of precise-vs-surfy (between Unleashed 98 and M-Free 100); easy to throw sideways yet impressive edge hold underfoot; middle swing weight/stability. Directional skiers may prefer Unleashed 98/M-Free 100.
- J Skis Allplay -- 123.5-96.5-120.5 (186); 20.5 m; 2009 & 2015 g; 57/5.5/49.5; 185.5; -3.5 / 88.1; 158-186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Broad playfulness; intuitive for all reviewers; very accessible flex -- go-to for beginners/intermediates; experienced freestylers enjoy it at lower speeds and creative features; easy butters; not for support/stability. Softer conditions: J Skis Vacation.
- Nordica Unleashed 98 (186) -- 134.3-97.6-123.4 (186); 18.8 m; 1938 & 2003 g; 73/3.5/57.5; 183.6; -7.3 / 84.5; 156-186; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Very versatile across styles; conservative rocker/taper make it an excellent carver, far from surfiest; edge hold and energy on firm steeps. Recommended line = maneuverable directional all-mountain; mount ~-5 cm = strong lively freestyle setup. Studio 2 more balanced/surfy; Allplay/Reckoner 102 more engaging slow. Supportive freestyle or intro to playful skiing.
- Season Kin -- 139.5-97.6-135.5 (181); 15 m; 1824 & 1843 g; 61/6/62; 179.2; -2.6 / 87; 163, 175, 181; karuba + fiberglass. Fun-carver meets soft freestyle, cohesive; pliable flex and deep rocker easy to bend/slash; tight radius and strong edge hold = genuine blast on groomers with little effort; predictable off piste but not for big fast rough-snow turns. One of most engaging at lower speeds/small features. Calmer in variable: Reckoner 102.
