- Dynastar M-Free 100 -- 130-100.5-123 (185); 18 m; 1951 & 2003 g; 72/8.5/51; 181.3; -7.7 / 83; 162-192; poplar/PU + fiberglass. Keeps M-Free 108/112 traits for everyday conditions; looser side for ~100 mm; not impressive firm carver but better in pow/chop than most similar freestyle skis; damp/stable for width (much less than wider siblings). Directional-friendly; freestylers mount closer to center. Stiffer than average; lighter alternative to big-mountain models.
- RMU Professor 101 -- 127.5-100.5-120.5 (186); 20.5 m; 1945 & 1972 g; 61.5/9/37; 184; -4.5 / 87.5; 168, 178, 186; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Balanced, symmetric Apostle; well-rounded: carves non-icy groomers well, drifts easily, middle flex; quick but not sub-par stability. M-Free 100 more composed in heavy chop; Allplay more engaging slow. Safe bet.
- Rossignol Sender 100 (184) -- 130.5-100.5-122.5 (184); 20 m; 1984 & 1986 g; 63.5/8/46; 181.6; -3.2 / 87.6; 162-190; poplar + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. Narrower, lighter, more agile Sender 110; more at home in the park, nimble and lively; edge hold/energy > suspension/surfiness; strong preference for centered stance (directional: Unleashed 98). Grip, support, rebound when pushed.
- K2 Reckoner 102 -- 133.5-101-126 (184); 19.4 m; 2040 & 2061 g; 61/7.5/56; 184.1; mounts Team -2.1/89.9, Midsole -4.2/87.8; 163-191; aspen + carbon stringers & "spectral braid" + fiberglass. Class-leading playfulness + versatility; soft, easy butters, forgiving; very big sweet spot -- skiable hard; slightly above-average maneuverability; fun carving on firm. Stiffer: K2 Omen Team (Park). Soft and surfy yet holds up at speed.
- Faction Studio 2 -- 130.5-101.5-126.5 (183); 20 m; 1917 & 1934 g; 61.5/7/58.5; 180.7; mounts Newschool -3.7/86.7, Progressive -5.2/85.2, Classic -6.7/83.7; 166-188; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. For playful skiers who ski with speed/aggression; stiff, poppy, needs input; light and balanced in air; precision > surfiness but easier to slash than Unleashed 98/Sender 100; impressive carver (tighter than 20 m radius). Not for slow jibs (Reckoner 102). Impressive predictability at speed; directional-friendly at -6 cm.
- Volkl Revolt 104 -- 131.4-103.6-121.6 (188); radii 27/21/25 m; 1973 & 1997 g; 68.5/3.5/43; 186.7; -1.7 / 91.6; 172-188; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Looks surfy, rides like an oversized park ski; strong centered-stance preference; balanced and quick; less intuitive for directional skiers, below-average crud stability for weight; strong landing support in loose package. Easier: Vacation, Reckoner 102; stronger at speed: Studio 2. New Revolt 112.
- J Skis Vacation -- 126.3-103.7-122.3 (186); 20.1 m; 2010 & 2018 g; 70/3/53; 183.3; -3.7 / 87.9; 166-186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Easygoing, adaptable; trades max-speed stability for accessible playfulness; leans jibby; carves respectably; easy pivoting; manageable swing weight; intuitive directionally too. Hotshot and stiffer skis better in chop, but none as engaging/forgiving at moderate speeds. Allplay for firm days.
- Moment Deathwish 104 -- 132.7-103.8-124.1 (184); 22 m; 1970 & 1993 g; 67.5/4/66.5; 181.8; -5.8 / 85.1; 164-190; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Strong & precise yet balanced & surfy; deep rocker easy to slash; camber pockets give excellent grip when driven; poppy, low swing weight; strong but not especially damp; Triple Camber takes adjustment. Nimble ski you can drive through soft variable snow.
- Whitedot Altum 104 -- 134.3-104.4-124.4 (187); 22 m; 2049 & 2053 g; 67/3.5/55; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 171, 179, 187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Directional/freestyle merger, wide appeal; forward or centered, skis switch, stiff enough to push; minimal taper/low rocker = excellent carving for class; fairly easy pivoting if not backseat. Less forgiving than Vacation/Dirty Bear XL; high speed limit without heavy weight. Vs Deathwish 104: Deathwish quicker/looser in soft; Altum better suspension, more engaging on slow groomers.
- Fischer Nightstick 104 -- 140-104.5-129.5 (188); 20 m; 2090 & 2122 g; 56.5/7.5/54; 186.1; -3.4 / 89.7; 172, 180, 188; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Extension of Nightstick 97 with more stiffness/suspension for mixed conditions; stronger, more centered preference; composed in soft chop, still quicker than heaviest here; middle-of-pack carving/maneuverability. Directional: Deathwish 104, Altum 104. Lower swing weight: Revolt 104.
- FullSend Max K 106 (customizable) -- 138.5-104.5-129 (187); 18 m; 2077 & 2083 g; 62/0/50 (reverse-camber version measured); 185.2; -5.1 / 87.6; 187 cm; bamboo/poplar + fiberglass + carbon fiber stringer. Choose cambered or reverse-camber build, optional small swallowtail cutout; differences not drastic (camber bites more on firm; reverse camber surfier). Very loose, agile, lively, impressive stability for weight esp. in soft snow; fairly stiff, stance-agnostic; surfier than Wildcat 108/Altum 104. More off-piste: 4FRNT Devastator.
- Majesty Dirty Bear XL -- 139.4-104.6-129.5 (186); radii 18/20/21/19 m; 1960 & 1962 g; 59/1.5/51; 184.5; -3.9 / 88.3; 162-186; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Doesn't fit subcategories; minimal taper = impressive edge hold; easier to slash than most good carvers; supportive middle, ends deceptively easy to bend into butters/presses/ollies -- reviewer favorite for that; lively, easy to load off lips; low swing weight. Surfier: Devastator, Max K 106. Park-ski grip with better soft-snow ability.
- Season Nexus -- 135.4-105.2-131.9 (183); 17.5 m; 2073 & 2074 g; 53/5/48; 181.5; -2.6 / 88.1; 158-189; karuba + fiberglass. Brings variety: nearly symmetric ~106; blast to carve, surprisingly surfy in soft, damp/supportive for fairly high speeds. Like Line Bacon 108 (centered, fun on piste) but much stronger and more agile; minimal rocker/taper yet slashy in soft; above-average suspension. Centered stance; park backgrounds feel at home. More float: 1000 Skis All Mountain.
- 1000 Skis All Mountain -- 134.5-105.5-127 (186); 21 m; 2085 & 2086 g; 62.5/8.5/54; 185.6; mounts Team1 -2.5/90.3, Team2 -5/87.8; 161-186; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Their ideal daily driver; surprisingly traditional (little taper, mostly cambered); stands out for firm-snow precision over surfiness; detune edges and it smears easily; long effective edge -> respectable suspension/stability; ends still easy to bend. Balanced firm grip + soft maneuverability.
- Armada ARV 106 (188) -- 131.5-105.5-124 (188); 22 m; 2015 & 2170 g; 56/7.5/54.5; 186.2; mounts Freestyle -2.5/90.6, Factory -5/88.1; 164-188; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Very well-rounded modern all-mountain freestyle, built for park skiers hitting big features in technical terrain; intuitive across styles yet balanced, surfy, agile; safe bet. Middle ground between M-Free 108/Altum 104 and Dirty Bear XL/Bacon 108. Damper/more precise: ARV 106 Ti.
- Armada ARV 106 Ti -- 131.5-105.5-124.5 (188); 22 m; 2171 & 2228 g; 62/4.5/54; 185.8; mounts "FS" -2.4/90.5, "Standard" -4.9/88; 164-188; poplar + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Best Ti execution yet: tapered metal layers borrowed from AntiMatter -> better edge grip/suspension through the middle while keeping surfy accessible ends (older Ti versions felt "planky"). Not as damp as M-Free 108/Sender 110 but more agile and playful.
- 4FRNT Devastator -- 135.5-107.2-130.6 (186); 21 m; 1947 & 2011 g; 60/0/55 (reverse camber); 183.3; -4.7 / 86.9; 172, 179, 186; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Name misleading: one of surfiest, most maneuverable; reverse camber + low weight = extremely nimble; surprisingly predictable on smooth firm, fun to carve when not bulletproof; firm crud knocks it around but composed for weight. Not very soft. Heavier surfy option: M-Free.
- Dynastar M-Free 108 -- 137-107.6-127.4 (192); 20 m; 2322 & 2322 g; 64/9.5/50.5; 189.6; -8.3 / 86.5; 162-192; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. For variable snow at pace; big-mountain composure in freestyle chassis; strong flex, crud-erasing suspension; surfy but needs more effort to go sideways; directional-friendly mount (center-mounted folks push forward). Reviewers often prefer the M-Free 112.
- Line Bacon 108 -- 141.4-107.6-137 (184); 17.4 m; 2194 & 2208 g; 61/6/55; 184; -4 / 88; 166-190; maple/aspen + fiberglass. One of most playful mid-fats; very easy to bend, symmetric, engaging at slow speeds; fun on piste plus float/maneuverability; swing weight lower than weight suggests but not as agile as Bent 110; nice suspension for soft ski but not much high-speed composure in deep chop. Lighter: Bent 110; more support: Reckoner 110, Prior Northwest 110.
- Moment Wildcat 108 -- 134.0-107.7-126.9 (184); 24 m; stated 2100 g; 74/5/71; 181.8; -6.1 / 84.8; 174-190; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Freestyle/freeride blur; balanced, supportive, adaptable; drive shovels or ski centered; not unwieldy in air; nimble, surfy; strong flex encourages aggressive skiing except ice/refrozen crud. Not as planted as heavy skis or easy to bend slowly. More tip/tail support than Reckoner 110; lower swing weight than M-Free 108.
- Atomic Bent 110 (188; specs in 50/50) -- max playfulness in soft-ish snow; very light, loose, balanced; stiff middle + low swing weight great for tricks; one of less stable here in crud; amazing 50/50 or BC freestyle ski for centered skiers.
- K2 Reckoner 110 -- 137.5-109.5-134.5 (184); 22 m; 2070 & 2103 g; 75.5/5.5/67.5; 183.7; -5.5 / 86.4; 170-191; aspen + carbon stringers + "spectral braid" + fiberglass. Stands out for playfulness without majorly compromising variable stability; deep splayed rocker, accessible ends; high-speed composure close to stiffer/heavier skis thanks to huge sweet spot; carves far better than rocker suggests. Most stable: Sender 110, M-Free 112. More float: new Reckoner KF.
- Prior Northwest 110 ("quad-glass") -- 138.5-110.0-131.2 (190); 23 m; 2188 & 2190 g; 64/4.5/59; 189.2; -3.1 / 91.5; 178, 184, 190 (custom); yellow-cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Built for freestyle on natural features in challenging snow; high speed limit, great suspension; not quickest; strong flex & heavy swing weight less fun slow (lighter carbon version available). Damper ARV 106/Professor 111; centered preference (directional: M-Free). For sessioning cliffs/cornices after storms.
- Rossignol Sender 110 -- 140.3-110.4-132.6 (184); 20 m; 2196 & 2211 g; 69/6/50; 181.5; -3.4 / 87.4; 160-191; poplar + partial titanal + rubber inserts + "Air Tip" + carbon & fiberglass. Supersized park ski with big-mountain charger internals; one of dampest/most stable here; centered, symmetric, balanced; very impressive firm edge hold; loosens in soft. Stout/heavy -- not for low-speed play. Directional: M-Free 112.
- RMU Professor 111 -- 138-110.5-131 (188); 19.8 m; 1948 & 1957 g; 70/3/38.5; 186.3; -4.8 / 88.3; 168, 178, 188; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Neither burly charger nor soft-snow specialist; low weight, middle flex -> agile, practical in shallow; knocked around more than Northwest 110/Sender 110 but much lower swing weight; more support than Bacon 108/Bent 110. Directional version: RMU Apostle 114.
- Dynastar M-Free 112 (190) -- go-to for many; big sweet spot caters to many stances; surfy + phenomenal suspension + high-speed stability; vs Sender 110: more intuitive for directional reviewers, trades firm precision for maneuverability/forgiveness. Heavier swing weight but less burdensome than paper; size up if between lengths.
- DPS Koala 111 -- 140-111.1-127.6 (184); 18 m (all lengths); 2180 & 2195 g; 68/4/60; 181.8; -6.9 / 84; 168, 176, 184, 189; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Unlike DPS's light directional skis: heavier, freestyle-friendly; more composed in soft chop than Professor 111/Bent 110, easier to bend and livelier than Sender 110/Northwest 110; forward or centered; carves reasonably, nice suspension. More directional Reckoner KF or softer, tighter-turning Deathwish 112.
- Faction Studio 3 -- 139.5-111.5-133.5 (184); 21 m; 2078 & 2091 g; 62/9/61.5; 182; mounts Newschool -5.6/85.4, Progressive -7.1/83.9, Classic -8.6/82.4; 165-190; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Stout, balanced, surprisingly agile; for playful AND aggressive skiers; light build, stiff flex, symmetric minimal taper -> high-speed tricks in soft snow; not exciting slow, not especially damp; pushable even with forward style; carves very well for width. Studio 2 more versatile; Studio 3 better float/composure in deeper snow.
- Moment Deathwish 112 -- 137.5-111.5-128.5 (190); 27 m; 2240 & 2242 g; 63/6/67; 187.9; -6.6 / 87.3; 174-190; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. Decade-old but surprising: Triple Camber = deep rocker maneuverability + camber-pocket grip; notably stiff; blasts soft chop and carves firm far better than width suggests; balanced, agile. Less slow-speed engaging/backseat-forgiving; suspension not as smooth as M-Free 112/Sender 110. Rare versatility. More: Wildcat 118.
- Volkl Revolt 112 (new) -- 133.5-111.5-125.5 (185); 19.1 m; 2122 & 2125 g; 73/4.5/42.5; 183; -2.8 / 88.7; 178, 185, 192; aspen/maple + fiberglass. Fills gap between Revolt 104 and 121; looser/damper than 104, quicker/more practical on firm than 121; one of most nimble/surfy at width; light in air with decent suspension; shovels knocked around in variable (prefers upright centered stance). For freestylers on shallower soft days. More: Revolt 121.
- K2 Reckoner KF (26/27 update; Karl Fostvedt) -- 137-113.5-133 (184); 23 m; 2225 & 2232 g; 79.5/5.5/73; 182.8; -4.2 / 87.2; 172-191; aspen + "spectral braid" + flax + carbon & fiberglass. Made stiffer (original among softest ever tested): less absurdly easy at slow speeds but much more composed in chop and supportive on deep landings; still softer side of average; surfy, carves oddly well, stance-versatile. More stable/float alternative to Reckoner 110; softer Deathwish 112.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- All-Mountain Freestyle (top -> bottom):
- Most Stable in Crud -> Least: M-Free 112 (190), Sender 110 (191), M-Free 108 (192), Deathwish 112, Northwest 110, M-Free 108 (185), Wildcat 108 (190), Reckoner KF, ARV 106 Ti, Sender 110 (184), Koala 111, 1000 All Mountain, Altum 104, ARV 106, Studio 3, Wildcat 108 (184), Reckoner 110, Max K 106, Revolt 112, Deathwish 104, Studio 2, Nexus, Professor 111, M-Free 100, Nightstick 104, Bacon 108, Nightstick 97, Unleashed 98, Professor 101, Dirty Bear XL, Reckoner 102, Deathwish 96, Devastator, Sender 100, Revolt 104, Vacation, Allplay, Bent 110, Season Kin.
- Most Park-Friendly -> Least: Reckoner 102, Allplay, Nightstick 97, Kin, Revolt 104, Professor 101, Sender 100, Studio 2, Vacation, Dirty Bear XL, Deathwish 96, Unleashed 98, 1000 All Mountain, Nexus, Nightstick 104, ARV 106, M-Free 100, Reckoner 110, Revolt 112, Max K 106, ARV 106 Ti, Deathwish 104, Professor 111, Devastator, Bacon 108, Bent 110, Wildcat 108 (184), Altum 104, Studio 3, Reckoner KF, Sender 110 (184), M-Free 108 (185), Northwest 110, Wildcat 108 (190), Koala 111, M-Free 112, Sender 110 (191), Deathwish 112, M-Free 108 (192).
- Most Surfy/Loose -> Least: Devastator, Bent 110, Revolt 112, M-Free 112, Reckoner KF, Reckoner 110, Koala 111, Professor 111, Max K 106, Bacon 108, ARV 106, ARV 106 Ti, Wildcat 108 (184 & 190), M-Free 108 (185 & 192), Deathwish 112, Northwest 110, Studio 3, Vacation, Professor 101, 1000 All Mountain, Reckoner 102, Nightstick 104, Nexus, M-Free 100, Altum 104, Dirty Bear XL, Deathwish 104, Deathwish 96, Sender 110 (184 & 191), Allplay, Nightstick 97, Kin, Sender 100, Studio 2, Revolt 104, Unleashed 98.

### Park Skis [L9428-]
Reviewer pictured: Abel Sosa (Crested Butte). Class overview: park skis trending toward playfulness/versatility over max support/precision; traditional ultra-stiff comp skis "endangered species"; more options for slow-speed/creative skiers, plus more skis covering both ends; many perform well all over the mountain.

- J Skis Joyride -- 117-89.5-113 (182); 21 m; 1825 & 1858 g; 59/4/47; 178.9; -4 / 85.5; 140-182; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Easygoing, playful; soft, low swing weight, one of easiest ~90 mm park skis to whip around; forgiving; little inherent stability -- needs balanced stance on big features (vs Omen 90/Jeffrey 92); agile in bumps/trees; good carver (tighter than 21 m suggests). Makes park laps easy; firm/shallow conditions.
- K2 Omen 90 -- 119-89.5-112.5 (179); 18 m; 1956 & 1978 g; 40.5/6/41; 180; -2 / 87.3; 149-179; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Throwback with modern tech: abundant camber, minimal rocker, strong flex, substantial core -> more precise/supportive than most; excellent on big features on firm; less maneuverable/more demanding at slow speeds. Stout, lively, grippy platform for old park-and-pipe fans. Softer-snow: Omen Team.
- ON3P Jeffrey 92 -- 125.5-93-119 (181); 17.9 m; 1792 & 1806 g; 65/7.5/50; 182; -4.6 / 86.4; 171-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Narrowest, most precise Jeffrey; shallower "Ripper" rocker (still more rockered than most at width); maneuverable in soft snow; more firm precision and big-jump stability than wider Jeffreys; low swing weight for its composure. Recent subtle tweaks for edge grip and softer ends. Max grip: Omen 90; softer ends: BDOG 94.
- Armada BDOG 94 -- 120.7-93.7-116.5 (180); 15 m (all lengths); 1970 & 1990 g; 58/5/56; 178.2; -0 / 89.1 (true center); 164, 172, 180; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Exhibits every kind of playfulness: very, very soft tips/tails transition smoothly to stiffer middle, no hinge points; heavily tapered -- less catching on rails/landings/butters; very surfy for 94; tight radius quick carves on non-icy snow. Less stable/supportive on big jumps and firm. Few skis as fun to bend and slide.
- Head Oblivion 94 -- 128.1-93.9-117.5 (184); 22.8 m; 1846 & 1857 g; 64/7/44; 181.7; mounts SC -0/90.5, All-Mountain -5/85.5; 163-184; poplar + elastomer reinforcements + fiberglass. Precision, agility, support; minimal taper/rocker = solid edge hold; easier to release than Omen 90; low swing weight + stout flex -> less damp in crud but reliable on big landings. Butters: Joyride/BDOG; planted: Omen 90. All-mountain: Oblivion 102, Unleashed 98.
- K2 Omen Team (successor to K2 Poacher) -- 125-95.5-118.5 (184); 20 m; 2100 & 2115 g; 50/8.5/47; 185; -2 / 89.7; 163-184; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Comp roots but surprisingly intuitive, even for reviewers avoiding big features; notably damp and stiff; capable on natural features; less precise than Omen 90 but much easier to slash/pivot/bend; holds edge well; rewards aggressive skiing; enough rocker/taper for butters. Softer: Allplay, BDOG 94.
- J Skis Allplay (186; specs in Freestyle) -- tons of accessible playfulness on wider platform; soft, ideal for butters/presses; not for big high-impact landings (be centered) vs Unleashed 98/Omen Team; respectable mountain stability for its flex; handles soft and firm, fine in slush and boot-top pow. Narrower Joyride for hardpack; lighter alternative to Line Chronic 101.
- Nordica Unleashed 98 (186) -- not technically a park ski but one of more stable/precise ~100 mm options here; mounted a few cm forward it's a dependable all-round park ski between Omen Team and Allplay; can feel hooky before detuning; not easiest to bend. Intuitive entry for directional skiers dabbling in park. Softer/looser: Reckoner 102.
- Prior Northwest 100 ("traditional camber" version; customizable) -- 128.1-100.2-123.3 (184); 21 m; 1910 & 1919 g; 54/4.5/45; 182.8; -2.9 / 88.5; 158-190 (custom); yellow-cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). Wider platform with narrow-park-ski traits: impressive edge hold, excellent pop, solid stability on heavy landings; less maneuverable in variable and for small-feature butters; low swing weight. "Hybrid Rocker" version is looser, like Omen Team. All-mountain: Northwest 110.
- Line Chronic 101 -- 129.1-100.4-124.2 (186); 16.6 m (179); 2126 & 2129 g (186); 59/4/50; 186; -4.5 / 89; 165-186; aspen + fiberglass. Breaks stereotype that soft surfy skis are twitchy; accessible flex + substantial construction -> playful and smooth; lively rebound disguises mass; very capable outside park (maneuverable, composed in rough snow, fun on piste); engaging at moderate speeds in park, manageable in variable landings; not most nimble in air (Allplay).
- K2 Reckoner 102 (184) -- longtime reviewer favorite; current version keeps versatility and playfulness with better durability; consistently soft, easy butters, forgiving; capable on moderately large jumps if centered; deep low-slung rocker easy to throw sideways yet carves well on non-icy snow; not lowest swing weight but nice suspension, burly build. Support/precision: Omen Team.
- Faction Studio 2 (183) -- narrow-park-ski stiffness/rebound in wider silhouette; for aggressive high-speed trick skiers; tons of pop/amplitude but effort to bend; narrower skis better firm precision/lower swing weight; Studio 2 better float and less grabby in slush/variable. Softer: Reckoner 102, Chronic 101.
- Head Oblivion 102 -- 135.3-102.2-125.3 (189); 25.1 m; 1806 & 1884 g; 65/5/57; 186.7; mounts SC -0/93, All-Mountain -5/88; 173, 181, 189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer layers + carbon & fiberglass. Lightweight + fairly strong flex + minimal rocker + long radius -> very low swing weight yet precise/stable for weight carving and on big impacts; more locked-in, less pivoty on firm; rewards aggressive dynamic approach (like Studio 2, which is easier to slash). For hopping and carving rather than pivoting.
- ON3P Mango 102 (Jake "Mango" Mageau) -- 130.5-103-127 (181); 18.6 m; 1970 & 2014 g; 76/5/56.5; 181.8; -1.6 / 89.3; 161-186; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. More balanced/freestyle than Jeffreys; more agile (more taper), more precise on edge (tighter radius); stiffer, more composed and surfier than average; explosive pop. Slightly more accessible Studio 2, stiffer Reckoner 102. Not for ice; needs input. Uncommon stability/maneuverability/agility/energy combo.
- Salomon Depart .01 -- 139.5-106-127 (180); 15 m; 1763 & 1783 g; 62/5/38; 178.4; -5.3 / 83.9; 170, 180, 188; karuba/poplar + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Far from conventional park ski: 106 waist, light, lots of taper and deep rocker, stout flex, somewhat directional shape/mount -- yet extremely playful; loves going sideways; very low swing weight; stiff mid + excellent suspension-to-weight give respectable soft-snow stability; cumbersome on super firm. New 98 mm Depart .02 not yet tested.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Park (top -> bottom):
- Most Playful Overall -> Least: BDOG 94, Joyride, Depart .01, Reckoner 102, Allplay, Chronic 101, Mango 102, Jeffrey 92, Studio 2, Oblivion 102, Unleashed 98, Northwest 100, Omen Team, Omen 90, Oblivion 94.
- Most Stable on Jumps -> Least: Omen 90, Omen Team, Northwest 100, Oblivion 94, Unleashed 98, Oblivion 102, Jeffrey 92, Studio 2, Mango 102, Depart .01, Reckoner 102, Chronic 101, Allplay, Joyride, BDOG 94.
- Most Versatile -> Most Specialized: Northwest 100, Omen Team, Allplay, Reckoner 102, Jeffrey 92, Mango 102, Depart .01, Oblivion 102, Unleashed 98, Chronic 101, Studio 2, Joyride, Oblivion 94, Omen 90, BDOG 94.

### Powder Skis -- More Directional [L9863-]
Reviewers pictured: Jonathan Ellsworth (Snowbird), Paul Forward (Chugach Powder Guides AK), Luke Koppa (Snowbird). Class overview: line between playful and directional pow skis keeps blurring; not simply "traditional mount (~-9 cm or farther back)"; these aren't necessarily heavier, more stable, or straighter than More Playful -- look at both sections. Generally for forward-stance skiers not spinning/switch often; covers a few inches of fresh to bottomless to chop.

- Fischer Nothing -- 142.5-111.5-133 (186); 22 m; 2113 & 2130 g; 63/7.5/39.5; 184.1; -8.5 / 83.6; 176, 186, 196; wood + partial titanal + fiberglass. Deliberately dubious name; 112 mm freeride ski, not a pow specialist but fun there; narrowest/among most versatile here; carves well; floats well for width; forward or somewhat centered; practical on shallow days; stable for weight in soft chop; more agile than next two. Floatier alternative to Rustler 11.
- Head Kore 112 Ti -- 139.5-111.5-127.5 (184); 21 m; 2224 & 2285 g; 64/6.5/33.5; 182.8; -9.1 / 82.3; 170-191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Big-mountain charger + surfy pow ski; shines in latter half of resort pow day (leftovers, chop); much damper than old ultralight Kores. Vs Revolt 114: Revolt floats/drifts better; Kore more accessible slow and firm. Easier than heaviest here, more composed than lighter ones; doesn't plane up easily in bottomless but turns fine moving. More float: Kore 118 Ti.
- Volkl Revolt 114 -- 146-113.8-128 (184); radii 25/19/22 m; 2288 & 2291 g; 72/4/35; 182; -8 / 83; 177, 184, 191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Floats/drifts well, but stands out in less-ideal conditions; very deep rocker makes this burly ski easy to release/slash; mount splits centered/set-back; among more stable yet maneuverable when moving; big shovels cumbersome very slow. Slashing soft chop nearly as fun as deep pow -- great resort option.
- Armada AntiMatter 114 (new) -- 137.5-113.5-131 (187); 21 m; 2089 & 2116 g; 60/5/55; 185.4; mounts Palm -2.2/90.5, Standard -5.2/87.5; 181, 187, 193; poplar + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Designed with Freeride World Tour athletes (who also throw park tricks); very nimble, playful, can drive shovels in rough snow but balanced/freestyle-friendly; more stable than ARV 112 but on less damp side of this section; accessible, surfy, agile, versatile.
- Blizzard Rustler 11 -- 142.3-114-132.6 (186); 20.5 m; 2082 & 2089 g; 81/3/49; 183.7; -7.6 / 84.2; 168-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. As much fat all-mountain as pow ski; precision, energy, agility define it; versatile: soft chop OK for weight, carves impressively, nimble in bumps/trees; most others here float better; similar agility with better deep snow: Blaze 114. Not excessive on firm; blast in a few inches.
- Volkl Blaze 114 (184) -- vs Revolt 114 (charge-y), Blaze is super friendly/easygoing; shovels produce lots of flotation; easy to carve/slash from many stances; low swing weight; not damp/stable in chop but carves and pilots trees well (forward stance on firmer snow); knocked around in big fast dense-chop turns but easy to shut down. Intuitive, approachable powder setup.
- Atomic Maverick 115 CTI -- 140-114.5-129 (185); 18 m; 2117 & 2132 g; 61.5/6.5/29.5; 184; -9.5 / 82.5; 177, 185, 193; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Balances pow-ski compromises; surfy, agile but enough weight/support/precision to not be niche; soft shovels + directional mount = impressive float; mount a few cm forward for centered stance; knocked around in dense chop but big sweet spot. Looser, stabler Blaze 114; floatier Rustler 11; more directional, damp Bent Chetler 120. "Happy medium."
- Heritage Lab RC116 -- 140-117-130 (190); 31.5 m; 2497 & 2583 g; 72/4/35; 189.5; -9.7 / 85; 184, 190; poplar/bamboo + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Modern big/stiff/heavy pow ski inspired by first-gen Blizzard Bodacious; extremely stable and powerful, knack for heavy maritime snow; more effort and forward stance; breaks into drifts easily for those skiers; very powerful on edge, though not as plush as original Bodacious or Heritage Lab's heavier, softer R120 ST. For planted ride and supreme composure.
- Head Kore 118 Ti -- 144.5-117.5-129.5 (184); 24.3 m; 2238 & 2257 g; 63/5.5/33.5; 183.1; -7 / 84.5; 177, 184, 191; karuba/beech + 2 titanal + graphene + flax & fiberglass. Biggest Kore: deeper rocker, more taper, less set-back mount than 112; looser, more balanced in air, more backseat-forgiving; impressive suspension in variable. 112 better in shallow/firm carving; 118 pivots/drifts easier in deep. For directional skiers who find Wildcat 118 too rockered.
- Moment Wildcat 118 -- 142-117.5-133 (190); 27 m; 2371 & 2375 g; 63.5/6/68; 187.8; -5.9 / 88.1; 174-196; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Many reviewers' resort pow ski of choice; rare combo of directional stability and freestyle playfulness; very composed in chop/crud yet looser and quicker than similarly heavy rear-mounted skis. Billy Goat 118/Folsom Rapture float better; lighter skis easier slow. Key reference for making chop nearly as fun as pow. More float/stability: Moment Chipotle Banana.
- ON3P Billy Goat 118 -- 146-119-128.5 (186); 29 m; 2334 & 2353 g; 79.5/3.5/32.5; 186; -9.8 / 83.2; 176-191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Longtime fixture; planes up really well yet has mass/support for poor conditions; deep rocker, short effective edge, long radius -> easy drifts (not from backseat); not a firm carver; not easily unsettled by heavy chop; pin-tail & rear mount = distinctly directional (tips float, tails sink). Balanced alternative: Folsom Rapture, ON3P Woodsman.
- Folsom Rapture -- 149.4-120.9-138.4 (192); 28 m; 2288 & 2301 g; 74/0/34.5 ("Shallow Reverse Camber"); 190.5; -7.7 / 87.5; 178-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). One of Paul Forward's (AK heli guide) all-time favorites; rare combo of excellent stability in all conditions, very good float except deepest/lightest snow, smooth roll into drifts; composed even across avalanche debris. Narrower skis more versatile at resort; 4FRNT Renegade more engaging on low angles. One of best big-mountain pow skis tested. Wider: S-Class, Rotor.
- Heritage Lab R120 ST (small batches) -- 144-121-139 (193); 27 m; 2689 & 2717 g (one of heaviest ever weighed); 60/0/35; 193; -8 / 88.5; 186, 193; poplar/bamboo + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Near-unrivaled suspension; progressive mount and fairly forgiving flex -> more balanced, less traditional than RC116; respectable float; exceptionally damp; intuitive carving yet easy drifts; not stance-picky. Vs Rapture: Rapture more composed in big fast turns; R120 smoother, more "plush."
- 4FRNT Renegade (Eric "Hoji" Hjorleifson) -- 136.7-121.2-130.4 (191); 30 m; 2173 & 2204 g; 95/0/35.5 (reverse camber); 188.9; -5.3 / 89.1; 177, 184, 191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Long radius + reverse camber: calm in big carves in clean snow, extremely easy to feather/release in trees/pillows; deflected by chop more than Wildcat 118/Chipotle Banana but planes better and not grabbed by crusts. More centered/surfier alternative to Maverick 115 CTI; lighter, much less directional Billy Goat 118. Versatile: 4FRNT Hoji.
- Moment Chipotle Banana (limited editions) -- 140-122-134 (193); 35.5 m; 2323 & 2352 g; 71/0/71 (fully rockered); 190; -5.8 / 89.2; 178, 186, 193; poplar/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Looks like pow tool but weight/straight shape/stout flex tell otherwise; float not impressive for width, but blown away by chop/crud/mixed -- busts cut-up inbounds snow better than almost any >120 mm ski, yet breaks into quick skids. R120 ST floats better and plusher in firm crud; Chipotle quicker, better in deep chop. Wildcat 118 more practical.
- Photo: Paul Forward, Chugach Powder Guides AK.
- Heritage Lab Ultra Taper 122 (190; specs in BC Touring) -- modern reverse-camber reverse-sidecut; ultralight for dims -- great for skinning; unmatched surfiness; vs Renegade: Renegade better outside deep pow, UT122 looser and much lighter; float comparable to ~120s; pivots/releases with ease. Opposite of versatile; wholly unique deep-snow experience.
- Line Pescado -- 157.5-124-144.5 (180); 19.5 m; 1808 & 1809 g; 66/5/22; 178.9; -9.8 / 79.6; 180 cm only; paulownia/maple + fiberglass. Decidedly directional (shape, mount, cut-out swallowtail) yet one of most "playful" here in its own way: creative on-snow approach -- sharp carves in terrain corridors, quick slashes; huge shovels give lots of float despite 180 only; fun in any soft snow (bottomless trees to slush carves); light weight/single length limit stability in dense chop. Riot in rolling terrain. Versatile: Poisson, Sakana.
- DPS Lotus 124 -- 146-124.6-134 (191); 23 m (all lengths); stated 2200 g; 76/6/35; 189; -11 / 84; 178, 185, 191; poplar + carbon. Big pow ski for mellower terrain; easy to slash/pivot/carve when not full throttle; natural extension of iconic Wailer 112 (low swing weight, loose slarvy) with longer radius for extended drifts/more stability. Heavier Rapture/R120 ST much better as speeds rise; UT122 surfier. Lots of float, accessible maneuverability, lower-speed fun.
- Faction La Machine 5 (current version) -- 147.5-125.5-138.5 (192); 27 m; 1947 & 1981 g; 72.5/7/34; 190.2; mounts Newschool -10.6/84.5, Progressive -12.1/83, Classic -13.6/81.5; 178, 186, 192; paulownia + phenol binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Original (subtle reverse camber) was a favorite pow-touring ski; current keeps light build and 126 mm shape but adds significant camber -- still balanced and floats well, but harder to drift, more prone to hook up, needs deliberate weighting. UT122 much surfier; La Machine 5 more predictable on firm. Best mounted ~-9 to -10 cm.
- Folsom S-Class (new) -- 152-126-142 (190); 28 m; 2275 & 2322 g; 77.5/0/45.5; 187.4; -8.4 / 85.3; 188-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). Slots between Rapture and Rotor (~127 mm); Rapture's versatility/stability with more surface area/float for bigger terrain, deeper snow, larger skiers; "Shallow Reverse Camber" preferred; very intuitive balanced mount; drive into carves, deliberate tail unweighting to drift; softer tail likely easier. Customizable.
- Folsom Rotor -- 157.5-131-147.5 (193); 25 m; 2343 & 2376 g; 73/0/58.5; 190.3; -7.2 / 88; 184-195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass (custom). For dream-deep days; built off Rapture with ~10 mm more width and modified shape; planes up quicker in bottomless, turns easier slow (shorter radius); consensus: excellent heli ski, esp. with slightly softer tail. S-Class keeps more Rapture high-speed composure; Rotor wins when you can't feel the base.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Powder More Directional (top -> bottom):
- Most Flotation -> Least: Rotor, La Machine 5, S-Class, Rapture, UT122, Billy Goat 118, R120 ST, Renegade (191), Lotus 124, Pescado, RC116, Chipotle Banana, Wildcat 118 (190), Blaze 114, Renegade (184), Revolt 114, Maverick 115 CTI, Nothing, AntiMatter 114, Kore 118 Ti, Wildcat 118 (184), Kore 112 Ti, Rustler 11.
- Best in Deep Chop -> Worst: Chipotle Banana, Wildcat 118 (190), RC116, Rapture, R120 ST, S-Class, Revolt 114, Wildcat 118 (184), Kore 118 Ti, Rotor, Billy Goat 118, Kore 112 Ti, Maverick 115 CTI, Renegade (191), Rustler 11, Nothing, AntiMatter 114, Blaze 114, Renegade (184), Lotus 124, La Machine 5, Pescado, UT122.
- Best in Refrozen Crap -> Worst: R120 ST, Kore 112 Ti, RC116, Wildcat 118 (190), Kore 118 Ti, Revolt 114, Wildcat 118 (184), Chipotle Banana, Rapture, S-Class, Billy Goat 118, AntiMatter 114, Rustler 11, Nothing, Renegade (191), Maverick 115 CTI, Blaze 114, Renegade (184), Rotor, Pescado, Lotus 124, La Machine 5, UT122.

### Powder Skis -- More Playful [L10488-]
Reviewer pictured: Luke Koppa (Snowbird). Class overview: increasingly interesting/hard to describe; less about driving shovels, more about surfing, smearing, tricking from upright centered stance; includes some of the most playful AND some of the most stable skis in the guide; all reviewers get along with at least a few, even non-trick skiers.

- Liberty Scope 110 -- 140-107.5-127 (190); 22.5 m; 1848 & 1940 g; 61/7.5/47; 188; -7 / 87; 176, 182, 190; poplar/bamboo + phenolic binding plate + carbon & fiberglass. Wide all-mountain or narrow pow ski; notably light, easy to flick, surfy; big shovels float well for size; fun on moderate pow days and practical when shallow; knocked around in dense chop. Fun to carve; directional or playful. More capable: Icelantic Nomad 112.
- Armada ARV 112 (185) -- agile, freestyle-focused with support for bigger hucks on soft landings; surfy maneuverability > variable stability; more well-rounded than ARV 116 JJ UL; soft ends, solid middle. More directional: Nomad 112, Apostle 114; better float/stability balanced: Revolt 121.
- Icelantic Nomad 112 -- stated 147-112-139 (188); 20 m; 2026 & 2118 g; 67.5/4/58; 185.2; mounts "PC" -5.2/87.4, "BC" -7.2/85.4; 171-194; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Uncommonly adaptable; moderate flex, deep rocker -- accessible, maneuverable, yet impressive soft-chop stability for weight; stance-intuitive; few weaknesses; excellent float. Stability without heavy weight; doesn't need tons of speed.
- Salomon S/Lab QST Blank (186) -- 139-112-128.5 (186); 19 m; 2187 & 2215 g; 69/5.5/37; 184.5; -7.5 / 84.8; 170-192. One of surfiest narrower skis here but not noodly; stiffer tail less backseat-forgiving (vs Bacon 115/J Best Friend) but supportive landings; respectable composure for maneuverability though less than weight suggests. Planted: Wildcat 118, Sender 118; surfier/lighter: QST X.
- 4FRNT Sinister (for athlete Stinius Skjøtskift) -- 133.5-112.5-124.5 (186); 22 m; 2276 & 2358 g; 74.5/3.5/44; 183.9; -4.2 / 87.7; 179, 186, 192; aspen/maple + partial titanal + fiberglass. Combines MSP, Renegade, Devastator: very tapered, centered mount, twin tail, very stiff cambered metal mid; loose in soft with low swing weight for weight; precise on firm once used to centered stance; tips knocked around more than Wildcat 118/Sender 118 but surfier/quicker. Not playful slow; rare surfiness/agility/support for aggressive freestylers.
- Ellis Meraki 112 -- 139-112.5-128.5 (186); 20.7 m; 2022 & 2052 g; 58/4/48.5; 184.9; -7 / 85.4; 174-191; poplar + fiberglass binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Support in playful agile chassis; light, progressive mount, nimble; stiff flex drives through low-density chop; not forgiving/easy slow but easy to slash. Much lighter Wildcat 118 or more directional, less damp Sinister; stronger, more agile Nomad 112; less directional Apostle 114.
- Photo: Paul Forward (Chugach Powder Guides).
- Whitedot Altum 114 -- 142.5-113-131.3 (187); 24 m; 2122 & 2151 g; 63/3/58.5; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 179, 187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Pow ski keeping all-mountain versatility; fairly light, moderately tapered, strong flex, long radius -> very good stability except heavy chop/firm crud; low-slung deep rocker -- slides easily yet engages on edge. Middle-ground skier; more stable/precise Nomad 112. Damp charger: Sender 118.
- Line Optic 114 -- 138.3-113.4-130.9 (186); 24 m; 2318 & 2322 g; 65.5/1.5/47.5; 185.2; -7.8 / 85.2; 178, 186, 192; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Surfy shape, charger guts; for playful skiing at speed in unpredictable snow; one of better here for soft-chop composure; deep rocker easy drifts; balanced in air; not forgiving on firm; versatile across styles. Softer: Bacon 115.
- Meier Leeper -- 146-113.5-138 (185); 19 m; 2216 & 2246 g; 65/4/38; 183.7; -4.5 / 87.5; 171-192; aspen/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Allows big airs/high speeds without requiring them; hefty, good suspension, not as sluggish on firm as Sinister/Optic 114; centered mount, deep rocker -- maneuverable slow; intuitive for directional reviewers. Sender 118/Wildcat 118 more composed; Leeper lower swing weight, more practical on firm; less float in really deep. Days you can feel the bottom.
- Nordica Unleashed 114 -- 147-113.6-135.7 (186); 20.4 m; 2086 & 2088 g; 78/6/57; 183.7; -8 / 83.5; 174-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Contradictions: playful but not soft, directional but not demanding, light but not flimsy; impressive float for 114; excellent carver; average surfiness. Quicker, floatier Altum 114; stiffer, lighter Nomad 112; more directional AntiMatter 114. Lighter pow ski that isn't soft and center-mounted.
- Armada AntiMatter 114 (187) -- FWT-winning mystery ski; stance-versatile, more directional/supportive than ARVs; not heavy/stiff (lighter, more forgiving Optic 114); adaptable for huge cliffs or cruising; balances looseness/precision/quickness/damping. Versatility over specialty.
- RMU Apostle 114 -- 142.5-114-131.5 (184); 22 m; 2086 & 2125 g; 66.5/4.5/35; 182.5; mounts FR -4.7/86.5, TR -6.7/84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. One of RMU's more directional pow skis but playful/agile in market; like Professor with more shovel float and tail support; not particularly damp -- pop and slash through soft chop rather than plow; centered or directional; doesn't need much fresh snow. Symmetric: Professor 121; better suspension: Optic 114, Anima.
- Line Bacon 115 -- 147.6-114.2-143.2 (188); 18.4 m; 2260 & 2290 g; 61/4/54; 188.2; -4.1 / 90; 168, 178, 188; maple/aspen + fiberglass. Throwback early freestyle pow ski shape/flex; very accessible flex, tighter radius, centered; hefty -> far better crud suspension than ARV 112/Scope 110/Bent Chetler 120, though not for fast chop (Optic 114). Soft, balanced, damp. Less freestyle "soft and heavy": Heritage Lab HB122 V2.
- Black Crows Anima (189) -- 146.2-114.8-135.5 (189); 19 m; 2259 & 2279 g; 64/6/55; 187.4; -6.3 / 87.4; 176.6-194.4; poplar + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Pow-category "all-rounder": fairly heavy, composed in chop/crud, easy to carve, not punishing (more accessible than Wildcat 118); balanced in air, lands switch; conservative rocker -> powers through firm; respectably surfy deep. One of most adaptable here; alternative to Unleashed 114/Apostle 114/Nomad 112 if you accept heavier swing weight for better suspension.
- Head Oblivion 116 -- 141.5-116-131.5 (189); 30.8 m; 1998 & 2024 g; 66/7/55; 187.2; mounts SC -0/93.6, All-Mountain -5/88.6; 181, 189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer layers + carbon & fiberglass. Unique recipe: stiff, light, long radius, centered mount, more camber than rocker, little taper; easy to slash in untracked with speed, surprisingly calm in big fast turns given low swing weight; poor suspension -- soft-snow-specific tool; for centered-platform skiers airing feature-filled pow lines. Looser/damper: ON3P Jeffrey 118, Prior CBC.
- Salomon S/Lab QST X (184; specs in 50/50) -- not freestyle-focused but extremely surfy and agile; lighter, looser, more centered than QST Blank; not composed in dense chop (light, stiff underfoot, short edge); not a trick ski (no true twin); predictable in big open turns vs tighter-radius surfy skis.
- Prior CBC (customizable) -- 139.6-116.5-137.5 (184); 16.5 m; 2147 & 2286 g; 64/3/61; 182.3; -2.1 / 89; 178-193 (custom); yellow-cedar/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass (custom). For freestyle skiers with pillow lines/technical terrain; balanced, near-symmetric, stout flex, damp, surfy; easy to flick, eager to air, very supportive landings; more composed than lighter skis; swing weight lower than weight suggests; less big-turn stability than Wildcat 118/Sender 118 but more composed than Oblivion 116/Bacon 115. Could be an all-time favorite for the right skier. Open terrain: ON3P Jeffrey 118.
- Blizzard Canvas 118 (188) -- one of lightest here but doesn't ski like it; low swing weight, low-speed agility, skiable aggressively in lower-density cut-up snow; far more freestyle-friendly than recent Blizzards yet intuitive for directional skiers; minimal taper -> impressive float, stability-to-weight, firm precision. Not very loose/easy to bend here; knocked around in dense chop. Great if also skinning.
- J Skis Best Friend -- 140.5-117-130 (191); 21 m; 2259 & 2326 g; 63/3/49.5; 189.6; -6.5 / 88.3; 177, 184, 191; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Asymmetric shape but not niche: intuitive, balanced, drifts with very little effort in a few inches to bottomless; soft tips plane easily; centered or slightly forward. Larger/aggressive skiers may want more shovel support in heavy pow/dense chop; shines in tighter terrain and moderate pitches; respectable suspension; fun in spring slush; capable freestyle option.
- Heritage Lab AM118 -- 143-117.5-136.5 (186); 24.7 m; 2490 & 2502 g; 72/4.5/57; 185.6; -7.1 / 85.7; 180, 186, 192; poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Reminds reviewers of favorite resort pow skis -- for how it handles chop; minimal taper, very heavy, yet balanced freestyle-friendly; not surfiest/quickest but one of most capable fast through cut-up pow; looser/easier than most equally stable skis. Vs Sender 118: AM118 slightly softer, livelier, more directional-friendly; Sender more top-end stability, lower swing weight. Comes over 190 cm.
- Moment Wildcat 118 (190) -- stiffer, heavier, straighter than many here -> one of best for mobbing soft chop; deep splayed rocker and balanced mount keep it surfy and comfortable in air; directional and playful skiers enjoy it; more energy than Sender 118 (takes effort). Go-to for not slowing down once tracked; plusher: Sender 118, AM118.
- Rossignol Sender 118 (aka Blackops, Blackops 118, Blackops Gamer, Sender Free 118) -- 145.5-117.5-140.5 (186); 25 m; 2457 & 2533 g; 64/7/60; 184.2; -2.7 / 89.3; 176, 186; poplar + "AirTip" insert + fiberglass. Renamed again, performance unchanged; all-time favorite: one of dampest/most stable in soft chop yet balanced, surfy, forgiving; absurdly calm, not punishing; not agile; shovels can submerge if driven (directional skiers mount ~-5/-6 cm). Narrower: M-Free 112; longer: AM118.
- ON3P Jeffrey 118 -- 146.5-119-138.5 (186); 23.5 m; 2377 & 2394 g; 81/3.5/56.5; 186.5; -4.7 / 88.6; 181, 186, 191; bamboo + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Pop, slash, or charge; deep splayed rocker surfy like Wildcat 118/Sender 118 but easier to bend; strong, hefty, smooth planted ride yet a touch more agile/lively than Sender 118. Directional: ON3P Woodsman; floats and handles crud much better than Bacon 115/Prior CBC. Looser: Revolt 121.
- Atomic Bent Chetler 120 (192) -- easy recommendation for any playfulness; very surfy, light, lively, touring-friendly; easy slashes, eager airs, balanced; knocked around in dense chop; more capable than weight suggests in soft snow; supportive middle for landings. Damper loose feel: Revolt 121; directional maneuverability: J Skis Best Friend.
- RMU Professor 121 -- 146.3-120.3-139.6 (188); 21 m; 2127 & 2161 g; 72/3/41; 186.5; -5 / 88.5; 178, 188; poplar + beech underfoot + fiberglass. Big Professor keeps freestyle capability and variable versatility; soft deeply rockered ends surfy/forgiving; strong mid supports off-center landings; good in deep snow across terrain types; directional skiers prefer Apostle 114; middle weight balances agility & suspension (more suspension than Bent Chetler 120, more nimble than heavy skis). Safe bet.
- Volkl Revolt 121 -- 142.5-120.4-134.2 (184); radii 20/19/20 m; 2240 & 2250 g; 87/4.5/53; 182.2; -2.8 / 88.3; 177, 184, 191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Exception: extremely easy to pivot/slarve yet smooth ride and stout middle -- skiable hard through chop/soft crud. Few merge looseness and stability so cohesively. Directional high-speed: Revolt 114, Wildcat 118. Shallower: Revolt 112.
- Whitedot Ragnarok ASYM -- 144.8-120.9-132.9 (190); 30 m; 2062 & 2080 g; 72/3/37; 187.8; -7.4 / 86.5; 190 cm; poplar/ash + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Approachable, maneuverable despite odd shape; ASYM = more tip taper on outside edge than inside for balanced weight distribution and easier skids; dedicated pow tool (122 mm); fun slarvy ride in blower or slush; "skis short," not easy to carve firm; nice suspension for weight; compelling 50/50 pow ski. Vs Best Friend: BF better suspension/firm; Ragnarok more support at lower weight.
- Black Crows Nocta -- 147-121-136.5 (190.6); 19 m (all lengths); 2118 & 2124 g; 64/3.4/45; 187.5; -5.5 / 88; 177.6, 185.5, 190.6; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. Black Crows' pow specialist; not super deep rocker yet remarkably easy to slarve -- one of surfier here; centered upright stance (more drivable than Revolt 121); fairly light, not a charger, but handles chop/crud/crusts better than similar-weight skis; predictable steep or low-angle; reasonable on skin track. More composure: next two skis.
- Heritage Lab HB122 V2 -- 150-122-141.5 (190); 27.9 m; 2443 & 2449 g; 83.5/3/77.5; 193.2; -5.0 / 91.6; 180, 190; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Original HB122 was one of last season's biggest surprises/favorite inbounds pow skis (stability + maneuverability + unusually forgiving flex); V2 refined -- slightly stiffer shovel for composure in dense snow at speed; supportive yet easily released tail forgiving from centered/slightly backseat stance; easy to pivot despite weight. Versatile damp pow ski for directional & freestyle skiers on lift-served deep days.
- K2 Reckoner 124 -- 149-123.5-147 (189); 25 m; 2423 & 2446 g; 85/3/68; 188.3; -5.3 / 88.8; 159-189; aspen + "spectral braid" + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Framed as BC booter ski but broader: very deep splayed rocker loose/surfy; substantial weight -> nice suspension in cut-up pow; more effort at slow speed (keep momentum). Stiffer/narrower Sender 118/Jeffrey 118 better for chop; softer flex more playful than most stable skis; HB122 V2 more intuitive for directional skiers.

BLISTER SPECTRUMS -- Powder More Playful (top -> bottom):
- Most Surfy/Loose -> Least: Ragnarok ASYM, Best Friend, QST X, Revolt 121, Bent Chetler 120, Nocta, Sinister, QST Blank, Prior CBC, Reckoner 124, Professor 121, Jeffrey 118, ARV 112, Optic 114, Meraki 112, Apostle 114, AntiMatter 114, HB122 V2, Wildcat 118 (184), Wildcat 118 (190), Sender 118, AM118, Anima, Bacon 115, Nomad 112, Canvas 118, Leeper, Scope 110, Unleashed 114, Altum 114, Oblivion 116.
- Lightest in the Air -> Heaviest: Scope 110, Canvas 118, QST X, Bent Chetler 120, ARV 112, Meraki 112, Oblivion 116, Apostle 114, Nocta, AntiMatter 114, Unleashed 114, CBC, Ragnarok ASYM, Nomad 112, Altum 114, QST Blank, Revolt 121, Sinister, Wildcat 118 (184), Professor 121, Leeper, Best Friend, Jeffrey 118, Bacon 115, Optic 114, Anima, Sender 118, Wildcat 118 (190), AM118, Reckoner 124, HB122 V2.
- Most Stable in Chop -> Least: Wildcat 118 (190), Sender 118, HB122 V2, AM118, Reckoner 124, Optic 114, Jeffrey 118, Anima, Wildcat 118 (184), Sinister, Revolt 121, Leeper, Altum 114, AntiMatter 114, Nomad 112, Nocta, CBC, Bacon 115, Best Friend, Professor 121, QST Blank, Apostle 114, Meraki 112, Unleashed 114, Oblivion 116, Canvas 118, Bent Chetler 120, ARV 112, Scope 110, QST X, Ragnarok ASYM.

## 3. Non-product article: Ski-Quiver Selections [L11323-11492]
Reviewers pictured: Jonathan Ellsworth (Palisades Tahoe), Luke Koppa (Crested Butte). A "quiver menu" for 1- and 2-ski quivers (and building larger ones); reviewers' personal quivers online; BLISTER+ for one-on-one help.
- ONE-SKI QUIVER (RESORT), narrowest to widest, with ability range: Blizzard Black Pearl 88 (low-intermediate to advanced); Folsom Spar 88 (int-expert); Renoun Endurance 88 (int-expert); Nordica Santa Ana 92 (adv-expert); Black Crows Serpo (high-int to expert); Salomon QST 94 (beginner-expert); Blizzard Black Pearl 94 (adv-expert); Head Kore 94 Ti & Kore 93 Ti W (int-expert); Stockli Stormrider 95 (int-expert); Volkl M7 Mantra & M7 Mantra W (adv-expert); Elan Ripstick 96 & Ripstick 94 W (beg-expert); Line Optic 96 (beg-expert); Wagner Summit 97 (int-expert); Fischer Nightstick 97 (beg-expert); Nordica Unleashed 98 (int-expert); K2 Mindbender 96 & 96 W (high-int to expert); Head Kore 100 Ti & 99 Ti W (int-expert); Romp Zorro 100 (beginner-advanced); Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti & M-Pro 98 W (high-int to expert); Salomon QST 100 (beg-expert); Atomic Bent 100 (low-int to expert); Rossignol Soul 102 (beg-expert); Armada AntiMatter 100 & 100 W (high-int to expert); Line Optic 104 (low-int to expert); Nordica Enforcer 104 & Santa Ana 102 (adv-expert); Whitedot Altum 104 (int-expert); Shaggy's Ahmeek 105 (int-expert); J Skis Hotshot (int-expert); Icelantic Nomad 106 & Maiden 102 (beg-expert); RMU Apostle 105 (low-int to expert); Season Nexus (low-int to expert); ON3P Woodsman 108 (int-expert); K2 Reckoner 110 (int-expert); Folsom Giver (int-expert); Moment Deathwish 112 (int-expert).
- TWO-SKI QUIVER (RESORT) -- Lower-snow areas (narrow ski -> pick one of three wider partners): Line Optic 88 -> Optic 104 / Ahmeek 105 / Hotshot; Dynastar M-Cross 88 -> Mindbender 96 / M-Pro 100 Ti / AntiMatter 100; Black Crows Mirus Cor -> Optic 96 / Apostle 105 / Reckoner 110; Folsom Spar 88 -> Kore 100 Ti / Ahmeek 105 / Folsom Giver; Blizzard Anomaly 88 -> Mantra 102 / Enforcer 104 / Summit 107; Head Kore 93 Ti W -> Pandora 99 / Maven 103 CTI / Folsom Cash 106; Nordica Enforcer 94 -> Mantra 102 / Kore 106 Ti / Summit 107; Blizzard Black Pearl 94 -> Santa Ana 102 / Summit 106 / Sheeva 11; Salomon Stance Pro 96 -> Revolt 101 / Romp Oso 105 / Countach 110; Volkl M7 Mantra -> Enforcer 104 / Dancer 106 Ti / Summit 107; Nordica Unleashed 98 -> ARV 106 / Valhalla 107 / Maiden 108; Line Pandora 99 -> Optic 104 / Nomad 106 / Meier Leeper; Faction Studio 2 -> Sender 110 / Prior Northwest 110 / Deathwish 112.
- Higher-snow areas: Anomaly 88 -> Enforcer 104 / Kore 112 Ti / Revolt 114; Heritage Lab AM90 -> AM110 / Moment Wildcat / HB122 V2; Santa Ana 92 -> Summit 106 / Sheeva 11 / QST Blank; Stormrider 95 -> Blaze 114 / Maverick 115 CTI / Anima; Line Optic 96 -> Nomad 112 / M-Free 112 / Optic 114; Mindbender 96 W -> Maiden 108 / Sheeva 11 / Anima; Mindbender 96 -> Optic 114 / Anima / Folsom Rapture; Kore 100 Ti -> Countach 110 / Anima / Kore 118 Ti; Mantra 102 -> Kore 112 Ti / Revolt 114 / Heritage Lab R120; Sheeva 10 -> Sheeva 11 / QST Blank / M-Free 112; Altum 104 -> M-Free 112 / Apostle 114 / Sender 118; Apostle 105 -> Apostle 114 / Reckoner KF / Canvas 118; Woodsman 108 -> Anima / AM118 / Wildcat 118.
- TWO-SKI (1 RESORT + 1 TOURING): Spar 88 -> Majesty Superwolf / 4FRNT Raven / QST Echo 106; Serpo -> QST Echo 106 / Weston Summit Carbon / Heritage Lab UT110; Optic 96 -> Weston Summit Carbon / Canvas 108 / Wildcat Tour 118; M-Pro 98 W -> Wagner Summit 106 / QST Echo 106 / Atomic Backland 108 W; Mindbender 96 -> Wagner Summit 105 / QST Echo 106 / Faction Agent 3; Nomad 100 -> Weston Summit Carbon / Wildcat Tour 108 / Canvas 108; Mantra 102 -> Ronin 108 / La Machine 3 / UT110; Altum 104 -> Wildcat Tour 108 / UT110 / Wildcat Tour 118; Enforcer 104 -> Raven / QST Echo 106 / UT110.
- ONE SKI FOR EVERYTHING (RESORT + BACKCOUNTRY): Black Crows Serpo; Salomon QST 94; Elan Ripstick 94 W & 96; Line Pandora 99; Romp Zorro 100; Atomic Bent 100; Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti & M-Pro 98 W; Blizzard Sheeva 10; Liberty Scope 104; Whitedot Altum 104; Black Crows Atris; DPS Kaizen 105; RMU Apostle 105; Folsom Cash 106; Nordica Unleashed 106 CA; Wagner Summit 106; Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro; Blizzard Canvas 108; Folsom Giver; J Skis Slacker; 4FRNT Hoji; Moment Deathwish 112.

### Ski Boots [L11493-]
Reviewers pictured: Luke Koppa (Palisades Tahoe), Kristin Sinnott (Crested Butte). Section overview: best way to find boots = experienced bootfitter; foot shape & biomechanics most important. Also be clear about how/where you ski. Three classes: (1) Alpine (fixed cuff); (2) Alpine/Touring "AT" (alpine boots with walk mode -- some good for touring, some just for walking to lift); (3) Touring (lighter, better ROM, all work with pin/tech bindings, less ideal for constant lift laps). "Progressive" flex = gets stiffer as you flex further; "linear" = resistance stays similar. Many tested boots are stiffest (e.g., 130) in their series; notes largely apply to softer (e.g., 120) versions. Specs: stated flex, stated last width, sole type, measured weight per boot (size 26.5 unless noted).

ALPINE BOOTS:
- Head Raptor WCR 140S PV -- flex 140 (spine bolt -> stated 150); 96 mm; Alpine sole; 2391 g. Race-inspired, phenomenal suspension/support in relatively accessible fit for class; lower volume than most ~98 LV boots but not as low as Atomic Remedy 130 or (esp.) Dobermann 5 96. A main reference for boot suspension. For those finding LV boots too roomy/light/unsupportive.
- Nordica Dobermann 5 96 - Stiff -- 140; 96 mm; Alpine; 2304 g. More suspension and even lower volume than Promachine 3, less bootfitting than true race boots; lowest-volume of race-inspired 96 boots esp. midfoot/toe box; among stiffest (flex can be softened). Comparable to Remedy 130/Raptor 140; most precise last shape. Too snug/heavy: Promachine 3 130.
- Atomic Remedy 130 -- 130; 96 mm; GripWalk; 2272 g. Built for freeriders using race boots; nearly identical to Redster TX 130 but with vibration-damping bootboard & spoiler, GripWalk soles; as stiff or stiffer than most 130s; consistent support across temperatures; suspension slightly less smooth than Raptor 140/K2 Cortex 140.
- Fischer RC4 Carbon Pro LV ZF BOA -- 130+; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2171 g. "Pro" features: race-inspired plastics, carbon-reinforced cuff, big power strap, ZipFit cork liner (moldable, add/remove cork for years), lower BOA; "strong 130"/"slightly soft 140." Not as smooth or low-volume as above; more support/suspension than lighter 130s; liner keeps precise fit many seasons.
- Fischer RC4 Carbon Pro MV ZF BOA -- 130+; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2170 g. Roomier version; strongest MV boot in section; roomy midfoot/toe box, take up ankle volume with cork; very stiff for 130 (spine bolt -> ~140); smoother than lighter boots; BOA praised for fine-tuning and uniform pressure. For feet that can't do LV.
- K2 Cortex Team 140 Zonal BOA -- 140; 96-98 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Toe; 2423 g (27.5). K2's "race-inspired" take; dual-BOA works well; precise fit, smooth ride, powerful flex; power transmission & suspension comparable to Raptor 140 -- big step up from lighter 130s; many tuning options; can be tricky on/off in cold with high instep; roomier than previous boots. Softer: new Cortex Team 120.
- Lange Shadow 140 Pro LV -- 140; 97 mm; GripWalk; stated 1986 g. Several reviewers use Shadows daily; extra cuff pivot and unique spine -> excellent power transmission without harshness at moderate weight; not as stiff as some; livelier/less damp than heavier models; far roomier (instep, midfoot) than 96 mm boots.
- Salomon S/Pro Race 140 -- 140; 96 mm; Alpine; 1933 g. More precise fit and support than other S/Pros; not as low-volume as Remedy/Dobermann; try if you want low volume with a fairly high instep; "strong 130"/"soft 140"; light for flex; not as damp but not an outlier for weight. More room with power transfer: new S/Pro Alpha C BOA 130.
- Fischer RC4 Free Pro MV ZF BOA -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Toe; 2219 g (27.5). RC4 Pro MV plus tech toe inserts and slightly lighter parts; built for downhill, for hybrid bindings using tech toe only uphill; same roomy fit and ZipFit liner; very strong 130, above-average suspension. Narrow use case; rare MV performance.
- Kastle K130P LV -- 130; 97 mm; Alpine; 1996 g. Blurs generalist LV and race boots; powerful; actually one of lowest-volume here despite 97 mm; also "Standard Fit" 100 mm and lower-volume race "R" boots. Flex/rebound like RC4 Pro LV; heavier Remedy/Raptor/Dobermann better suspension; race precision in lighter, livelier package. High instep: S/Pro Race 140, Shadow 140 Pro.
- Nordica Promachine 3 130 (updated) -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1915 g. Strong lively LV; similarly snug heel/midfoot as prior but more room over instep and toe box; perhaps slightly softer initial flex, more progressive; less damp than Dobermann 5 96; nice lateral support. Better power transfer: Shadow 130, S/Pro Alpha C 130.
- Tecnica Mach1 LV 130 -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1842 g. Balanced lighter/livelier LV; middle-of-spectrum 130 flex like Promachine 3 130/Atomic Hawx Ultra 130; light without harshness; a tad snugger than average 98 but more accommodating than previous Mach1 LV; MV/HV variants exist.
- Lange Shadow 130 LV -- 130; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1977 g. Many reviewers' daily driver for seasons; noticeably easier ski bending vs conventional 130 (tested one-foot comparisons); different flex takes getting used to but addictive; nice suspension for weight; LV is considerably higher volume than most LVs (MV also). Rare combo of power transfer, suspension, rebound, weight.
- Salomon S/Pro Alpha C BOA 130 (new last season standout) -- 130; 98 mm; Alpine; 1905 g. Unique shell layout like Shadow concept for efficient power transfer; side-by-side with Shadow 130 LV: Alpha C arguably transfers power a tad better, Shadow slightly smoother suspension...
  ...for most folks the choice should come down to fit; Alpha C a bit lower-volume than Shadow LV but roomier in midfoot/instep than most 98 mm boots; transfers power better than most light boots without harshness.
- Atomic Hawx Ultra 130 S BOA -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1861 g. Adaptable low-volume; Atomic started the light alpine boot trend with original Hawx Ultra; latest is best yet; less supportive/damp than Remedy 130 but smoother, more progressive flex than many light boots; distinctive rebound; one of lowest-volume 98 mm boots out of box but heat-moldable shell/liner easily open up room. Offered with upper BOA or buckles. More room: Hawx Prime.
- phaenom fs 02 130 (2nd gen) -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1833 g. "Hybrid cabrio" shell; more linear than 2-piece but progression later in flex; stiffer than prior fs 01 120; sizing now ~one size smaller (no longer long); middle-of-road MV fit, slightly lower instep; excellent elastic power strap; burly liner with rubberized sole doubles as apres boot. Forgiving initial flex, supportive deeper.
- Armada AR ONE 130 MV -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Toe; 2094 g. Between 2-piece overlap and 3-piece cabrio: softer first half than e.g. Tecnica Mach1 130, ramps more than 3-piece (e.g., K2 FL3X Revolve KF). Vs phaenom fs 02: AR ONE a tad softer, more linear, smoother; average MV last but open-throated -> roomy instep.
- Head Kaliber 130 MV BOA2 -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2137 g. Dual BOA; Blister: BOA shouldn't be a deciding factor but better for fine-tuning lower shell, no notable upper-BOA downsides; nice suspension, softer than average 130 (closer to 120 even with extra spine bolt); takes edge off harsh snow.
- Rossignol Vizion 4B Elite 130 LV -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2053 g. Designed for easy on/off: lever unlocks cuff from lower shell -- step in/out hands-free; roomy for 98 esp. instep (MV/HV variants); substantially softer than most 130s, linear initially then ramps. Strong flex: Rossignol Hi-Speed. Forgiving linear flex + easier entry.
- K2 Cortex Team 120 Zonal BOA (new) -- 120; 96-98 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Toe; 2351 g. All the Cortex Team 140 features with a more accessible flex; lower-volume than some 98s (roomy instep); thick race plastics -> much smoother/damped than lighter boots; "strong 120," as supportive as previous three 130s; tech toe inserts for hybrid bindings. Both Cortex Teams available down to 22.5 (welcome trend).
- Photo: pro skier Eric Hjorleifson, Blister Summit 2026 @ Snowbird.
- Salomon S/Pro Supra D BOA 120 -- 120; 100-106 mm; GripWalk; 1850 g. Wide range of medium-to-higher-volume feet; dual-BOA, lower-BOA, upper-BOA variants; similar suspension to S/Pro Alpha but more forgiving and less efficient power transfer; slightly softer than average. Try multiple BOA layouts. More power: S/Pro Alpha C (accommodating for LV).
- Rossignol Pure Elite 125 LV (women's) -- 125; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1707 g (24.5). Very supportive women's boot; notably progressive -- easy initially, ramps a lot; damp/smooth; low-volume but roomier instep/toe box than Hawx Ultra W/Mach1 LV W; vs Lange Shadow 115 LV W (ramps earlier), Pure Elite stiffer deeper; removable spine bolts to soften. Lots of top-end support, not a brick wall.
- Tecnica Mach1 LV 115 W -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1663 g (24.5). Tecnica's stiffest, narrowest women's (non-race); fit like Hawx Ultra 115 (quite low volume), a tad more instep room; a tad stiffer, maybe less damp than Hawx Ultra 115/S/Pro Alpha C 115 W -- fit should decide. Wider: Mach1 MV 115 W; more support/taller cuff: Mach1 LV 130 (down to 22.5).
- Salomon S/Pro Alpha C BOA 115 W -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1754 g (24.5). Very impressive power transfer for low-volume fit; easier to bend skis than equally stiff traditional boots (better edge grip on firm); not harder to flex; respectable suspension; roomier than Mach1 LV/Hawx Ultra, lower volume than S/Pro Supra. Shares much with Lange Shadow 115 W LV.
- Lange Shadow 115 W LV -- 115; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1769 g (24.5). Shadow's distinctive exceptional power transmission without harshness, in smaller sizes/accessible flex; not as low-volume as Mach1 LV/Hawx Ultra (instep, midfoot); like Pure Elite/S/Pro Alpha C fit; MV also. Efficient power transfer, smooth suspension, snappy rebound -- reviewer favorite.
- Atomic Hawx Ultra 115 BOA W -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1650 g (24.5). For notably low-volume feet or those who want to make it work: Mimic liner, heat-moldable shell; one of lowest-volume women's boots; heat molding creates room esp. midfoot; flex like Mach1 115, a tad damper, progressive and supportive; livelier, less smooth than Pure Elite 125. Roomier: Hawx Prime.
- Dalbello Cabrio LV 115 W -- 115; 99 mm; GripWalk; 1727 g (24.5). 3-piece: more linear (stiffer at start, not as stiff at end vs strong 2-piece 115); moderately low-volume but much more accommodating instep than Mach1 LV/Hawx Ultra; 3DWrap liner cushions, eliminates shin slop; pivoting tongue easy on/off. More support than nearly any women's 3-piece.
- Salomon S/Pro Race 110 (unisex; down to 22.5) -- 110; 96 mm; Alpine; 1787 g (24.5). As supportive as women's 115s here with smoother suspension; notably low-volume (snugger than most 98s); heat-moldable shell/liner despite race inspiration; stiff like Hawx Ultra 115 but damper, less lively. More room: S/Pro Alpha C BOA 115 W.
- Fischer RC4 105 MV BOA -- 105; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1686 g (24.5). Outperforms its flex rating; roomy MV (midfoot/toe box); "Vacuum" plastic allows big fit tweaks incl. reducing volume; lower BOA snugs enough that 98-last skiers got a secure fit; flex comparable to Mach1 LV 115 W/Hawx Ultra 115. Softer/other fit: S/Pro Supra BOA 105 W, Head Kaliber 105 W.
- Salomon S/Pro Supra BOA 105 W -- 105; 100-106 mm; GripWalk; 1772 g (25.5). Supportive, adaptably roomy; softer than RC4 105 MV but stronger than Kaliber 105; S/Pros feel a touch softer than rated; decent suspension for weight; heat-moldable shell/liner; adjustable spine (direct/progressive). Stiffer: S/Pro Supra Dual BOA 115 W; more powerful: S/Pro Alpha C BOA 115 W.
- Head Kaliber 105 W MV BOA2 -- 105; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1979 g (24.5). Accessible, comfortable; softer than rated (~95 feel) but engaging with little input; heavier build gives smooth ride; roomy MV, can work for near-high-volume feet; upper BOA takes getting used to, uniform cuff fit (single-BOA versions exist). Lighter/stronger: S/Pro Supra 105.

ALPINE / TOURING (AT) BOOTS:
- Lange Code 140 BOA LV -- 140; 97 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 2002 g (27.5). New benchmark for downhill performance of walk-mode boots; stiffer than other AT boots here and Lange's prior XT3 140; linear start then substantial support, avoiding abrupt feel; nearly matches fixed-cuff Shadow responsiveness. For Blister's Alaska reviewer (heli & skintrack with heavy pack), first crossover that can truly replace an alpine boot. Not light/efficient uphill (Scarpa 4-Quattro Pro for stiff+light).
- Scarpa 4-Quattro Pro -- 130+; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1531 g. Uphill performance rivals stiffest Touring boots yet nearly strongest AT boot here; but nearly all others have smoother suspension in variable/firm at speed; much lower volume than most 100-last boots. Less support/more mobility: new Scarpa Maestrale RS.
- Tecnica Cochise 130 -- 130; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1832 g. Easy recommendation: well-rounded AT, very capable in resort, viable on skintrack; stated 50 deg ROM; skis like a light fixed-cuff 130 (PU shell, burly liner). Zero G Tour Pro much lighter/walks better. One of best 1-boot quivers for aggressive skiers. Lower volume: Hawx Ultra XTD, Shift Alpha; higher: Cochise HV, Lange Code MV.
- Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 130 BOA -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1791 g. Downhill nearly identical to fixed-cuff Hawx Ultra 130; commendable walk mobility; heavier than touring boots but much better suspension; lower volume than most AT, heat-moldable; not for extended tours (Backland XTD). Extremely impressive. MV: Hawx Prime XTD.
- Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 130 -- 130; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1799 g. "Do anything": low-volume last like S/Pro Alpha but higher instep, heat-moldable, lower BOA -> can work for MV feet; walk mechanism and instep strap like S/Lab MTN Summit -- impressive heel hold, respectable ROM. Vs Hawx Ultra XTD/Cochise: a bit softer off the top, quicker rebound. More support: new 140-flex S/Lab Shift Alpha BOA.
- Atomic Hawx Prime XTD 130 BOA -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1873 g. MV between Hawx Ultra (LV) and Hawx Magna (HV); mirrors fixed-cuff Hawx Prime 130 -- legit 130, not watered down; roomier, more with heat molding; not quite as plush as heavier alpine boots but close; better suspension in resort crud than Dynafit Tigard 130/Roxa R3 130 with comparable ROM.
- Dynafit Tigard 130 -- 130; 101 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1702 g (27.5). Dynafit's most downhill-oriented boot; lighter end of AT; substantial flex/power, a tad softer and less progressive than Cochise 130; Hoji-Lock loosens entire upper cuff (buckles, power strap, walk mechanism) in one motion -- effectiveness depends on leg/foot shape; excellent fore-aft ROM for a 4-buckle AT boot; comfortable skinning; not much loss of support vs light fixed-cuff boots.
- phaenom fr 02 130 -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1885 g. Nearly identical to fs 02 130 plus walk mechanism/tech fittings; hybrid cabrio/overlap -- soft linear start, stiffer later; medium-to-slightly-low-volume fit, good for those finding 3-piece boots roomy over instep; smooth suspension stands out among AT; less fore-aft ROM than most AT but viable for downhill-focused skiers.
- Roxa R3 130 TI -- 130; 99-101 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1580 g. Blurs AT/Touring; 3-piece, lots of support at light weight; linear but strong -- much stiffer at first than fr 02, maybe softer deep; hinged tongue gives some ROM, removed it enables easier strides than all other AT boots; consistently stiff flex (two settings) polarizing. Strong 3-piece touring or light crossover. Smoother resort: phaenom fr 02 130.
- K2 Mindbender 130 BOA -- 130; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1961 g. Nice suspension, respectable uphill; low volume out of box but heat-moldable/lower BOA for MV feet; softer, more linear than Cochise 130/Hawx Ultra XTD, comparable ROM; heavier side; thick alpine liner & PU shell less harsh. More accessible/forgiving; stiffer: Mindbender Team BOA.
- Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 115 BOA W -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1616 g (24.5). Reviewer favorite; alpine-like downhill (like Hawx Ultra 115 W) with functional uphill; respectable ROM; less harsh than lighter boots like 4-Quattro; one of lower-volume AT fits, very customizable. Higher volume: Tecnica Cochise 115 W, Hawx Prime XTD 115 BOA W. Go-to do-everything AT for slimmer fit.
- Scarpa 4-Quattro XT W -- 115; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1323 g (24.5). Very strong but light; skins like freeride touring boots (e.g., Zero G Tour Pro W) but flex as strong or stronger than women's AT boots here; less smooth suspension; progressive/supportive like Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W; fits more like 98 mm. Scarpa reportedly phasing it out; 100-flex 4-Quattro SL W continues.
- Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 115 W -- 115; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1665 g (24.5). Resort-downhill focus with skinning option; not as supportive/damp as Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W but close to 115 alpine boots; walks as well or better than Hawx XTD; smoother than 4-Quattro XT W; adaptable LV-MV fit. Similar to Mindbender 115 W but lighter, ramps quicker. Stiffest Shift Alphas down to 22.5.
- K2 Mindbender 115 BOA W -- 115; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1858 g (24.5). Adaptable fit (heat-moldable shell/liner + lower BOA; snug heel/ankle, MV midfoot); average AT ROM; not light; touch softer and more linear than other 115 AT boots but more plush and muted. Lighter/stronger: Hawx Ultra XTD 115 W, Shift Alpha BOA 115 W.

TOURING BOOTS:
- Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro -- 130; 99 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1354 g (27.5). Still baffling how well it skis for weight; first boot to try for "freeride touring"; less progressive/smooth than heavy AT boots but seriously impressive downhill for weight -- big skiers drive big skis; walks far better than nearly every AT boot; weight rivals softer touring boots. Not as efficient as Zero G Peak. Fairly low-volume.
- Scarpa Maestrale RS (latest) -- 130; 101 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1549 g (27). Best version yet for strong downhill on big BC lines; a bit stiffer than prior, just shy of Zero G Tour Pro; walks better than 4-Quattro Pro, maybe roomier, but fits lower volume than 101 mm suggests; ROM as good or better than Zero G Tour Pro; less harsh in firm variable. More mobility: new Scarpa F1 RS.
- La Sportiva Sender -- 130; 102.5 mm; GripWalk w/ Tech Inserts; 1504 g. Broadest ROM of freeride 130s here; fits like ~100 mm; softer and more linear than most 130s (much softer than previous two); forgiving in tricky snow from centered stance; notably stiffer laterally and rearward than most light touring boots.
- Dynafit Radical Pro -- 120; 103.5 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1483 g. Reliable up and down; more supportive/progressive than <1300 g boots; ROM a bit better than average for 120-130 touring boots; "Hoji Lock" integrates walk mechanism with upper buckle/power strap; forward flex not as stout as Zero G Tour Pro/Maestrale RS but much more accommodating for higher-volume feet; more alpine-like than open-throat designs (e.g., Backland XTD 120). Efficiency: Ridge Pro, Blacklight.
- Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro W -- 125; 99 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1165 g (24.5). Still the stiffest women's touring boot tested; much lighter than anything close in downhill performance; stiffer fore-aft than some AT boots while walking better; less smooth in rough snow; low-volume (ankle, toe box), though some high-instep reviewers fit. Alternative fit: Scarpa Gea RS (women's Maestrale RS).
- Atomic Backland XTD Carbon 120 -- 120; 100 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1372 g. Splits Hawx XTD and Backland; fair 120 forward but less rigid laterally, less precise than previous 120-130s; nearly endless ROM so it feels nicer uphill; middle ground for skintrack mobility without drastically changing downhill technique.
- Dynafit Ridge Pro -- 120; 101 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1376 g (27.5). For skintrack-efficiency priority with better downhill than ~1000 g boots; excellent fore-aft ROM; quick transitions with Hoji Lock (some extra adjustments needed); not very progressive but stiffer forward/lateral/rearward than nearly all equally efficient boots (Dalbello Quantum Free Pro, La Sportiva Kilo XTR, Head Crux Pro); fairly low volume above instep (try Blacklight).
- Scarpa F1 RS (new) -- 115; 101 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1433 g (28). Moves F1 platform toward efficient-but-freeride touring; heavier than F1 LT/Zero G Peak Carbon but much more downhill capability; lower closure is a current favorite (uniform pressure, micro-adjust both directions); linear initial flex then stronger support; a bit softer than Ridge Pro initially; can drive 110+ mm skis at speed in variable. Fit should decide among Backland XTD/Ridge Pro/F1 RS.
- Salomon S/Lab MTN Summit -- 120; 99 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1166 g. Outlier among 1000-1300 g boots: 75 deg ROM, sub-1200 g, walks incredibly, skis quite well; fairly progressive; rating realistic (unlike Quantum Free Pro/Crux Pro); best in smoother snow. Medium-volume, generous toe box. Alternatives: Ridge Pro (heavier), Zero G Peak Carbon/F1 LT (softer).
- Dynafit Blacklight -- flex not listed; 101 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1255 g (27.5). Lighter, slightly less downhill-biased Ridge; partial external tongue removed -> less support but more instep room; fits shorter than competitors in same size; exceptional ROM; Hoji Lock worked more consistently than on Ridge; consider wider power strap for downhill. Drives big touring skis in soft snow.
- Tecnica Zero G Peak Carbon -- 130 (stated); 99 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1077 g (27.5). One of favorites near ~1 kg; far more efficient uphill than Zero G Tour Pro; stiff enough for fat skis in soft snow (learning curve from heavier boots); "not close to a 130"; not drastically better than Scarpa F1 LT but a bit lighter. Stiffer with wider toe: S/Lab MTN Summit.
- Head Crux Pro -- 130 (stated); 99 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1273 g. 130 & 3 buckles suggest freeride, but stands out for uphill efficiency; softer/more linear than MTN Summit/Ridge Pro; actual power closer to high-performance 2-buckle boots (Quantum Free Pro, Kilo XTR); excellent ROM; excellent comfy liner; lower volume, fairly roomy toe box. Competitive if you ignore the rating.
- Atomic Backland Carbon -- 110; 98 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1112 g (28)...
  ...walks very, very well (quick closure/walk mode, open-throat shell); enough power to pilot various widths from centered stance; surprisingly progressive though not stiff; gives up forward/lateral support vs burlier shells; heat-moldable shell notable in class.
- Scarpa F1 LT (on its way out) -- 100; 100 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1104 g (28). Still one of best touring boots for efficiency-first skiers; stiff enough for a 200-lb skier on 120 mm skis in soft/consistent big terrain; harder fast on firm; learning curve; downhill as good as boots 100-200 g heavier. Better downhill: F1 RS.
- Dalbello Quantum Free Pro -- 130 (stated); 100 mm; Touring w/ Tech Inserts; 1367 g (27.5). Stiffness feels like its weight class, not its 130 rating; notably softer/more linear forward than Zero G Tour Pro, Ridge Pro, even lighter MTN Summit; better lateral power transfer than several ~1 kg boots; plenty of ROM; very low instep, moderately wide heel/toe. Stiffer: MTN Summit, Ridge Pro, Backland XTD 120, F1 RS.
- La Sportiva Kilo XTR -- 120; 100.5 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1352 g (28.5). Kilo + more downhill capability while keeping Kilo's excellent ROM; improvement mainly lateral stiffness when carving; fore-aft like Quantum Free Pro/Crux Pro; less supportive/progressive than Zero G Tour Pro.
- La Sportiva Kilo -- 110; 100.5 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1194 g (28.5). For covering miles; very nice to walk, quick transitions; cam-style power strap; softer end of ~1 kg boots (softer than F1 LT, Zero G Peak Carbon); best with <100 mm skis in soft snow; roomier heel/ankle than F1 LT/Backland Carbon but fits short. More control: Kilo XTR, Blacklight, MTN Summit.
- Dalbello Quantum Evo -- flex not listed; 101 mm; Tech-Binding Only; 1126 g (27.5). Dalbello's lightest, arguably the most ascent-oriented boot in guide; extremely low instep, moderately wide heel/toe; excellent ROM; quick-release power strap/upper buckle; among least powerful -- almost too soft to drive >100 mm skis even in soft snow. Unless fit is ideal, choose alternatives.
- Photo: reviewer Sascha Anastas (Snowbird).

### Touring Bindings & Climbing Skins [L12688-]
Reviewers pictured: Paul Forward (Chugach AK), Luke Koppa (Crested Butte). Section overview: bindings and skins significantly affect BC time (more time going up than down); good decision-making, risk assessment, trusted partners matter too. Bindings tested back-to-back on pairs of same ski. First four "hybrid" bindings truly match alpine-binding downhill performance; others far lighter and efficient; "very good time to be a backcountry skier."

TOURING BINDINGS (MSRP; measured weight per binding):
